Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

Mentioning the Feeding of Food:

(36)
Allah Almighty said that the righteous drink from a cup whose mixture is camphor. He said: "And they feed the people, for His love, the poor, the orphan, and the captive — 'We feed you only for the face of Allah; we desire from you neither reward nor thanks.'"

It was narrated concerning the virtue of the food of the imams from the family of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him.

(37)
Reward and no thanks. He and his God have many virtues, including what was narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, who said: "When the tables of the family of Muhammad are set, the angels sanctify them and ask forgiveness for them and for those who eat of their food." It is also mentioned, among the many virtues of feeding believers, that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said: "The Fire is a torment for the son of Jad'an," and it was said to him, "Why is that, O Messenger of Allah?" It was narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: "To gather a group of my brothers over a sa' or two sa's of food is dearer to me than to go out to this market of yours and free a soul." The Messenger of Allah, may peace be upon him and his family, was told by a man: "Feed food and spread peace, and people will come." He said, and Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: "There is no believer who feeds a believer but that Allah feeds him from the fruits of Paradise and gives him to drink of the sealed nectar." He said: "May the prayers of Allah be upon him — whoever feeds a brother in Allah the Almighty is like one who feeds all people. And sustenance comes faster to the one who feeds food than the knife comes to the hump." He said: "The guest is one of the noble morals, and hospitality is united for three days — what is beyond that is his sincerity." He said, may the prayers of Allah be upon him: "If your believing brother enters upon you or alights with you, then come to him with the best of what your house affords, as much as you are able. And if you invite him, do not burden him beyond your means."

Mentioning the Types of Foods and Ordering That They Be Preserved:

It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, that he said: "Meat is the master of food in this world and the Hereafter. You must eat meat, for it causes the flesh to grow. Whoever does not eat meat will be created poorly." And he,

(36) Al-Insan: 5
(37) Al-Insan: 8–9

<!-- page 68 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on Archaeology

May the prayers of Allah be upon him and his family — he loved meat and said: "We, the community of Quraysh, are people of meats." He said, may the prayers of Allah be upon him and his family: "Al-tharid is the food of the Arabs, and the first to prepare tharid was Ibrahim, may the prayers of Allah be upon him. The first of the Arabs to make it was Hashim ibn 'Abd al-Manaf." The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, loved dates and said: "The 'ajwa date is from Paradise." If food was presented to him and he had dates, he would begin with them, and he would break his fast on them. He said, peace be upon him and his family: "How excellent is vinegar — what a blessed condiment it is. And the oil is blessed, the best of condiments." He is the best of the prophets. The Messenger of Allah, may peace be upon him and his family, spoke of lentils: "They soften the heart." And of eating quince: "It purifies the heart and emboldens the coward." He used to like eating with bears and said: "It sharpens the mind and increases the brain." He, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, used to forbid spicy food and say: "It is not blessed." He was brought, peace be upon him and his family, very hot food and said: "What was not blessed by Allah — they have fed us fire. Let spicy food cool, for it effaces blessing, and the devil has a share in it." If possible, it possesses qualities by which one who accompanies it grows satisfied yet safe; it contains no death. He forbade, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, smelling bread as one smells herbs, and cutting it with a knife. He used to admire honey and raisins. He, peace be upon him and his family, would bless the musk-plant and say: "The dandelion is ours, and the watercress is the Lubna of Umayyah." He said, peace be upon him and his family: "Whoever begins his food with salt and seals it with salt — seventy-two diseases and afflictions will be kept from him." He used to say: "There is no leaf of henbane in which there is not a drop of paradise, and celery is due to the virtues of the prophets." 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, admired al-falwadhaj and sugar and would give charity with it. When asked about that, he said: "There is nothing dearer to me than it, and I love to believe in the most beloved of things." He, peace be upon him, admired the pomegranate and ate of its pith and ordered it, saying: "It is a tanner for the stomach." He said: "There is none who eats it but that it contains a grain of heaven in it — cut away the abnormal part and eat the rest." He said, peace be upon him: "Whoever finds a crumb of bread lying and takes it and wipes it and then places it in a niche — Allah has written for him a good deed, and a good deed is multiplied tenfold. And if he eats it, Allah has written for him two good deeds." Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "Food is a provision of sustenance from

<!-- page 69 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

Allah — it is the bounty of Allah. Whoever esteems it has esteemed the bounty of Allah, and whoever underestimates it has underestimated his merits." He used to urge his family and order them to protect food and forbade them to waste any of it. If he saw something in his house being thrown away, he considered it a loss of sustenance. His family was like him in this. He used to say, citing the words of Allah the Almighty: "Allah has set forth the example of a town that was safe and reassured, its provision coming to it in plenty from every place, yet it was ungrateful for the blessings of Allah, so Allah made it taste the garment of

(38)
hunger and fear for what they used to do." He said: they are the people of a village Allah the Almighty

(p. 112)
had expanded in sustenance, and they used stone to make use of bread and would seek help from it. Then Allah sent upon them bears smaller than locusts, who left no tree and no plant except that they ate it. They were afflicted with years of hardship until they returned to what they used to disapprove of eating — that bread. 'Ali ibn al-Husayn, may the prayers of Allah be upon him, entered and found a date on the ground. He said to his slave: "Wash it and hold it until I come out." The boy ate it, and when he came out he asked him about it. The boy said: "I have eaten it," and he said: "You are free for the sake of Allah — you ate it and brought me heaven; I intended to benefit a man among the people of Paradise." Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, looked at a fruit that had been thrown from his house and had not been eaten, and he grew angry. He said: "What is this waste? If you have been given bounty, many people are not satisfied. So eat what you are able of it and feed to those who are in need of it what remains." Al-Mahdi, may Allah's prayers be upon him, saw a piece of bread that had dried and was thrown in a corner of his palace, and he grew very angry. He said: "What is this disrespect for the sustenance of Allah, blessed and exalted?" Then he saw it again and ordered that a portion of it be given to those who needed it. Ja'far ibn Muhammad said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "There is no extravagance in food. Eat of what Allah the Almighty has made lawful for you and praise Him, for Allah the Almighty does not question you concerning what He has made lawful. Rather, the blessing He will ask you about is the bounty of Allah the Almighty upon you — whether you recognized it and did what was right by it." He said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "Do not be stingy with food, for one's food is sufficient for

(38) Al-Nahl: 112

<!-- page 70 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

The two meanings — may the prayers of Allah be upon him — are that whoever satisfies one strengthens two. And 'Ali said, may the prayers of Allah be upon him: "The most blessed of food is that over which the most hands have gathered."

Mentioning the Etiquette of Eating:

It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, that when food was brought to him he would sit on one leg and rest the other, and he, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, would say: "I sit as a slave sits and eat as a slave eats."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, never reclined from the time Allah the Almighty sent him until he was received. He, peace be upon him and his family, forbade eating while lying on one's back, lying on one's side, or reclining." He forbade, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, eating with three fingers, and he used to eat with five. He forbade, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, cutting bread with a knife, and he said: "If you are brought bread and meat, begin with the bread." He used to lick the plate and say: "They have spoiled hunger with it, and then they eat the meat with dry hands." The last of that is the greatest of blessing. Those who lick the plate — the angels pray for them and pray for them with abundance in sustenance. And for the one who licks the plate, it is doubly good. He said, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: "Permeate the trace of food, for it is health for the teeth and the gums, and it brings sustenance to the slave." And nothing is more severe upon the angel of the believer than to see food remaining while he is standing and praying. He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, encouraged the use of reeds, basil, and pomegranates, and said that they dispel the sweat of leprosy. The Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, ordered washing the hands before and after eating and forbade, may Allah be upon him and his family, lifting the basin from the hands of the people until it is full. He said, may Allah be upon him and his family: "No believing slave gathers his children and then places his food in the name of Allah and they call upon Allah, blessed and exalted, at the beginning of their food and praise Him at its end, but the table is raised and they are forgiven." He said, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: "Praise Him at the end, with hands washed before and after. Many hands upon the food and it being lawful — its blessing is fulfilled." He said, may the prayers of Allah be upon him: "Guaranteed for him whose food Allah, blessed and exalted, has blessed is that it does not harm him." Ibn al-Kuwi said to him: "Yesterday I ate food and named it, and then

<!-- page 71 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

it harmed me." He said to him, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "Perhaps you ate our dishes but named only some of them and not others." He said: "Yes, by Allah, it was so, O Commander of the Faithful." May the prayers of Allah be upon him — the blessing of food lies in ablution before and after it. The devil is fond of the smell of what has been immersed, so wash your hands of it. He disliked washing one's hands with some food remaining, saying that grace is averse to that. Ja'far ibn Muhammad said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "If food is placed before people and they name it, the devil says to his companions: 'Go out, for you have no share in it.' But the one who is not named — the devil shares in his food. And whoever says, at the beginning: 'In the name of Allah' — he is rewarded for what he forgot of the naming."

It is forbidden for a man to eat or drink with his left hand or to eat what is not before him, and he should lick his fingers before wiping his hand. He said: "My father, peace be upon him, disliked that a man wipe his hand with a handkerchief while something of food remained on it, in honor of the food." He forbade eating while in a place of need. He might call some of his servants and be told that they were eating. He said: "Let them be until they are finished," not wishing to preoccupy them from their food. If he came and the prayer was being held, he began by eating, finished, and then prayed within its time. He said, may the prayers of Allah be upon him: "If a man comes to his brothers for his food, he should eat with them to encourage them, and he should be the last to raise his hand from the food and the last to perform ablution before and after the meal." He said: "Among the marks of a man's affection for his brother is that he eats of his food, so eat of the food of your brothers — do not be modest and do not fall short."

Mentioning What Is Forbidden from Food:

Allah has forbidden in His Book the eating of carrion, blood, pork, and what has been sacrificed for other than Allah, as well as that which has been strangled — meaning that which is strangled and dies — and that which is beaten — meaning that which is struck with sticks and dies — and that which has fallen from a height and died, and that which has been gored — meaning that which is butted and dies — and what has been eaten by a wild beast, that is, its prey, if it dies from that before being reached and slaughtered. Also forbidden is what has been slaughtered on an altar — that is, what was sacrificed for other than Allah, blessed and exalted, as was done in the pre-Islamic era for the idols they erected. It is also forbidden by the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him.


<!-- page 72 -->
It is forbidden to eat every fanged beast among the seven predators, and every bird of prey with talons that hunts. Also forbidden are donkeys and mules, as well as the slaughter of healthy horses and the eating of their flesh, since the horse is credited with being kept for the sake of Allah — Blessed and Exalted — and held in readiness for jihad against the polytheists. As the Almighty said: "And prepare for them whatever you are able of power and of steeds of war" (al-Anfal: 60), and He said: "And horses, mules, and donkeys — for you to ride and as adornment" (al-Nahl: 8).

However, if a horse is rendered unusable by a fracture, surgery, or a disease from which recovery is despaired of and its usefulness is lost, it may be slaughtered and its flesh eaten. It has been narrated that the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) passed by a man with a horse in such a condition, and the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "Slaughter it — you will have a reward for the slaughter and a reward for counting it in the way of Allah." The man said: "O Messenger of Allah, I have a need of it." He said: "Yes — and eat from it and give us some if you wish." So the man slaughtered the horse and offered its thigh to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him), who ate from it and fed them as well.

It has also been narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that they forbade all food that is injurious and harmful, except that which is harmful yet permissible to use as a cure. It was narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: Sea animals that are considered pure are the whale and the locust. If the locust is taken alive its slaughter is valid, but if it died before being hunted it may not be eaten.

It has also been narrated that the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) forbade the meat of animals that feed on filth — that is, all animals that would not be eaten until they were confined and fed pure fodder and kept away from filth. He said (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him): Confine the filth-eating camel to fodder for forty days, then it may be eaten; cows for twenty days; sheep for seven days; ducks for five days; and chickens for three days — after which their meat may be eaten, their milk drunk, and their eggs consumed. All of this is forbidden before the prescribed period of confinement has elapsed.

From Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) it is narrated that he disliked vinegar made from wine that has spoiled, since its origin is wine. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) also said that he disliked eating glands, the testicles, the spleen,

<!-- page 73 -->
the genitals, the penis, the liver, the interior of the kidney. It has been narrated from him that he said: Nothing from sea creatures may be eaten except what is caught alive and has a shell; whatever dies in the sea before being caught, or has no shell, is forbidden to eat.

It is also forbidden to eat cheese made by Magians and polytheists, into which they introduce the rennet of dead animals, or over which the name of Allah has not been mentioned. However, if the origin of the cheese is unknown and one does not know whether it contains such things, it may be eaten broadly — particularly if it is sold in the Muslim marketplace, just as the sacrifices slaughtered in it are eaten. Everything that is forbidden to eat becomes permissible for one in dire necessity when he can find nothing else and fears for his life; he may eat from it as much as suffices him, no more.

As for all that is food for mankind — grains, pulses, the produce of the earth, and all its fruits — whatever does not harm or kill is permissible to eat. The meat of camels, cows, sheep, and beasts whose milk is lawful is also permissible to eat, unless the animal is among the fanged seven that prey upon other animals and eat them, in which case neither its meat nor anything from it is permissible.

As for birds, all are permissible to eat if they are sound, except birds of prey with talons that hunt other birds and eat them — these may not be eaten, nor their eggs consumed. If eggs are found and one does not know what laid them, then if the two ends of the egg differ from one another it belongs to an animal whose meat is not eaten, and such an egg is not to be consumed; if the two ends are alike it belongs to one whose meat is eaten, and it may be consumed.

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) is reported to have eaten rabbit, while disliking the dabb lizard, the hedgehog, and other insects of the earth. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said that it is permitted to use vessels that have been contaminated with impurity or wine if they are washed and cleaned. As for food in which flies or creatures without blood die, he said: That does not make the food forbidden or impure. If such a creature dies in a liquid, if it is rigid it spoils only what surrounds it and the rest may be eaten; if it is soft and has blood and spoils the liquid, then even what is around it must be discarded, but the remainder — if one sets aside the area around where the creature died — may be eaten if one wishes.

**Mention of the Etiquette of Drinking**

It has been narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he forbade drinking in one continuous draught and commanded that one drink in sips, that the drinker name Allah at the first sip and praise Him when finished,

<!-- page 74 -->
and that one not breathe into the vessel. One should say "Bismillah" when beginning and "Alhamdulillah" when pausing. It has been narrated: "I observed the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) drinking water more than once, and he would breathe three times during the drink, taking the vessel away from his mouth at each breath, saying 'Bismillah' when beginning and 'Alhamdulillah' when pausing." Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: Three breaths in drink are better than one.

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) forbade drinking from the mouth of a waterskin — that is, bending its opening and drinking directly from it — saying that this causes dizziness and the risk that a creature in the water, unseen by the drinker, might flow down his throat.

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) used to drink both standing and sitting, but he forbade drinking directly from a river, basin, pond, or the like. When he saw a man doing so, he said to him: "Kneel as an animal kneels. And if you find no vessel, drink with your hand, for it is the best of vessels."

He also forbade drinking water in a single gulp, saying: "Suck water as it is sucked — do not gulp it down as a burden upon yourself." It is narrated that 'Ali ibn al-Husayn (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) was seen gulping milk, and he was told not to drink that way, and he said: "The people of the Fire drink in that manner." When he drank milk he would say: "O Allah, bless us in it and increase us from it." When he drank water he would say: "Praise be to Allah who has given us fresh and pure water by His mercy, and has not given us bitter salt water on account of our sins."

From Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) it is narrated that he forbade drinking or performing ablution from in front of the spout of a pot.

**Mention of What Is Permissible to Drink**

It has been narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said: "Water is the master of drinks in this world and the Hereafter." Any naturally occurring water in which no human labor is involved, provided it is not mixed with impurity and is neither harmful nor injurious, is permissible to drink. One may also drink the milk of every animal whose flesh is lawful to eat. It has been narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said regarding camel milk: "It combines the benefit of every tree." He also said (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him): "Cow's ghee is a cure and its milk is medicine." The Prophet complained that one of the prophets experienced weakness in his body, so Allah — Blessed and Exalted — revealed to him:

<!-- page 75 -->
"The finest meat combined with milk — I have placed strength in both of them." And it is said: "Meat and milk are the broth of the prophets." He said (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him): "Honey is a healing." He also said: "No sick person is healed like the one who is healed by honey." He said: "If one of you falls ill, let him ask his wife and she may give him one or two dirhams of her dowry, and let him buy honey with it and drink it dissolved in rainwater." Allah — Blessed and Exalted — says regarding the dowry: "If you are satisfied with something of it" (al-Nisa': 4); and He says regarding honey: "There is healing in it for people" (al-Nahl: 69); and He says regarding rainwater: "And We send down from the sky pure water" (al-Furqan: 48).

Water mixed with honey, milk, or anything similar is permissible to drink. Drinks made from dates, raisins, or any other sweetener are lawful to drink as long as they have not changed, and similarly for everything extracted from grape juice, dates, raisins, or permissible fruits — the juice thereof is lawful to drink as long as it has not fermented; in its pure form it is lawful, as it is lawful mixed with water. The same ruling applies to selling, buying, and benefiting from it. As for cooking grape juice until it thickens without changing, it too is lawful to drink pure and mixed with water unless it changes. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) was asked about drinking juice and he said: "It is permissible to drink from the pure, non-intoxicating vessel as long as it has not become intoxicating. But if a large amount intoxicates, a small amount is also forbidden. Do not drink it when it has sat for a long time — after an hour or after a night — for its pleasing quality passes but its sin remains. Fear Allah and hold yourselves accountable."

It was a mark of the Shi'a of 'Ali that they were known for piety, diligence, restraint, avoidance of major and minor sins, and love of good. It has been narrated from 'Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said: "We used to soak raisins or dates in water in a purified vessel for the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him). He would drink it that day and the next; if it changed by the third day he would not drink it." This is the lawful drink that is called wine only because it is cast — that is, thrown — into water from dates, raisins, or similar sweeteners.

<!-- page 76 -->
If the water is a small amount, however — and it reaches the point of boiling, intensifying, and producing froth — then it is wine, and drinking a large amount of it is forbidden.

**Mention of What Is Forbidden to Drink**

Allah the Almighty said: "O you who believe, indeed intoxicants, gambling, stone altars, and divining arrows are but defilement from the work of Satan, so avoid it — that you may be successful" (al-Ma'idah: 90). By this He forbade alcohol, just as He forbade all forbidden things.

It has been narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said: "Alcohol is forbidden; whoever drinks it in this world shall not drink it in the Hereafter — in the barn of Jerusalem." He also said: "Allah has cursed wine: its presser, its squeezer, its seller, its buyer, its drinker, its carrier, and the one to whom it is carried, and the one who consumes its price." He said: "That which intoxicates in large quantities is forbidden even in small quantities." He forbade treating illness with alcohol and intoxicants and forbade giving them to children and animals, saying that the sin falls upon whoever gives it to them.

It has been narrated from 'Ali ibn al-Husayn (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said: "Intoxicating drink comes from five things: dates, raisins, wheat, barley, and honey — that is, in addition to grapes." And from Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his fathers (peace be upon them) it has been narrated: "A person addicted to alcohol will meet Allah the Almighty as one who worships idols, and whoever drinks of it will have no prayer accepted from him for forty nights."

It has been narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said: "Paradise is forbidden to three: the alcoholic, the worshipper of idols, and the enemy of the family of Muhammad." From 'Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) it is narrated: "Whoever drinks wine and dies within forty days of drinking it will meet Allah as an idol worshipper." He also said (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him): "The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) forbade every intoxicant from every drink; and whatever the Messenger of Allah has forbidden, Allah the Almighty has forbidden. Every intoxicant is forbidden, and that which intoxicates in large quantities is forbidden even in small quantities."

A man from the people of Kufa said to him: "May Allah set you right — the jurists of our land say that only wine made from grapes is forbidden." He said to him: "O Sheikh, I do not know what the jurists of your land say. My father narrated to me from his father from his grandfather from 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: 'Whatever intoxicates in large quantities is forbidden in small quantities.'" Ja'far ibn Muhammad then said (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him):

<!-- page 77 -->
"I adhere to the religion of my fathers in everything except the prohibition of intoxicants and the wiping over leather socks during ablution, and the pronouncing aloud of 'Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim' — meaning that it should be recited aloud — and the speaking aloud of the opening formula in prayer."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "One should not treat illness with alcohol, intoxicants, or use them in women's hair, for my father narrated to me from his father from his grandfather that 'Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: 'Allah has not placed a cure in what He has made forbidden.'" He (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) was also asked about a certain drink called pemphigus — the questioner described its nature — and he said: "It is forbidden; do not drink it." He (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said regarding strong vessels that wine was not forbidden because of its circumstances but was forbidden as something that intoxicates whether in small or large quantity; however, he disliked certain pots because they cause drinks to change.

<!-- page 78 -->

<!-- page 79 -->


<!-- page 80 -->

<!-- page 81 -->

A book that is brief on the effects

Mention of what is obligatory in the use of medicine:

In this chapter we have mentioned what was narrated from the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them, concerning medicine, what is permissible to seek treatment with, and what is forbidden in that regard — all of which falls within the gates of the lawful and the unlawful. Perhaps whoever doubts what has come from them — may Allah's prayers be upon them — when he hears it will deny it, and if he uses it without true belief he will not find benefit in it. But Allah, may He be blessed and exalted, causes benefit through it for the people of belief who submit to their command and receive what has come from them with acceptance, not as a test taken without belief, without intention, and without conviction. What supports what we have mentioned is the report narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him: Muhammad ibn Khalid, the governor of Medina, entered upon him returning from an illness that had afflicted him, complaining of a pain he found in his abdomen, and a group was with him. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: Shall I not tell you a hadith that my father narrated to me? His father narrated from his grandfather, from 'Ali, from the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, who said: Yes. He said: Abu Muhammad ibn 'Ali ibn al-Husayn told me from his father, from his grandfather, may Allah's prayers be upon them — that a man complained to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, of a pain he found in his abdomen. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said to him: Take a drink of honey and pour into it three, five, or seven handfuls of water, disavow it in the name of Allah, and it will benefit you by the will of Allah — so do that and take it. A man from the people of Medina who was present in the gathering objected and said: O Abu 'Abd-Allah, the hadith is true and we have heard it, but we have tried it and did not benefit from it. So Abu 'Abd-Allah, peace be upon him, became very angry and said: Allah causes benefit through it for those who believe in Him and believe in His Messenger, peace be upon him. The Muslims are those who accept it, but the hypocrites who doubt it — those who do not believe in him will not benefit from it. And perhaps you are a hypocrite: you took it without believing in the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, and so it did not benefit you. The man fell silent, was overcome with shame, and bowed his head. And Abu 'Abd-Allah, peace be upon him, spoke rightly before all who attended the gathering. We do not count what we have used of this without sincerity of intention — we have certainly benefited from it. And whoever intends any of what is mentioned in the chapters of this book, from the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, and from the Imams from the family of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him

<!-- page 82 -->
Volume Two
A book that is brief on the effects

and his family and peace be upon him — to seek treatment and be treated with it, let him approach it with intention, sincerity, and certainty, for it will benefit him by the will of Allah. But if he harbors doubt in his heart, or is humiliated and cannot be healed by its like, and he listens to what the physicians say and they have said otherwise, then he will not benefit from it. By Allah, He grants success in belief to the saints who submit to their command, receive and accept from His servants what He is pleased with, and chooses them for their guardianship.

Mention of healing through acts of righteousness:

We narrated from the Imams from the family of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, that he said: The black seed is a cure for every disease except death — that is, for every poisonous ailment. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said this to some of his companions, informing them of this hadith. They were pleased by it and said: O son of the Messenger of Allah, may peace be upon him — is that so? He said: Yes. And I will tell you something easier — something from which the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, excluded nothing except death. They said: What is it, O son of the Messenger of Allah? He said: Supplication — the one that the Judge answers. He then joined his fingers together, all of them, bringing the little fingers together, and the little finger tells of the conclusion of the decree. And it is narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, that a man from the Ansar came to him after an absence and he said to him: What has kept you from us? The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said to him: Shall I not teach you words that you call upon Allah with, and Allah, exalted and glorified be He, will remove from you what has befallen you? He said: Yes, by my father and my mother, O Messenger of Allah. He said: Say: There is no power except through Allah. I place my trust in the Living who does not die. Praise be to Allah who has not taken a son and has no partner in the kingdom, and who has no guardian against humiliation. And from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, he said: I complained to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, of forgetting — it had overcome me greatly. He said to me: 'Ali, I will teach you words that will dispel that from you and cause the Qur'an to be firmly established in your heart. Say: O Allah, have mercy upon me by my abandonment of sins for as long as You keep me, and have mercy upon me by my abandonment of what does not concern me. Grant me a good opinion of what pleases You regarding me, and preserve my heart in Your Book as You taught it to me, and cause me to recite it in a manner that satisfies You. O Allah, illuminate

<!-- page 83 -->
Volume Two
A book that is brief on the effects

by Your Book my sight, release my tongue, open my chest with it, employ my body in it, and grant me strength through it. He said: His prayers were upon him, and I prayed with those words, and Allah established the Qur'an firmly in my chest.

It was narrated from him that he said: If one of you places a vessel in his hands containing food, or fears that something in it may harm him, let him name Allah over it and take from it, for nothing harms alongside the name of Allah.

It was narrated from Abu 'Abd-Allah, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that a man from his companions complained to him of a corruption he found in his stomach — that he could not eat any food without it harming him and distressing him. He said to him: Say the name of Allah over every food you eat, and over every variety of it as you eat it, and it will not harm you.

He said: A man from his companions was struck with a whiteness between his eyes and complained of it. He said to him: You must make du'a' while you are in prostration. The man said: O son of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, teach me the words. He said: Say: O Allah, O Most Merciful, O Hearer of prayers, O Giver of goodness — grant me the good of the Hereafter and the good of this world, turn away from me their evil, and remove from me this affliction that is in my eyes, for it has made me grieve and saddened me. He did so and was cured.

It was narrated from him that he said: Hasten to give charity and give it early, for there is no believer who sincerely desires it for the sake of Allah alone except that Allah wards him away from the evil of what descends from the sky on that day. He said: Except that Allah protects him from the evil of what descends from the sky on that day.

He said: Do not think little of the prayers of the poor for the sick among you, for they are answered in your favor, though they may not be answered for the suppliant himself.

Isma'il ibn 'Ali entered Medina while he was ill and Abu 'Abd-Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, visited him. He saw his condition and said to him: Prepare a gathering, place some charity in it, and hold it in your hands. When a questioner comes asking to enter upon you, give him of that charity from your hand and ask him to pray to Allah for you. He said: Shall I not simply give them dirhams? Abu 'Abd-Allah, peace be upon him, said: Do what I have commanded you, and do as I have instructed you.

Abu 'Abd-Allah, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: Three things strengthen the memory and dispel forgetfulness: reciting the Qur'an, using the siwak, and fasting.

And it is narrated from him that a man from his companions complained to him of being greatly overwhelmed with worry and sadness. He said: When any of that afflicts you, place your hand on the site of your prostration, then pass your hand over your face from the left side of your cheek and across your forehead to

<!-- page 84 -->
Volume Two.
A book that is brief on the effects

the right side of your cheek, then say: In the name of Allah, there is no god but He, the Knower of the unseen and the witnessed, the Most Merciful, the Most Compassionate. O Allah, remove from me worry and sadness. Do this three times.

And it is narrated from him, peace be upon him, that he said: Whoever says thirty times every day: In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful; praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds; and may Allah bless the best of His creation — and there is no power except through Allah, the Almighty — Allah will ward off from him ninety-nine types of affliction, the least of which is madness.

Mention of bearing illness with patience and its reward:

We narrated from the Imams from the family of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, said: The groaning of the patient is recorded if he is patient and counted as good deeds; but if it is a panic, it is called panic and there is no reward for it. We have mentioned this chapter in full in the chapters on funerals and the reward of those who are patient and count their afflictions as blessings. It is narrated from them that a woman came to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, with an afflicted son of hers and said: O Messenger of Allah, pray to Allah for this son of mine that He may heal him. He said: It is your choice — if you wish, I will pray to Allah for him and He will heal him; and if you wish, I will leave him and there will be no reckoning or punishment upon him. She said: Leave him, O Messenger of Allah, may Allah's prayers and peace be upon him.

Mention of the talisman and seeking refuge:

We narrated from the Imams from the family of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, would seat al-Hasan and al-Husayn, peace be upon them, on his thighs and seek refuge for them, saying: I seek refuge for you in the perfect words of Allah from every devil and illusion, and from every envious eye. And he said: This is how Abu Ibrahim, peace be upon him, used to seek refuge for his two sons Ishmael and Isaac, may Allah's blessings and peace be upon them.

It is narrated that Labid ibn al-A'sam, the Jew, and his daughter — a Jewish woman known as Umm 'Abd-Allah — worked magic against him in a necklace of red and yellow threads in which they had tied eleven knots, then placed it in a hollow and put it in one of the wells of Banu Zurayq in Medina. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, could neither hear, nor see, nor stand, nor speak, nor eat, nor drink. Then Jibril, peace be upon him, descended with the two incantations. He said to him: What I know has befallen the one whom I see. He said: Labid ibn al-A'sam and the Jewish woman Umm 'Abd-Allah are your sorcerers.


<!-- page 85 -->
So he told him where the magic was located. Then he recited: *In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. Say, I seek refuge in the Lord of the Falaq* — and then *Say, I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind* — until the end of the two chapters. Each time the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) recited, he sat up and another knot was dissolved. He recited eleven times, and eleven knots were dissolved. The Prophet (peace be upon him) then ordered Ali to retrieve the magic from the well in which it had been placed, and summoned Labid ibn Asim and Umm Abdullah, saying, "What compels you to do this?" Labid had been a wealthy man of great means. A slave had passed before him wearing an earring worth a dinar, so Labid seized the boy, pierced his ear, and took the earring. For this, his hand was cut and cauterised, yet the blood would not stop and he bled until he died.

It was also narrated: "I became gravely ill, and the Prophet (peace be upon him and his family) came to me as I lay upon my bed. He said to me, 'O Ali, the most afflicted of people in this world are the best of people — the Prophets, then those who resemble them, then those who resemble them. Receive glad tidings, for this is your share of the trial of Allah the Almighty.' Then he said to me, 'Would you like Allah to reveal to you what you should do?' He said: 'Say: O Allah, have mercy on my thin skin and my delicate bones, and I seek refuge in You from the heat of the Fire and from Umm Maldam. If you believe in Allah, do not consume meat, do not drink blood, and do not incline toward one who claims that there is another god besides Allah. I bear witness that there is no god but Allah alone, with no partner, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and messenger.'" He said: "I recited it and was relieved of my pain." Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: "I was never afflicted by anything except I found relief through it, and they used to teach it even to women and children."

It is narrated from Ali (may Allah be pleased with him) that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) used incantations drawn from the Book of Allah the Almighty and from what is known of His remembrance and His names. He stated that this was customary practice, and that Sulayman, the son of Dawud, also took this course.

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said: "There is no ruqyah except in three cases: for a festering wound, an afflicted eye, or blood. The ruqyah for a scorpion's sting is unlike its thorn, and the sting of a wasp is a matter for the public domain. Yet that view is incorrect; the poison comes from it and from the serpent and others. Fever is linked to poison. As for the scorpion — it strikes with a stinger, and it is a stinger." A company sent by the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) passed through an Arab settlement, and their leader was stung.

<!-- page 86 -->
They asked whether anyone among them could perform a ruqyah by reciting the opening of the Book. He was informed of this by the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him), who approved it. He used to raise some of his wives with the two incantations and asked, "How do you know that it is ruqyah?"

He said (peace be upon him): "There is no contagion, no ill omen from birds, no *ham*, but the evil eye is real and the omen is real." He said (peace be upon him): "If one of you looks upon a human being or a beast or something that pleases him, let him say: 'I believe in Allah; may Allah bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad' — for then his eye will not harm him." It was also narrated from him that if a person was afflicted by an eye or had a headache, he would stretch out his hands, recite the opening of the Book over them, and then wipe his face with them, so that whatever he was experiencing would depart.

The Prophet (peace be upon him and his family) forbade magic, divination, amulets, *tiwalah*, and any charms attached to anything other than the names of Allah the Blessed and Exalted. He also forbade what some women do to draw the love of their husbands. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: "There is no objection to a charm that contains Qur'an."

A man came to Abu Abdillah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him and his family) while in pain. He told him: "Say *Bismillah* and wipe over it, then say: 'I seek refuge in the glory of Allah, I seek refuge in the power of Allah, I seek refuge in the majesty of Allah, I seek refuge in the greatness of Allah, I seek refuge in all the limits of Allah, I seek refuge in the names of Allah, I seek refuge in the names of the Prophet (may Allah bless him and his family) — from the evil of what I am experiencing' — seven times." The man said: "I said it, and what I had been suffering departed from me."

It was also narrated from him (peace be upon him and his family) that he said: "If you wish to seek refuge, clasp your palms together and recite over them the opening of the Book and *Say, He is Allah, One* three times, then place them on the area of pain, then join them again and recite the opening of the Book over them and *Say, I seek refuge in the Lord of the Falaq* three times, then place them on the pain again, then join them and recite the opening of the Book and *Say, I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind* three times, then place them on the pain — three times in all."

It was narrated from him (peace be upon him) that he said: "Whoever has been harmed, let permission be given in his ear." Abu Abdillah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him and his family) said: "There is no objection if one does what he knows." Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon him) said: "If you wish to heal a wound from pain, or from blood, or from something you fear, place your hand upon the wound and say: 'In the name of Allah, I treat you; in the name of Allah,

<!-- page 87 -->
the greatest, made of iron and stone and the thick tusk and sweat — so be not jealous, and let the eye not keep vigil over its frequency' — three times."

**Mention of Treatment and Medicine**

We narrate from the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah's prayers be upon them) that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) was asked whether the sick should seek treatment and take medicine. He said: "Yes. Allah the Blessed and Exalted has not sent down a disease without sending down a medicine for it — so seek treatment. Except for poison, which means death, for there is no cure for it."

Some of the Ansar asked him about one of their men who was complaining of pain in his stomach, and about a Jewish physician who proposed treating him by opening the abdomen. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) did not give them permission. They repeated the request two or three times, and each time he turned them away. Finally he said: "Do as you see fit." So the Jew was summoned, and he opened the abdomen, removed a great deal from it, washed the abdomen, and stitched it up. The patient recovered quickly. When the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) was informed of this, he said: "He who created the disease creates the medicine for it. The best of medicines are cupping, phlebotomy, and black seed — which is *shuneez*." The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) forbade placing a sick person on a strict diet.

He looked at Salman while he was eating dates and said: "O Salman, you are eating dates, and you are unwell." Salman replied: "O Messenger of Allah, I have been chewing with my left tooth while protecting my right eye." The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) smiled and did not prevent him.

It is narrated from him (peace be upon him and his family) that he said: "Leave the evening meal aside — it is a shelter." He also said: "Do not force your sick to eat, for Allah the Almighty feeds them and gives them drink." It is narrated from him (peace be upon him and his family) that there is no objection to an enema so long as it does not greatly distend the abdomen. He said (peace be upon him and his family): "If blood seizes one of you — even with a knife — he should not persist." He said: "If blood seizes him, let him say—" and Allah bless him. "Cupping of the head is a cure for every disease, and medicine is found in four things: cupping, the enema, *al-lat* (scarification), and vomiting." He said to me (may Allah's prayers be upon him), "The Messenger of Allah, whenever he saw you in a panic, used to say: 'If one of you wishes to let blood, let him note on which days it was done, recite Ayat al-Kursi, seek forgiveness from Allah the Almighty, and send blessings upon the Prophet (may Allah bless him and his family). Do not repeat the days lest they turn against you. Draw blood in whichever manner you wish.'" This is also called a restriction.

<!-- page 88 -->
The head is the one who is assisted in this. Fever is from the heat of Hell, so it is extinguished with water. When one was sick, he called for water and placed his hand in it.

Al-Hasan and al-Husayn (may Allah's prayers be upon them) and Fatima came to the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) seeking help. She said: "O Messenger of Allah, pray to Allah to heal your son, for he is gravely ill." So the Messenger of Allah arose, came to him, sat at his head, and found him in a serious condition. He said: "O Fatima, it is Allah the Almighty who gave him this, and He is able to heal him." Then Jibril (peace be upon him) came to him and said: "O Muhammad, Allah the Almighty has not sent down to you a chapter of the Qur'an except that there is in it a remedy, and for every remedy there is a cure — except for al-Hamd, for there is no completion without it. So pray over a cup of water, recite al-Hamd forty times over it, then pour it over him, and Allah the Almighty will heal him." The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) did that, and it was as though the boy had been freed from a restraint.

It is narrated from him (peace be upon him) that he was asked about cauterisation. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him and his family) said: "There is no objection to cauterisation. As for what is mentioned regarding its prohibition, that concerns what is feared to cause destruction or what disfigures the creation. As for what you hope will bring benefit, there is nothing wrong with it."

From the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family): he used to command that the hair be combed before sleep. He said: "Combing the hair removes dandruff, stimulates hair growth, perfumes the beard, and guards against the evil eye." He said (peace be upon him and his family): "*Al-Ajwa* is from Paradise, and in it is a cure for poison." Some of the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah's prayers be upon them) described its preparation thus: "Take *Ajwa* dates, remove their stones, crush them well, and knead them with old cow's ghee. Set it aside, and if it is needed when someone has taken poison, it will benefit them."

It is narrated from him (may Allah's prayers be upon him): the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) was stung by a scorpion and rubbed the spot, then said: "May Allah curse you — for you spare neither believer nor disbeliever from your sting." He then pressed it with his thumb until it was dissolved. He also said: "If people knew what was in salt, they would have no need of any antidote."

It is narrated from him (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: "Whoever eats twenty-one raisins of *Ajwa* every morning on an empty stomach will not fall ill with any sickness that causes death." And: "Whoever eats seven dates at his sleeping time will be spared from colic and the worms in his stomach will be killed." He said (may Allah's prayers be upon him) to Ja'far ibn Abi Talib (may Allah's prayers be upon him): "All quinces give strength."


<!-- page 89 -->
This book, a brief work on the effects of remedies, records that the heart emboldens the coward. It is narrated that the Prophet (peace be upon him and his family) said that honey is a healing. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said that Allah Almighty has mentioned honey in His Book, wherein He said it is a healing for people.

It is narrated from him (peace be upon him and his family) that al-Shabram is hot and sharp, and that one should seek a cure through it. He said that if death claims something, sins will hasten it, and that one should treat illness with the black seed — for if his nation knew what benefit lay in it, they would treat themselves with it even if it weighed as much as gold. He forbade (peace be upon him and his family) eating clay and charcoal, and said that Allah Almighty created Adam from clay and therefore forbade eating clay for his descendants. Whoever eats clay has harmed himself, and whoever persists in eating clay has brought about his own ruin.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said that eating clay breeds hypocrisy. It is also narrated that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said that eating soft fish dissolves the body, and so one should not become accustomed to eating it. If one does eat it, he should say: "O Allah, bless it for us."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: "Whoever eats fish, let him eat dates afterward, otherwise it will harm him." The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) forbade drinking stagnant water and rainwater that has been heated by the sun. Similarly, rainwater that falls in the heat and runs along the ground becomes harmful. For this reason, he forbade drinking it — not because it is forbidden or impure, but because of his aversion to the harm it causes.

It is narrated from him (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: "Whoever is a physician — whether Arab or non-Arab — should not treat a patient until he has obtained a written declaration from the patient releasing him from liability, witnessed by witnesses. He should fear Allah Almighty and strive diligently, and advise sincerely; that is, he should be a learned physician and not treat people while he is ignorant, for if he does so, he bears responsibility for what he destroys."

It is narrated from him (peace be upon him) that he used to say: "Whoever wishes to preserve his health — or words to that effect — let him lighten his evening meal, let him wear sandals, let him eat morning and evening meals, and let him reduce intercourse." He was asked: "O Commander of the Faithful, what does it mean to lighten the evening meal?" He replied: "It is the religion itself."

The prayers of Allah be upon him, he said that the truffle is from the manna, and its water is a cure for the eye. Some of the Ahl al-Bayt described its preparation as follows: take a truffle and wash it until it is clean, then squeeze out its water into a vessel and heat it over a fire until it thickens; then add musk to it. This preparation removes all eye ailments. If the saliva dries up, he would moisten it with water from the sky and be cured by it. It is narrated from him (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: "Nothing revives the soul

<!-- page 90 -->
like eating fresh, moist food." Allah Almighty fed Maryam fresh dates in her moment of need. It is also narrated from him (peace be upon him and his family) that he said: "Eat pomegranate with its pith, for it is a tanner for the stomach." He said: "You should eat apples, for they are a soother for the stomach."

It is narrated from him (peace be upon him and his family) that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said: "Celery — there is no leaf of it except that water from heaven rests upon it. You must consume it, for it purifies the heart and mind, increases eyesight, and clears the head of stones." He (may Allah bless him and his family) used to step upon the cress — which is the watercress, the foolish herb — and he would dwell near al-Ramada. He blessed it, ate it, and was fond of it. He said (may Allah's prayers be upon him): "The patient is cured by drinking honey."

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he was asked about a man who seeks treatment from a Jew or a Christian. He said: "There is nothing wrong with that, for healing is in the hands of Allah Almighty."

It is narrated that he (may Allah's prayers be upon him) was asked: if a woman has a disease in her body, or a fracture or wound in a place that is not permissible to look upon, is it allowed for her to uncover it to the one who treats it? He said: "There is nothing wrong with that if it is necessary." Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) was also asked about a man who practices medicine and takes payment for it, and he said: "It is permissible."

It is narrated from him (peace be upon him) that he said: "If people were moderate in their food and drink, their bodies would remain sound." He said (peace be upon him): "Abandoning the evening meal destroys the body." He said: "Allah Almighty says that they have their provision — morning and evening meals." A man complained to him of pain and hunger, and he said: "Eat your midday and evening meals, and do not eat anything in between, for that is the corruption of the body." He said: "Abandoning the evening meal diminishes a strength that cannot be regained." The Shaykh said: "Even if it is only one bite." He said: "Eat meat with milk, for it makes the flesh grow and strengthens the bone." The Prophet (peace be upon him) said: "A prophet among the prophets complained to Allah Almighty about his lack of offspring, so He commanded him to eat meat with eggs."

It is narrated from him (peace be upon him and his family) that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said to Ali: "O Ali, sleep on your back. Chew your food thoroughly. If your neighbors cannot benefit from the meat of your food, let them benefit at least from its broth, and seal it. Cut a thread — it will brighten your sight. Anoint yourself, for it is in accordance with the Sunnah of your Prophet. Wear sandals, for they are the footwear of men. Turbans are the crowns of the Arabs. When you cook a pot of soup, add more broth.

<!-- page 91 -->
If they cannot be given of its meat, let them be given of its broth, and seal it with a ruby or garnet, for it is blessed — whenever a man looks at his face in it, it increases his light." He said: "The prayer offered in it carries seventy prayers, and it is concluded at your right hand, for that is the Sunnah of the Messengers. Whoever abandons my Sunnah is not of me." He said: "Do not seal it with other than ruby or garnet."

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that a man from among his companions complained to him of pain in his loins. He said to him: "Pick up what falls from the food of the brothers." The man followed this advice and said: "I humbled myself and was healed." He said: "Eat of the food that falls from the brothers, for there is healing in it, Allah willing, for every disease."

A man saw apples in the hands of Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him), and he said to him (may Allah's prayers be upon him): "The apple cools the heat, extinguishes fever, and removes it." He mentioned vinegar (may Allah's prayers be upon him) and said that it tightens the stomach and kills worms in the belly.

He said (peace be upon him): "Rice sawiq — if it is roasted and ground — tightens the belly. Some of it tightens the belly." He said: "Sawiq builds the flesh and strengthens the bone. It is beneficial for one who has fever, even if it is washed three times." He said: "Take sawiq dry on an empty stomach, for it dispels phlegm and bitterness."

It is narrated from Ali ibn al-Husayn (may Allah be pleased with him) that he permitted treatment with donkey's milk, and that if one finds the milk of a wild donkey, it is better.

<!-- page 92 -->
