<!-- page 93 -->
This book is a brief work on the effects of remedies. Volume Two.

Chapter on Gathering: Dress and Related Matters.

<!-- page 94 -->
This book is a brief work on the effects of remedies. Volume Two.

<!-- page 95 -->
On the Etiquette of Dress. Volume Two.

We narrate from the Imams (may Allah's prayers be upon them) that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said: "If a man buys a garment for a dinar, or half a dinar, or a third of a dinar, and then praises Allah Almighty when he puts it on and it reaches his knees, he will be forgiven." It is narrated from him (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that when he put on a shirt, he would say: "When I wear it, I thank Allah, who provided me with clothing with which to adorn myself before people, to cover my nakedness, and to conceal my faults. Praise be to Allah, Lord of the Worlds." He (peace be upon him) said: "I heard the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) say this when he put on a garment."

He said that a man wearing clothes should find nothing objectionable if Allah has blessed him with a grace and He wishes to see its effect upon him in his clothing, provided he does not seek fame thereby. He said (peace be upon him): "I heard the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) forbid the trailing of the lower garment." He said to Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him): "Trailing was the practice of the Umayyads." It involves letting the robe, shirt, and garments hang below the ankle, and trailing of this kind is forbidden. He said: "The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said: 'Whoever acquires a garment, let him keep it clean.'" Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: "The cleanliness of a garment suppresses the enemy, and washing it removes grief and sorrow. Folding garments keeps them clean." The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said: "The comfort of a garment is in its being folded, and the rest of the house is in its being kept orderly."

He (may Allah's prayers be upon him) once addressed the people while wearing a patched garment. He was questioned about it and replied: "The heart should be humbled, and the believer should imitate him." A believer had bought a shirt for three dirhams, put it on, and cut it at the fingers, leaving the excess, but did not trim it further. A man looked at Abu Abdullah (may Allah's prayers be upon him) and saw on him a raised, worn garment. He became fixed in staring at it, and Abu Abdullah, understanding this (peace be upon him), took what was in his hands and turned it over, then threw a portion of it to the man, saying: "Look at this."

He said: "A new garment is for the one for whom there is no worn one." It is narrated from him (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that if the body wears new, soft clothing, it becomes tyrannical. He said: "We treat ourselves with such things." He had two rough garments in which he prayed, and when he wished to supplicate to Allah Almighty, he wore them. These were from the clothes of humility, which those who preferred Allah's prayers chose out of humility. As for the one whom Allah has made prosperous, he may adorn himself, and it is permissible to wear the finest of clothes. They (peace be upon them) used to wear such garments. It is narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) that

<!-- page 96 -->
he brought two fine silk garments and sent them to Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him), and he put them on.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) narrated that al-Husayn ibn Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) was wounded on a certain day — they counted forty wounds upon him, combining stabbings and blows. He said: "The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) sent Abdullah ibn Abbas to the Kharijites to argue against them, and commanded him to wear his finest clothes." So he took off his clothes and dressed in his finest, mounted his best horse, and went to them. But they objected, and he recited to them: "Say: Who has forbidden the adornment of Allah that He has brought forth for His servants, and the good things of provision?" (Al-A'raf: 32).

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: "Dress and adorn yourself with what is halal, for Allah Almighty loves beauty." It is narrated that he (may Allah's prayers be upon him) went out on a cold day wearing a yellow and exotic veil. He saw the people looking at him and said: "He was a prophet, son of a prophet, son of a prophet, son of a prophet" — meaning Yusuf (peace be upon him) — "who wore brocade garments buttoned with gold and sat upon his throne to judge among the people."

Ali ibn al-Husayn (may Allah's prayers be upon him) used to wear two garments in the summer, each bought for five hundred dirhams, and would wear fur in the winter. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) was asked about a man who acquires many garments to adorn himself and to protect himself — whether this constitutes excess. He said: "No, it is not extravagance, for Allah Almighty says: 'Let the one who has the capacity spend according to his capacity.'" (Al-Talaq: 7).

Sufyan al-Thawri entered upon him and saw that he was wearing fine garments. He said: "O son of the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family), you have told us that Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) used to wear coarse clothes and rough fabric, yet here you are wearing al-Quhi and al-Muwri." He replied: "O Sufyan, the prayers of Allah be upon him — that was a time of hardship and difficulty. Allah Almighty has commanded that the one whom He has blessed with abundance should show the effect of that blessing upon himself." Ali ibn al-Husayn (may Allah's prayers be upon him) used to have fine garments bought for him for fifty dinars. He would wear them when he entered the mosque and prayed in them. When summer came, he would command that they be sold as charity on his behalf. He would command that two garments known among the clothes of Egypt be bought for him — two pieces — which he would wear in the summer, and he would wear what


<!-- page 97 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on Transmitted Reports

Between fine garments and coarse ones, and when he mentioned this, he recited the words of Allah the Almighty:

"Say: Who has forbidden the adornment of Allah which He has brought forth for His servants, and the good things of sustenance?" (47)

May Allah bless him and grant him peace — he approached a people who wore wool and hair and said to them: "Wear cotton." That was the clothing of the Messenger of Allah, and it was the finest thing he could find, which is our dress. He did not wear wool or hair, so do not wear them except out of necessity or when nothing else is available, for Allah the Almighty is beautiful and loves beauty, and loves to see the effect of His blessing upon His servant.

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he performed Hajj while in a state of ihram wearing two thin garments. As he was performing tawaf in them, a man pulled at the end of his robe. He turned to find that it was 'Abbad al-Basri, who said to him: "O Abu 'Abdullah, you wear such garments in such a place, and you are of the lineage of 'Ali, peace be upon him — knowing your station and knowing how his clothing was." He replied, peace be upon him: "I had to pray for him in a time when he wore what he wore. If I were to wear his clothing today, people would say: 'This one resembles 'Abbad al-Basri.'" And 'Abbad was described with hypocrisy, so he became flustered and ashamed, while those around him exchanged glances.

It is also narrated from him that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, said: "Maintain turbans, for they are the crowns of the Arabs." And it is narrated that Abu Ja'far Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, received some of his companions while he was in a furnished house with cushions, decorated coverings, and fine adornments. Then they entered upon him on another occasion and found him in a house furnished with mats. They said to him: "May we be your ransom — what is this house?" He said: "This is my house, and the house you saw before is my wife's house. Let me tell you something my father told me. He said: Some people once entered upon al-Husayn, may Allah's prayers be upon him, and in his house were carpets, rugs, and mattresses. They said: 'O son of the Messenger of Allah, we see in your house things that were not in the house of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him.' He said: 'We marry women and give them their dowries, which they use to furnish and prepare their homes.'"

As for dress — whether fine or coarse, its display and its concealment — all of it is permissible, and there is nothing disliked in it. Whoever wears humble clothing when he can afford otherwise, there is nothing upon him; and whoever wears fine clothing, finding it available and appreciating it —

(47) Al-A'raf: 32

<!-- page 98 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on Transmitted Reports

— it is permissible for him, and it is recommended to show the grace of Allah the Almighty. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, wore both fine and coarse clothing — whatever he wore and whatever others clothed him in.

**On what is permissible and what is forbidden or disliked in clothing:**

It is narrated to us from the Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them, that clothing made from anything that grows from the earth — and covering oneself with it and praying in it and upon it — is lawful. The skin of every animal whose flesh is permissible to eat, along with its wool, hair, and fur, is likewise permissible. If one is discerning and dresses in the wool, hair, and fur of a dead animal, it is permissible to wear it, cover oneself with it, and put it on — though anything that is not permissible to eat is impure as we have mentioned, and one may not pray in any of it, just as one may not pray in an impure garment.

The Imams disliked men wearing pure silk clothing and considered it beneath them, though it was not rendered impure. It is narrated that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, permitted it for certain people. It is permissible to display it and adorn oneself with it before enemies, so that they may recognize the strength of the Muslims — as Allah says: "And prepare against them whatever force you are able." (48) The harness of the horse with which you frighten an enemy of His is included in this.

The Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them, also permitted silk woven with other materials — whether pure or mixed. They permitted it before the enemies of Allah to display the glory of Islam and the Muslims, and to impress those appointed over them and their enemies from all peoples — even though the world and all it contains is the most trivial of things to them. Yet they display from it readiness and the power of Islam and the Muslims, showing distinction before those appointed to them and before their enemies among all people.

It is narrated that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, used to love white clothing and would say: "It is among your best garments, so wear it and shroud your dead in it." Nothing of forbidden dye is part of his dress. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, and the Imams of his family used to take some dyes and avoid others. It is narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: "Saffron is ours, and yellow is for the Umayyads." And Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, disliked an intensely saturated red, saying: "It is the garment of Qarun and the dye of the sons of Umayyah." He used to wear saffron that was not excessive. It is also narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he went out to

(48) Al-Anfal: 60

<!-- page 99 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on Transmitted Reports

the people of Kufa one day from the palace to the Rahba wearing a yellow garment and a black khamisa, with sandals on his feet and a staff — meaning a stick upon which he leaned.

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he was seen wearing a green cloak. And from 'Ali ibn al-Husayn, may Allah's prayers be upon them, that he wore a black upper garment with blue trim. However, the Imams, may Allah bless them and grant them peace, disliked black for adornment, because the Banu al-'Abbas claimed to wear it out of grief for al-Husayn ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him — even though if there were virtue in that, it would have been obligatory upon the Imams to precede them in it, as they are of his descendants, may Allah's prayers be upon him and upon them. And if they truly grieved for him as they claimed, they would not have committed what they committed against his descendants. The view of the Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them, was that one should adorn oneself in one's customary dress. They did not see anything wrong, may Allah's prayers be upon them, with images or statues depicted on garments.

It is narrated from some of the companions of Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that one of them entered upon him while he was sitting on a carpet containing images. He said to him — and the Sunnah indicates — that one should tread upon it. He mentioned that some people disliked representational images because Allah the Almighty created them. He said: "Or is it not He who created everything — trees and inanimate objects — all that is seen is a depiction?" They depicted it and saw nothing wrong in it. So what is the difference between one and the other?

**On Adornments and What May Be Worn of Them:**

We have narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's prayers be upon them, that all of it is narrated from him, may Allah bless him and his family. He permitted women to adorn themselves with gold, and it was asked of Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him. He said: "My father, peace be upon him, used to adorn his children and women with gold and silver." He said: "There is nothing wrong with ornamenting swords and copies of the Qur'an with gold and silver." It is narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, say: "A woman should not pray unless she has a garment upon her and above — that is, if she is able to find it." He forbade women from discarding their adornments and from imitating men, and he cursed those who did so among them. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "A woman should not leave herself unadorned — even if only by hanging a necklace around her neck." The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, forbade women, when they go out, from wearing adornments that produce an audible sound. This is in accordance with the saying of Allah the Almighty: "And let them not strike their feet so as to reveal —" (49)

<!-- page 100 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on Transmitted Reports

It is narrated from him that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, said — regarding what they conceal of their adornments that may displease their husbands — that Allah knows what they hide of their adornments. (49)

It is narrated that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, forbade men from sealing with gold. His own seal, may Allah bless him and his family, was of silver with a lobe of it, engraved with "Muhammad, Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him."

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, forbade sealing with iron and adorning oneself with iron, saying: "It is the adornment of the people of Hell." It is narrated from him that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, used to place his seal on his right hand. It is also narrated from the Imams that they sealed their rings in their right hands and commanded their followers and companions to do likewise.

It is narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, say: "Whoever seals with a red carnelian — Allah seals for him with goodness." He used to recommend it, along with rubies, and would say: "Whenever a man looks into it, light increases upon his face, and the prayer in it is equivalent to seventy prayers." He also said: "A fine crystal lobe" — and a ring was engraved for 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, forbade sealing with a lobe that bears an engraved image. The engraving of his own seal was: "My Lord, You are my trust — protect me from the evil of Your creation." It is narrated from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, that he used to wear his seal facing inward toward the palm of his hand.

**On Fragrance, Its Virtue and Its Desirability:**

It is narrated to us from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's prayers be upon them, from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: "The Prophets were given three perfumes: marriage, the siwak, and fragrance." The Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family, when he traveled, carried with him a bottle, scissors, kohl, and a comb, a siwak, and other items. He said, may Allah bless him and his family: "A slave's garment is not made deficient except that his flaw is reduced, nor does his scent become pleasant except that his intellect increases."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "A pleasant fragrance draws the heart near and increases good sense. The excellence of a man's fragrance is when its scent is evident and its color hidden; and the excellence of a woman's fragrance is when its color is apparent and its scent concealed — except when she goes out, for then it is disliked that she perfume herself." Some narrations also mention this. It is narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family —

(49) Al-Nur: 31

<!-- page 101 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on Transmitted Reports

May Allah bless him and his family — he used to apply fragrance until the color of his hair changed. And he said, may Allah bless him and his family: "Whoever attends Friday prayer, let him apply even a drop from his family's perfume bottle." From 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, it is narrated that he would sometimes accept the kindness of his wives with regard to perfume — meaning fragrance for women that does not show its color but only what appears of it, such as a creature and the like.

Men are disliked from perfuming themselves except with their wives. The Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them, disliked returning a gift of fragrance to one who had offered it. It is likewise discouraged to send it back as water. Al-Ghayz said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: Al-Mansur, may Allah sanctify his soul, told me — he came one day when I was a young boy to the table of al-Mahdi, may Allah's prayers be upon him, and we were a group of his sons and the sons of his son, eating. A slave girl stood offering us water. She offered it to one of the boys of al-Mahdi, may Allah's prayers be upon him, who refused it. He said to him: "Had your food not been forbidden, I would have considered it well-mannered not to ask for it. But when you offered him water and he did not ask for it — water is more honorable than to be offered to one who did not ask for it, or to be returned to one to whom it was offered." Al-Mansur said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "I had not known anything like him at that time, but when I came to know, I recognized his intention." May Allah's prayers be upon him in excellence as well.

From 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, it is narrated that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, used to anoint himself with musk and recommended that it be placed in his embalming. And something better than it was used over him, may Allah's prayers be upon him, in his embalming. And on the authority of the Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them, it is narrated: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, forbade a woman to pray unless she applies henna or touches the place of henna with something. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: Therefore, a woman should not leave off the dye — even if she is elderly — even if she merely wipes her hand with henna as an anointing. And she should not imitate men.

From 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, it is narrated that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, said: "It is not for a menstruating woman to tell a story or engrave." From 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "The Children of Israel perished through stories, engravings, henna, and excessive ornamentation."

<!-- page 105 -->
A discussion of what is permissible in hunting and what is forbidden:

Allah the Almighty said: "He has made permissible for you the hunting of the sea and its food, as a pleasure for you and for travelers, and forbidden for you is the hunting of land as long as you are in the sacred state" (50), and He said: "But when you leave the sacred state, then hunt" (51). Thus the Almighty permitted sea fishing even while one is in the sacred state. He said this applies to the generality of the text, except for what was excluded from it by the Messenger of Allah and the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt, which is what is meant by His saying: "And We have sent down to you the Remembrance that you may make clear to the people what has been revealed to them" (52).

We have narrated from the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he forbade hunting birds in their nests, and said: "The bird in its nest is under the protection of Allah; but if it takes to the air with its wings, then hunt it if you wish — set a trap for it and throw at it." It was narrated from him (prayers of Allah be upon him) and from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (prayers of Allah be upon them both) that pigeons should not be hunted with snares, because they are among what belongs to their owners. But if they fly freely, they may be hunted. We have mentioned in the chapters on food what is permissible to eat from the animals of land and sea, and what is forbidden. Therefore, what is caught from that which is permissible to eat is eaten, and what is caught from what is forbidden to eat is left and not eaten, except in cases of dire necessity for the one in need.

A discussion of what raptors catch from prey and kill:

Allah the Almighty said: "They ask you what has been made lawful for them. Say: Lawful for you are all good things, and what you have trained of hunting animals — training them as Allah has taught you — so eat of what they have caught for you, and mention the name of Allah over it, and fear Allah. Indeed, Allah is swift in reckoning" (53).

We have narrated from the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him):

It was narrated from Ali (prayers of Allah be upon him and upon them all) that he said: If birds catch prey and it dies before its slaughter can be performed, it is not to be eaten. But if the raptor is trained and the Muslim sends it and invokes the name of Allah upon releasing it, then if it catches prey and the prey dies, it may be eaten. If the hunter forgets to invoke the name of Allah at the moment of release, he invokes it when he remembers. But if he deliberately omits it, the prey is not to be eaten. The term for wounding in the language of the Arabs in which the Qur'an was revealed refers to the striking of prey, and the like.

(50) Al-Ma'idah: 96
(51) Al-Ma'idah: 2
(52) Al-Nahl: 44
(53) Al-Ma'idah: 4

<!-- page 106 -->
The Arabs say: "So-and-so wounded his family," meaning what he gained for them. This applies to dogs and the like. The birds of prey used for hunting are those which bring back the catch for their owners when they have been properly trained — even if they are not trained — provided they seize the prey while it is still alive, so that the hunter can slaughter it. If the prey is not alive when recovered, it is not eaten. They said (peace be upon them) that what is caught by predatory birds may be eaten even if its slaughter was not performed before death. They said (peace be upon them) that all known predatory animals — such as cheetahs and others of the earth's predators, as well as predatory birds — may have their catch eaten.

It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) that he said: "Dogs used for hunting are those trained for it, and they are the ones commanded to be employed for this purpose." They are all alike in this ruling, except that among the Kurds and the greyhound breeds, one may eat what they have caught if they are trained. They also said: if the hunter reaches the prey while it is still alive after a raptor has struck it, no harm comes from the raptor's strike, and if it then dies in his hands from that strike, the prey may be eaten. But if the raptor did not strike it and it was carried off and died afterwards, it is not to be eaten.

It was narrated from Ali (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he forbade eating what dies before its slaughter can be performed. The dogs of the Magi and the Christians among the Arabs were not considered trained raptors, except for those trained afterwards by Muslims.

A discussion of what is killed by projectiles:

Allah the Almighty said: "O you who believe, Allah will surely test you with something of the game that your hands and spears can reach" (54).

It was narrated from the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt of the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him): A man asked about hunting — he had thrown at prey, struck it, and it died. He was told: "Whatever you have aimed at deliberately, do not eat what has fallen out of your sight, for the uncertainty is in the throw: the man throws and strikes, and it dies in that very place; but development occurs when it strikes and the prey moves on and goes out of sight until it hides from him and disappears and is then found dead — he does not know whether it died from his throw or from something else."

It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (prayers of Allah be upon him) that he said: If he shoots at fish with an arrow and strikes it mortally, then loses sight of it, but later finds the arrow in it and knows that it died from his shot, he may eat it. But if he does not know this,

(54) Al-Ma'idah: 94

<!-- page 107 -->
he does not eat it. It was narrated from Ali (prayers of Allah be upon him) that he said: If he throws at prey, strikes it, and then it falls from a height or falls into water and dies, he does not eat it unless he knows that it died from the blow he struck. Whenever we mention what is thrown at or struck and then dies, it is permissible to eat it if it has been struck with a sword, spear, arrow, or iron implement. But what is struck with a stick, stone, or blunt object is not to be eaten according to the saying of the Ahl al-Bayt (prayers be upon them), unless its slaughter is performed while it is still alive. An exception is made when the hunter has no arrow other than one without feathers — if he throws it and it strikes and kills the prey, it is eaten. When a rope is set for a fish and it dies in it before its slaughter can be performed, it is not eaten. If a limb, leg, or organ is severed from the prey while it is still alive and its slaughter is subsequently performed, the animal is eaten except for the severed organ. The rope refers to what is set up like nets, traps, and the like.

<!-- page 108 -->
<!-- page 109 -->
Doors of Sacrifices

<!-- page 110 -->
<!-- page 111 -->
A discussion of the acts of slaughterers:

We narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that they narrated from him (peace be upon him) that he said: Whoever slaughters a sacrifice, let him sharpen his blade and give his animal ease.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali said: If you intend to slaughter a sacrifice, do not cause the animal distress by sharpening the blade in front of it. Face it toward the qiblah, invoke the name of Allah, and do not restrain it until it dies — meaning do not cut off its head. They also forbade skinning it until it has died and its movement has ceased. They said (prayers of Allah be upon them): Whoever slaughters and forgets to invoke the name, or fails to face the sacrifice toward the qiblah, or neglects the name of Allah the Almighty, should invoke the name of Allah when he remembers, and the sacrifice may be eaten. But if he deliberately omits it, his sacrifice is not to be eaten. Allah the Almighty mentioned what should be said at slaughter, and they preferred that one say at the time of slaughtering: "In the name of Allah, and Allah is the Greatest." They narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) that he forbade the use of animals as targets, and ordered that slaughter be performed properly when necessary, and forbade al-sabr and al-muthla. Al-sabr refers to imprisoning, burying, or tying up an animal and casting it until it dies.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (prayers of Allah be upon him) said: Whoever kills a bird in vain, Allah the Almighty will bring it before him on the Day of Resurrection and it will cry out, saying: "O Lord, this person killed me without slaughtering me — why?" So beware of such an act. He also said (peace be upon him): Slaughter at the throat — meaning the proper place of slaughter — and slaughter is the cut at the knot in the throat; the head is to be left attached. The slaughterer must cut the throat, the esophagus, and the two jugular veins, being careful not to cut into the bone, for this is the Sunnah of slaughter.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn al-Husayn was asked (prayers of Allah be upon him and his family) about breaking the neck of the sacrifice before it dies. They said (peace be upon them): Whoever does so by accident or out of ignorance — meaning the knife slips and cuts off the head of the sacrifice without intention — there is no objection to this and the sacrifice may be eaten. But if he does so deliberately, he has sinned and his sacrifice is not to be eaten. They also said (peace be upon them): If the animal escapes or refuses to be presented for slaughter and cannot be slaughtered, or falls into a well and cannot be lifted or reached at the throat, then it may be stabbed with a spear, struck with a sword, or struck with iron until it dies, and it is then eaten. They said (peace be upon them): Slaughter must be performed only with iron — not with stone, tooth, nail, bone, or any other material. It is only to be done with iron.

<!-- page 112 -->
A discussion of what is eaten from sacrifices and what is not eaten from them:

We narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and upon them all) that they regarded as disliked the slaughter of a nursing mother and of a foetus, though they did not forbid it outright; rather, they disliked it because of the benefit that could be obtained from them by others. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (prayers of Allah be upon him) was asked about the sacrifices of Jews, Christians, Magians, and those who hold divergent beliefs. The words of Allah the Almighty were recited: "Eat not of that over which the name of Allah has not been mentioned, if you are believers in His signs" (55), and also: "If you hear them invoking the name of Allah, eat; but if they do not invoke the name of Allah, do not eat it." Whoever among them is known to have abandoned the invocation of the name, it is permissible to eat his sacrifice, except when one actually witnesses and hears at the moment of slaughter that he has not invoked the name of Allah. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (prayers of Allah be upon him) said: There is no objection to meat sold in the markets of Muslims unless it is known that the slaughterers have deviated from the Sunnah in their method of slaughter, as observed from their practice.

It was narrated from them (peace be upon them) that they regarded the sacrifices of Magians and Arab Christians as disliked, while they permitted the slaughter of a blind person if he is directed toward the qiblah and performs the slaughter well. Likewise, women may slaughter if they do so properly, and a boy before he reaches adulthood may slaughter if he does so properly and there is a need for it. They saw no objection to slaughter performed in the other direction, and they permitted a mute person to offer a sacrifice if the rational invocation is intended in his heart.

A discussion of the intelligent and unintelligent among sacrificial animals:

We narrated from the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him), from Ali (prayers of Allah be upon him), that he was asked about the foetuses of camels whose mothers are slaughtered while they are still in the womb. If the foetus emerges, he said: "Its slaughter is subsumed in the slaughter of its mother, and it is as one of her organs — if it comes out alive and is left to die, it is then eaten, provided it has shown signs of life such as movement or a tremor or some such sign in its body, even in its tail, for the tail is the first thing in which sensation is felt." He regarded as disliked what shows no signs of sensation or movement.

He (prayers of Allah be upon him) was asked about organs cut from a living animal — such as the fatty tail of a sheep, the hooves of camels, and the like — and he said: "What is cut from them while they are still alive, before slaughter, is dead, and the rest of the animal is not to be eaten if it is subsequently slaughtered." And he said (peace be upon him): Know that

(55) Al-An'am: 118


<!-- page 113 -->
Signs of intelligent slaughter are extreme movements: the animal runs, or the guilt moves, or the ear twitches. Whoever slaughters his sacrifice and she moves, or becomes agitated after slaughtering, or flutters in a certain way, or any part of her stirs — she is considered intelligently slaughtered. Whatever fell from her was slaughtered, and if nothing moved from her it is not to be eaten, even if blood came out of her during the slaughter.

He, peace be upon him, was asked about a sacrifice that is slaughtered and then falls from a height or into water. He said: "If you found the slaughter fulfilled and the obligation in it was met, then eat it."

It was said that the Sunnah in slaughter is to proceed in a measured and reasonable manner. The slaughter of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, is the exemplary way, and it is permissible if that is not possible. It is disliked to slaughter the sheep while standing except for a reason, and the Sunnah prescribes that it be laid down even if it is slaughtered while upright — there is no harm in that. He was asked about a sacrifice slaughtered from behind the skull; he said: If he was ignorant of that, there is no harm, but if he did so deliberately while knowing the Sunnah, he ought to observe proper conduct. He was asked about two sheep, one of which moved with signs of life and the other did not move, and they did not know which was which. He said: Do not eat either of them until you know which one showed signs of life.

**Mention of the Sacrificial Offerings:**

We have narrated from the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, that he addressed the people before the day of sacrifice and said: "O people, whoever has the capacity, let him sacrifice and magnify the rites of God. Whoever does not have the capacity — God Almighty does not burden a soul beyond its means."

It was narrated from him that a man from the Ansar met him on the day of al-Adha after he had left the prayer and said: "O Messenger of Allah, honor me today by coming to my home, for I have prepared food for you." The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, went with him. The man said: "O Messenger of Allah, I slaughtered two sacrificial animals before I went out to the prayer ground and ordered my family to prepare this food that you have never had before." The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said to him: "If you have anything else, sacrifice it." The man said: "I have nothing but a young animal." He said: "Sacrifice it, though it will not be permissible for anyone after you."

The Sunnah in sacrifice is that it is not slaughtered except after the Eid prayer on the day of sacrifice. Whoever slaughters before the prayer, it does not count as a sacrifice — he must repeat it if he is able. It is best to slaughter on the day of sacrifice, and it may be done on the days of Mina, which are the day of the sacrifice,

<!-- page 114 -->
al-Adha, or the three days following it. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him, said that sacrifice is not rewarded in camels and cows except for a mature animal, and the mature young is rewarded from sheep. He said, peace be upon him: "The sacrifice is a duty for every Muslim who is able; whoever cannot find one is excused." The sacrifice of a man is rewarded for himself and for his household.

They narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, that he addressed the people on the day of sacrifice and said: "This is the day of the shedding of blood. Whoever offers his sacrifice with sincere intention, his first drop is an expiation for every sin." The supplication was made to Allah Almighty, who breathed life into Muhammad in His hand: "No one will leave this state unless he is forgiven, except the one who is a great sinner who insists upon sin and does not bring himself to abandon it."

It was narrated from him that the Prophet, peace be upon him, entered upon Fatimah, peace be upon her, on the day of al-Adha and said: "O Fatimah, arise and witness your sacrifice, for the first drop of its blood is an expiation for all your sins. Its flesh, its dung, its bones, its wool, and everything from it will be placed in your balance and multiplied seventy times for you." When al-Miqdad heard this, he said: "By my father and mother, O Messenger of Allah — is this something specified for the family of Muhammad?" He said: "No, it is for all Muslims in general."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him, said: It is desirable for a man to slaughter his sacrifice with his own hand. If he is unable, he should place his hand with the hand of the slaughterer. If he is unable to do that, he should stand over it, pronouncing the name of Allah upon it until it is slaughtered.

It is narrated from Ali, prayers of Allah be upon him, that he said: The sacrifice of a Muslim should not be slaughtered except by a Muslim. He should say when slaughtering it: "In the name of Allah and Allah is the greatest. I have directed my face toward the One who created the heavens and the earth, as a true Muslim, and I am not one of the polytheists. My prayer, my sacrifice, my life, and my death are for Allah, Lord of the Worlds, who has no partner. This is what I have been commanded, and I am the first of the Muslims."

From Ja'far ibn Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him: He was asked about the best sacrificial animals and said: "The females of camels are best, then the males from them, then females from cows, then males from them, then stallion rams from sheep, then ewes from them" — meaning those whose females are satisfied and fattened — "then the ewes, then the stallion goat, then the females." He disliked the one with cut ears. The best of all these types is the most magnificent and greatest in form. The finest of rams is the horned stallion that eats with blackness and drinks with blackness and walks with blackness and looks at blackness and urinates with blackness and rests in blackness. With such a sacrifice the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him,

<!-- page 115 -->
used to sacrifice. It was also said that he used to sacrifice two salt-colored rams. It was said that this description matches the ram that was revealed to Ibrahim, peace be upon him and his family.

From Ja'far ibn Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him: It is permissible to share in a sacrifice for one who cannot find a unique animal for himself but has the means to participate. From Ali, prayers of Allah be upon him: He disliked sacrificing an animal with a broken horn, or one that is lame, or one that is weak and emaciated, or one whose ear has been cut off. The severed one is that in which the incision is in the ear if it is a mark, or one that is old if there is no defect or imperfection in it.

Prayers of Allah be upon him: Whoever buys a sacrifice and it falls ill or dies before the day of sacrifice, he is permitted to slaughter it. If he can obtain another in its place and do so, that is better. From Ja'far ibn Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him: He was asked about the words of Allah, the Almighty: *"So eat of it and feed the contented and the petitioner"* (Hajj: 36). He said: "Feed of it the poor who are given to without asking, and the poor who ask, and the destitute who find nothing." The poor wretch is the worst off and the most straitened. He said: If Abu Radiwan, may Allah be pleased with him, slaughtered his sacrifice at Mina or slaughtered his gift animal, he would hold the head with his hands. If a questioner passed by and stopped, he would say: "Do not let the head go — if he sees it he will take it with joy." Then he would say: "Leave it," and give him some of the meat. If he offered it to someone and he did not accept, he would leave it and give him nothing, so as to test the condition of the poor.

From Ali, prayers of Allah be upon him: In the words of Allah Almighty — (Hajj: 36) — he said: "It is a matter of recommendation, not obligation. Whoever wishes, he eats from his sacrifice; whoever wishes, he does not eat from it. To eat from it is better." It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, that he had associated Ali with a gift animal, and it was of considerable size, so he ordered some of its flesh to be placed in a pot and cooked. He then prayed and ate as much of the meat as he was able, and they were all able to eat of it and tasted the broth. The Messenger of Allah said: "Broth is one of the two meats, because the strength of the meat comes out in it." Ja'far ibn was asked —

(Hajj: 36) (Hajj: 28) (Hajj: 36)

<!-- page 116 -->
Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him, about the extent of what should be given in charity from the sacrifices. He said: Ali ibn al-Husayn, prayers of Allah be upon him, and Abu Ja'far Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him, would divide it thus: one third to neighbors, one third in charity, and one third kept for the household — not in humiliation of its value. What one gives in charity from it is better. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, said: "Allah made this sacrifice to fill your poor with meat, so feed them."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him: It is disliked to sell any part of the sacrifice, and it is similarly disliked to use its skin and wool as payment for the slaughterer. It is disliked to feed from it to non-Muslims, for it is an act of nearness to Allah Almighty. He was asked about storing the meat of sacrifice, as the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, had forbidden it. He said: He forbade it because of the people's need at that time, but today it is permissible.

**Mention of the 'Aqiqah:**

We narrated from the Ahl al-Bayt of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, that he commanded the shaving of the 'aqiqah — that is, the hair with which the newborn is born on his head — on the seventh day. He said: "Every newborn is pledged with his 'aqiqah; his parents either fulfill it or leave it." He performed the 'aqiqah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, for al-Hasan and al-Husayn, prayers of Allah be upon them, sacrificing a sheep for each of them and shaving the head of each on the seventh day, giving charity in silver equal to the weight of the hair. He said: "Whoever performs the 'aqiqah for his son, let him give the midwife the quarter of the back of the sacrificial animal." The animal is called the 'aqiqah after the hair, because it is slaughtered on account of it.

It was narrated from Ali, prayers of Allah be upon him, that he said: The 'aqiqah is to cut the animal into its limbs and cook it, then give in charity from it, eat of it, and give gifts from it. He said: The 'aqiqah for a boy and for a girl is a single ram for each.

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, prayers of Allah be upon him, that it is recommended to name the newborn on the seventh day. He said that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said: "Give your children good names." He said: The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, prohibited four names as kunyahs: Abu 'Issa, Abu al-Hakam, Abu Malik, and Abu al-Qasim. As for what is carried as a given name and is permitted in this regard — prayers of Allah be upon him and his descendants

<!-- page 117 -->
and prayers of Allah be upon them all — the Mahdi said: "From my son whose name matches my name and whose surname matches my surname."

<!-- page 118 -->

<!-- page 119 -->
**Doors of Marriage**

<!-- page 120 -->

<!-- page 121 -->
**Mentioning the Merits of Marriage, its Command and Encouragement:**

Allah Almighty said: *"And marry the unmarried among you and the righteous among your male and female slaves. If they should be poor, Allah will enrich them from His bounty, and Allah is all-Encompassing and Knowing."* (al-Nur: 32)

We have narrated from the Imams of the House of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, that he said: "Whoever loves to follow my way among my nation, let him know that marriage is one of my Sunnahs." He said: "Whoever loves to meet Allah pure and purified, let him meet Him with a wife." He said: "There is no young man who marries in his youth except that his devil cries out: 'Woe to him — he has protected two-thirds of his religion!' Let the slave then fear Allah in the remaining third." He, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, whenever he learned that one of his companions had married, would say: "He has completed his religion." He said, peace be upon him: "The more a believer believes, the more he loves women." He said, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him: "There is no monasticism in Islam. Marry, for I shall boast of your numbers before the nations." He forbade, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, celibacy and said: "When a believing man is intimate with his believing wife, he is surrounded by two angels, and he is like a drawn sword in the path of Allah. When he finishes, his sins fall from him as leaves fall from a tree. If she conceives from him, he has the reward of a fasting worshipper. If she suckles him — meaning the child — no one earns a reward from that except Allah Almighty. With every suckle she is rewarded with good and erased of evil. Even if she dies from her labor, she will rise on the Day of Resurrection without reckoning, for she died of her travail." He said, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family: "Whoever abandons marriage out of fear of family expenses has misbelieved in his Lord the Almighty, who says: *'If they should be poor, Allah will enrich them from His bounty.'"* (al-Nur: 32)

He said, prayers of Allah be upon him and his family: "Whoever accumulates wealth and does not marry two wives, the sin of those women who remain unmarried because of him rests upon him." He said, prayers of Allah be upon him: "Whenever two believers meet in a lawful marriage, a caller from heaven calls out —"


<!-- page 122 -->

This is a concise book on transmitted reports, Volume Two.

Does Allah Almighty not marry one to another, and do two believing spouses not separate through marriage? A caller from heaven announces the union, yet Allah Almighty has also authorized the separation of one from another. It is related that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, left behind nineteen secrets concerning the four women. From Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, it is reported that he was asked about a man who had been so overtaken by fear of Allah Almighty that he abandoned women, wholesome food, and could no longer raise his head toward heaven out of reverence for Allah Almighty. The questioner said: "As for your statement about abandoning women, I understand what that refers to in relation to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, but what do you say about abandoning wholesome food, when the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, used to eat meat and honey? And as for your statement that the fear of Allah Almighty entered him until he could not raise his head toward the sky out of reverence — true reverence is in the heart. Who was more fearful of Allah Almighty than the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, yet he did not do this?" Allah Almighty said — true reverence comes from the heart. (61)

**On what is recommended regarding marriage and those who wish to marry:**

It is narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, that all of them are reported to have said: "Choose well for your lineage, for the maternal uncle is one of the two formative influences." And it is said: "Do not place your seed except in purity," meaning the mother of the child should not be of impure character. This is among the reasons the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, forbade breastfeeding from an immoral woman; if this is disliked in nursing, it is even more serious in the matter of lineage. He emphasized this and said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Marry those who are compatible and give your daughters in marriage to them, and choose well for your lineage." He cautioned against the married woman of ill omen, for she is a creature of misfortune.

He also said, peace and blessings be upon him, that Allah Almighty says: "If I wish to grant My servant the best of this world and the Hereafter, I will give him a tongue that remembers Me, a humble heart, a body patient in affliction, and a believing wife who pleases him when he looks at her, guards herself and his wealth in his absence." He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Five things constitute

---
(61) Al-Ahzab: 21

<!-- page 123 -->

happiness: a good wife, righteous sons, good companions, a man's livelihood in his own land, and love for the family of Muhammad, peace be upon him and his family." He also said, peace be upon him and his family: "A good woman among women is like a blind crow, and blind crows are rare." He meant, peace be upon him and his family, that "good" here refers to the chaste, beautiful, believing woman who fulfills all her qualities. He likened such a woman to the albino crow, which is one of the rarest of birds.

He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "There is no true wealth in gold, silver, or the possessions of this world, but rather in the one whose separation brings no harm, nor does the dust — yet the soil is better than it." And he said, peace be upon him and his family: "The world is but property, and the best of the world's possessions is a good wife." He also said: "The happiness of the believer lies in a righteous wife, a spacious dwelling, a blessed companion, and a righteous son." He forbade, peace and blessings be upon him, marrying a woman for her wealth or her beauty alone, saying that her wealth may dominate him and her beauty may corrupt him. He said, peace and blessings be upon him and his family: "Marry for religion." He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Among horses, al-dahm" — meaning the darkest — "and no woman compares to the daughter of her age." He said, peace be upon him and his family: "The best of your women are the women of Quraysh — they are most devoted to their husbands and most caring toward their children."

He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Marry the one with healthy complexion, for there is good in that. And when one of you wishes to marry a woman, let him inquire about her hair as he inquires about her face, for hair is one of the two beauties. And shorter stature is better suited to what you desire." He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "A woman is like a necklace — let each of you consider carefully whom he adorns himself with." He said: "If misfortune lies in anything, it lies in a woman, a house, or a mount."

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "There are four types of women: a mosque, a square well, a suppressed war, and a lice-shackle." The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, explained that the lice-shackle was a device used in the pre-Islamic era to punish captives — they would take a piece of camel skin while it was soft and moist, wrap it around the captive's limbs, and as it dried it would tighten, causing the skin to contract. Lice would breed within it and could not escape due to the constriction, causing the captive great suffering without inflicting a direct blow — a form of torment the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, used as a metaphor.

<!-- page 124 -->

He used this as an analogy for a woman of bad character who makes things difficult for her husband, while a mosque is a woman who brings together both her goodness and her purity. A square well is a woman who has borne children and has more in her womb. A suppressed war refers to a woman of bad manners with her husband.

He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Do not marry a foolish woman, for her companionship is without benefit and her child is lost." He said: "The best women of my community are those who are fairest of face and lowest in dowry." He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Among the blessings of a woman is that her first child is male." He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Women are four: a square well, a mosque, a suppressed war, and a barren woman." He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Marry the dark-complexioned woman who is fertile, and do not marry a beautiful barren woman, for I will show you pride before the nations on the Day of Resurrection through your many children. The children who die young are beneath the throne of the Most Merciful, asking forgiveness for their fathers. Ibrahim, peace be upon him, gathers them and raises them on a mountain of musk, amber, and saffron."

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "The best of your women is the chaste one who is devoted to her lawful husband and guards herself from others." He said, peace be upon him and his family: "Marry the firstborn, for they are sweetest of mouth, purest of soul, quickest of learning, and most stable in their affection." He said: "Marry mothers" — meaning Allah Almighty will improve their character, expand their provision, and increase their blessing.

His command here is to marry the ayyami — those who previously had husbands but whose husbands died or divorced them — out of compassion, lest they suffer, though this command is one of encouragement rather than obligation. Allah Almighty says: "And marry those among you who are unmarried." (62) Whoever marries as Allah and His Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him, have enjoined and instructed, and marries a firstborn, there is no harm in that. We have mentioned the virtue of beginning early according to what is narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him. However, the ayyami should not be neglected out of mere desire or preference for those who have not previously been married, for it was generally the case that the wives of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, had been previously married. And Allah Almighty says: ﴾Indeed in the Messenger of Allah you have a good example for those who hope for Allah and the Last Day.﴿ (63)

With the first chain of transmission from the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him. (21)

**Chapter: On the Virtue of the Dowry**

---
(62) Al-Nur: 32
(63) Al-Ahzab: 21

<!-- page 125 -->

A Muslim should respond to his Muslim brother if he proposes marriage to him, provided he is satisfied with his religion. He warned that if this is not done, there will be strife in the land and great corruption. He forbade, peace and blessings be upon him, a marriage intended to alter what Allah Almighty has decreed for chastity and forbade marriage undertaken for ostentation and reputation. He said, peace and blessings be upon him: "Whoever marries a woman for her status or her wealth, Allah will leave him to his own devices in that. But whoever marries her for her religion and her virtue, Allah Almighty will provide him with beauty and wealth." He then recited the words of Allah Almighty: "If they are poor, Allah will enrich them from His bounty, and Allah is All-Encompassing, All-Knowing." (64)

He said: "There is no one more blameworthy before Allah than a man who is asked by his Muslim kinsman for his daughter's hand and refuses, saying he is not wealthy enough for him."

Ibn al-Husayn, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, is reported to have passed by a woman in one of the districts of Makkah and admired her character. He inquired about her and found her to be without a husband, so he proposed to her and married her. Then a man from his family came to him and said: "O son of the Messenger of Allah, there has been something in my heart regarding your marriage to this woman that I could not bear with patience, for you married her without inquiring about her lineage. I could not restrain myself until I investigated, and I found that she is one of the most noble of her people, so I became satisfied with that and content in myself." Ali ibn al-Husayn, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, replied: "I did not fear that," meaning he had not been concerned about her lineage. The man said: "I feared that she might not be your equal." Ali ibn al-Husayn, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said to him: "I thought you were wiser in your opinion than this. Do you not know that Allah Almighty elevated Islam and raised the lowly through it and completed what was deficient? The most honored of people is not to be blamed for the Muslim matter — blame belongs to ignorance."

He, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, also married the mother of a son of a man who had died, and when Abd al-Malik ibn Marwan learned of this, he wrote to him reproaching him for it, suggesting he had dishonored himself, saying: "Is there not enough among the noble women of Quraysh and the Arab lineages to have kept you from marrying the mother of another man's son?" Ali ibn al-Husayn, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, wrote back to him: "As for what follows — Allah Almighty has raised the lowly through Islam and completed the deficient through it. There is no blame

---
(64) Al-Nur: 32

<!-- page 126 -->

upon the noble Muslims — blame belongs only to ignorance. And the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, married" — meaning Safiyyah bint Hayy ibn Akhtab — "and there is in him a good example." Even if it were permissible to be overly concerned about the mothers of children, the resulting disorder and the corruption that would appear in the land would argue against it. The Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them, are more deserving of demonstrating this so that people will not despair of them.

Allah Almighty said: "O mankind, indeed We have created you from male and female and made you peoples and tribes so that you may know one another. Indeed, the most honored of you in the sight of Allah is the most righteous of you." (65)

With the first chain of transmission from the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him — "Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware."

He addressed the people on the day of the conquest of Makkah, praised and glorified Allah, and then said: "O people, Allah Almighty has removed from you the arrogance of ignorance and its pride in ancestry, for you are all from Adam and Adam was from clay. The best of Allah's servants before Allah are those most fearing of Him. Arab lineage is not a closed door — rather it is fear of Allah expressed by the tongue. Whoever falls short in his deeds, his lineage does not advance him." All blood feuds and claims of the pre-Islamic era are placed beneath his feet until the Day of Resurrection.

It is narrated that he gave his daughter — his niece, the daughter of al-Zubayr ibn Abd al-Muttalib — in marriage to al-Miqdad ibn al-Aswad, so that marriage would be humbled and that people would know that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, honors through Allah-consciousness, not through lineage. Al-Zubayr was the brother of Abd Allah, the father of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, through both his father and mother. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: "Among the marriages of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, was the marriage of Dubaa bint al-Zubayr ibn Abd al-Muttalib — one of the Qurayshi women — to al-Miqdad ibn al-Aswad, a freed client, and the marriage of Tamim al-Dari to a woman from Banu Hashim ibn Abd Manaf."

The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "When one whose religion and trustworthiness are pleasing to you comes seeking marriage, then marry him — for if you do not, there will be strife in the earth and great corruption."

It is narrated from Abu Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, that he was in Makkah when it was mentioned to him that Dawud ibn Ali had ordered the killing of al-Ma'ali ibn Hunaish, who was one of his companions, and he was killed. Al-Sadiq, peace be upon him, stood in anger, pulling his garment, and his son Isma'il, peace be upon him, came to meet him and said: "O my father, where are you going? The people are defiant and turn away from Allah Almighty — leave them." So he struck him in the chest and said: "Step aside from me." He then went on until he entered upon Dawud and said: "O Dawud, you have committed a sin that Allah will never forgive you."

---
(65) Al-Hujurat: 13


<!-- page 127 -->
He said: "I killed a believing man from among the people of Paradise." David became angry and was silent for a time, then said: "And you too have committed a sin that Allah Almighty will not forgive you." He said: "And what is that?" He said: "Your daughter has married a man from Banu Umayyah." Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, cited in that regard that the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, had married his daughter Zaynab to Abu al-'As ibn Rabi'ah, and that 'Uthman ibn 'Affan married Umm Kulthum, and when she died he married Ruqayyah, his daughters, peace be upon him and his family.

"You are the one who forbade killing believers unjustly." David said: "I did not command him to be killed." He said: Al-Sarraqi said: "We have lost him," and he said: "I have taken responsibility for him." So Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, had his companions take hold of him and proceed with him to be killed. He said: "O servants of Allah, they command me to kill people and then bind me." Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "As for you, O enemy of Allah, you are condemned to be killed, and your companion has denied that they commanded you." He said: "Let whoever will bear witness against Dawud — retribution is obligatory upon him and it is obligatory to be exacted."

It was said to Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him: Is it permissible to give daughters in marriage to those of opposing views? He said that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, gave his daughters in marriage to certain men, and that 'Umar sought the hand of 'Ali's daughter Umm Kulthum, may Allah's prayers be upon him. Al-'Abbas came to him complaining of this, and the Banu 'Abd al-Muttalib urged him to refuse, so Al-'Abbas came to him, may Allah's prayers be upon him, and said: "O son of my brother, you see the situation we are in — 'Umar has sought her hand and we have urged you to refuse him." He then committed her affair to him, and he gave her in marriage to Al-'Abbas on his behalf. It is therefore better to give daughters in marriage to people of sound faith who do not stir up enmity against the family of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family. The marriage of a believer is better than the marriage of others, and it is permissible when necessary to give daughters in marriage to people of differing views from among the Muslims. The same applies to marrying them; it is not forbidden as is the case with marrying polytheists, but the virtuous choice is marriage among people of sound faith.

Then the man of Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, asked him about a sister of his wife, a believing woman of knowledge who was in Basra and had no male guardian to oversee her affairs. He said to him: "Do not give her in marriage to them. It is permissible for you to give weak women in marriage, but not a Nasibiyyah daughter of a Nasib." Zurara ibn A'yan asked Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, about a similar matter.

<!-- page 128 -->
He was permitted to give weak women in marriage, but not a Nasibiyyah daughter of a Nasib. Abu Ja'far, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said to Zurara ibn A'yan regarding weak women: Abu Ja'far, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "Marry among those in whom there is doubt and do not avoid them, for women take from a man's character when he marries her and she prevails over his religion." He said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "The marriage of people of knowledge and marriage among them is first and better and closer to Allah Almighty. If such are not available, then the weak — those who are neither loyal nor hostile — are better than those of enmity. After them come the Muslims of the people of truce, who are bound together by the Shari'ah in matters of fighting, inheritance, and testimony: it is permissible for them to testify against one another if they are upright and not accused. As for the people of disbelief — such as Jews, Christians, and polytheists — it is not permissible for any of them to marry a Muslim woman, though their marrying one another is permissible, and Muslim men may marry their women when necessary. Muslim women, however, are not permissible for them."

Abu Abdullah, peace be upon him, was asked about the saying of Allah Almighty: *"The adulterer shall not marry except an adulteress or a polytheist, and the adulteress shall not marry except an adulterer or a polytheist, and that is forbidden to the believers."* (66) He said: "There were women known for licentiousness in the pre-Islamic era who were openly hired out for adultery; they had established houses of ill repute for that purpose. The truce came with the provision that those who wished could return to them — among them Habibah, al-Rabab, and Sarah — whose blood was made permissible by the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, on the day of the conquest of Mecca, because she had been inciting the polytheists to fight the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family. As for a slave girl who has committed fornication, there is nothing wrong with purchasing her and having relations with her, and it is permissible to marry a woman when necessary and to guard and fortify oneself against sin; chaste women are better and preferable."

A man came to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, and said: "O Messenger of Allah, I have a woman who does not repel the hand of one who touches her, and I love her." He said: "Then keep her." It was narrated from a man who saw his wife committing adultery yet was averse to parting with her, and it was said: he may keep her if he wishes. Peace be upon him was asked about a man marrying an immoral woman, and he said: "What prevents him? But if he does so, let him fortify his household." He was asked about a man who marries a slave girl born of adultery, and he said: "There is nothing wrong with it; but keeping away from that is dearer to me." And concerning a man who has a slave girl who has committed fornication,

(66) Al-Nur: 3

<!-- page 129 -->
he said: "Yes, if he wishes." Regarding a man who marries a woman without full discernment, he said: "It is permissible if no fault remains attached to his son." As for a child of adultery — may one have relations with her and sell her if she is a slave girl, and buy her? Yes, and the forbidden does not nullify the permissible nor corrupt it. Even if one wishes for such people, forbidding their marriage leads to much corruption in the land, and Allah may reform the corrupters or accept the repentance of the sinners.

Some of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them, were asked about the saying of Allah Almighty: *"The adulterer shall not marry except an adulteress or a polytheist."* He said: "The word 'marry' here may refer to illicit union and intercourse, or it may refer to the contract and marriage." He said: "If it means illicit union and intercourse, then it may indeed mean adultery, not lawful marriage." He said: "Except an adulterer or a polytheist — one does not incur the wrath of Allah except when he is a polytheist." The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said: "The adulterer does not commit adultery at the moment of committing adultery while he is a believer."

**Mentioning the Seeking of Women's Hands in Marriage:**

We have narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, concerning him and his family: the Imams of his household forbade a man to make a proposal of marriage while his brother has already proposed, and to marry a woman over his brother's wife. Some of the Ahl al-Bayt, peace be upon them, explained this and said: The meaning of the words of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, is that a man should not propose to a woman after his brother has proposed and there has been mutual approach, acceptance, inclination, attentiveness, and agreement, such that nothing remains but the contract itself. As for what falls short of that stage, it is permissible for two men or a group to propose to a woman until she listens to one of them and responds to him. It is likewise permissible for a man to bid on a commodity and purchase it.

The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, commanded the practice of open auction, and people have continued to this day to outbid one another in the call, each surpassing the other until the commodity is settled upon and the seller accepts a buyer's offer — so no further bid should be made after that. There is no prohibition on addition to what has been commanded, which is a matter left open on account of its benefit. The suitor and the one who raises a bid on a commodity after agreement and before the marriage contract or sale is concluded occupy the same ruling: if someone surpasses another in merit before the contract is concluded, that does not invalidate the marriage or the sale, because no acceptance by contract has been given to another. The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said: "If one of you wishes to marry a woman, let him look at her, for he is like a buyer."

<!-- page 130 -->
"If one of you wishes to marry a woman, there is nothing wrong with looking at what draws him to her."

Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, mentioned this hadith of Jabir ibn 'Abdullah al-Ansari, and said to him: "Did you hear it from the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him?" He replied: "I sought the hand of a woman from among the women of the Ansar, and I hid myself by a wall of her father's house and looked at her to see what I wished to see, until I was satisfied and I liked what I saw, so I married her, and she was the best of women."

Ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: It is permissible for one who wishes to marry a woman to look at her, but he should not look at a slave girl except with the permission of her master — meaning that the master may not wish to sell his slave girl or offer her, but the command of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, to permit the gaze at a woman one wishes to marry extends the permissibility of that gaze even after he has come to know that they are willing to marry him and has not yet formally agreed, and there is nothing left but the marriage contract. He should look at her only to know her situation, not out of desire to look at her or to take pleasure in her, and not looking deliberately at her private parts or at what is not permissible to look at. It is not permissible for him to look at women who are not his women — that is, women who are not his wives or those prohibited to him by blood ties, marriage ties, or those in his lawful possession — with the eye of contemplation. It is not permissible to be impassioned or to gaze openly.

Allah Almighty says: *"Say to the believing men that they should lower their gaze."* (67) Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, was asked about a man past whom a woman walks and he looks at her. He said: "It is easier for a man to look at his own family and female relatives — be satisfied with yourselves." Peace be upon him said regarding the words of Allah Almighty in the story of Moses, concerning the woman who said: *"Indeed, the best one you can hire is the strong and the trustworthy."* (68) He said: "She meant Moses, peace be upon him and his family." He said to her — meaning to her father — "As for her statement about his trustworthiness, I saw the one who lifted the rock from the well. And as for his trustworthiness, when he went with me to you, I wished to walk ahead of him to guide him on the road, but he approached me and said: Walk behind me and guide me on the road, for we are a people who do not look at the backs of women.'" It was narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: "I heard the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, and he mentioned looking at women, and he said:"

(67) Al-Nur: 30
(68) Al-Qasas: 26

<!-- page 131 -->
"The first glance is for you — meaning the sudden and unintentional glance. The second glance is against you. The third glance is a poisoned arrow from the arrows of Satan. Whoever leaves them for Allah Almighty and for no other, Allah Almighty will grant him in return a faith whose sweetness he will find." Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "Does a believer not fear that he looks at the forms of women and is afflicted thereby in relation to his own wives?"

It is narrated from 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that a man came to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, and said: "O Messenger of Allah, my mother seeks permission to enter — should I grant her permission?" He said: "Yes." He said: "Why, O Messenger of Allah?" — peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family — He said: He said: "No." He said: "So grant her permission then." He said: "O Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, my sister has uncovered her hair in my presence." He said: "No." He said: "Why?" He said: "I fear that if she displays one of her charms before you, Satan will invite you to it." He said, peace be upon him and his family: "Before one of you, let a woman who is a mahram — whether a mother, sister, aunt, or any woman forbidden to him by marriage — kiss her on the head and between the eyes, or he should keep to her cheeks." Regarding Fatimah, may Allah's prayers be upon her, a blind man sought her permission to enter, so she veiled herself from him. He was told: "He is blind and cannot see anything." She said: "Even if he cannot see me, I can feel his presence and he can sense my voice and perceive my scent."

Peace be upon him and his family — may Allah's prayers be upon him — and a group with him were reflecting on what is good for women, and they did not know what to say about it. He went, with Allah's prayers upon him, to Fatimah, may Allah's prayers be upon her, and mentioned this to her. She said: "What is best for women is that they should not see men and men should not see them." He went, with Allah's prayers upon him, to the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, and said to him: "Did you arrive at this yourself or did someone else say it?" He said: "O Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, it was Fatimah who said it." The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, was impressed by it and said: "Fatimah is a part of me."

The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said: "Whoever wishes to marry, let him praise Allah and extol Him and say: O Allah, grant me a wife who is affectionate and fruitful, grateful and jealous in her honor — if I do good, she thanks me; if I do wrong, she forgives me; if I remember Allah, she helps me; if I forget, she reminds me; if I go out from her, she guards herself; if I come in to her, she gladdens me; if I command her, she obeys me; if I swear by her, she fulfills my oath; if she becomes angry, you satisfy me — O Lord of majesty and honor, grant me what I have asked for, and what You have apportioned and given." The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, said:


<!-- page 132 -->

Volume Two
A Book Brief on the Effects

Whoever says that and does so, Allah Almighty will give him what he asked. And from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him), he said: Every marriage in which there is no engagement is like leprosy. And Ja'far ibn Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon them), when he married or someone was married in his presence, would say: "Praise be to Allah; we praise Him and seek refuge with Him from the evils of ourselves and the evils of our deeds. Whoever Allah guides, none can mislead; and whoever He misleads, none can guide. I bear witness that there is no god but Allah alone, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His slave and messenger." He then said:

*O people, fear your Lord, who created you from a single soul and created from it its spouse, and from them spread many men and women. And fear Allah, by whom you ask one another, and the wombs, for Allah has been a watcher over you.* (al-Nisa: 1)

And the Almighty said:

*O you who believe, fear Allah as He deserves to be feared, and do not die except as Muslims.* (Al-Imran: 102)

*O you who believe, fear Allah and speak a righteous word. He will make your deeds sound for you and forgive you your sins.* (Al-Ahzab: 70–71)

Then the marriage is concluded and the parties are satisfied. Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger has indeed won a great victory.

Peace be upon the one who contracted the marriage contract, praising Allah for what He deserves, and praying upon the Prophet (peace be upon him and his family), and mentioning the virtue of marriage and what is desired of it.
