The husband should make known to his companion what is available to him, and then the contract is concluded. If the marriage contract is not preceded by an engagement, it is still permissible. The khutbah is preferred, and the scholars forbade a formal proposal during the 'iddah yet permitted hinting at marriage, in accordance with the saying of Allah Almighty:

*There is no blame upon you for what you hint at regarding a proposal to women, or what you conceal within yourselves. Allah knows that you will remember them, but do not make a secret commitment with them except in the form of honorable speech. And do not resolve the marriage contract until the prescribed term reaches its end.* (al-Baqarah: 235)

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said regarding the words of Allah Almighty, "Do not date them in secret": this means marriage, and exposing them to the proposal and speaking goodly words — that is hinting at the khutbah.

And from Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon them) regarding Sakina bint Handhalah: she had been separated from her cousin who had married her,

<!-- page 133 -->

and he died, after which Abu Ja'far (peace be upon him) greeted her and said, "How have you been, O daughter of Handhalah?" She said, "May I be your ransom, O son of the Messenger of Allah." He said, "You know my kinship to the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him), and my standing with Ali (peace be upon him), and my right in Islam, and my honor among the Arabs." She said, "May Allah forgive you, O Abu Ja'far — you have proposed to me while I am in my 'iddah!" He said, "What I have done is inform you of my status and standing, and that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) entered upon Umm Salamah, the daughter of Umayyah ibn al-Mughirah al-Makhzumiyyah, while she was in 'iddah from Abu Salamah who was her cousin. He continued to mention his standing to her until the impression of the mat was visible on his palm from the intensity of how he leaned upon his hand — and yet it was not a formal proposal." They said (peace be upon them): it is recommended for one who wishes to marry to inform his prospective companion of his situation so that she may know his circumstances.

When the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) proposed to her, Umm Salamah (may Allah be pleased with her) sent to him saying: "O Messenger of Allah, I am an elderly woman with children, and I am a very jealous woman, and I fear there may be something in me that you are unaware of." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) sent back to her saying: "As for your age, you have mentioned it — and I am older than you. As for your children, they shall be among the children of the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him). And as for your jealousy, I will pray to Allah Almighty to remove it from you." And he married her (peace be upon him and his family).

**Mentioning the Wedding Feast and the Consummation of Marriage:**

We have narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah (may the prayers of Allah be upon him and his family and upon them all) that he said: the feast is divided into four kinds — the walimah, the mut'ah, the 'adhirah, and the wakirah. The walimah is the food prepared when a man enters upon his wife; the mut'ah is the food prepared for the 'aqiqah on behalf of the newborn on the seventh day — this has been mentioned previously and it is obligatory in it; the 'adhirah is the food prepared when circumcising a boy; and the wakirah is the food prepared when a man returns from travel, and they invite people to it. He said (peace be upon him and his family): it is among the Sunnah of the Messengers to prepare food upon marriage. And he (peace be upon him and his family) held a wedding feast for his wives, and he fed them at some of his feasts — which is food prepared from dates, ghee, and condensed milk. He also said (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) to one of his companions when he had married: "You must give a feast, even if it be only a sheep's leg." He said: "The feast of the first day is truth, the feast of the

<!-- page 134 -->

second day is customary and known, and what is beyond that is ostentation and reputation-seeking." Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may the prayers of Allah be upon them) was invited to a feast on the first day and he attended; then he was invited on the second day and he attended; then a messenger came to him on the third day to invite him, and he ordered the messenger to be turned away and hid from him.

He passed by Banu Zurayq and heard singing, and said: "What is this?" They said: "O Messenger of Allah, so-and-so has married." He said: "He has completed his religion — this marriage is not a clandestine affair nor a secret marriage." He meant that it is known by its smoke being seen and the sound of the tambourine being heard. He said (peace be upon him and his family): "The distinction between marriage and fornication is the beating of the tambourine." It was narrated from him (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that on the night Fatimah was brought to Ali (peace be upon him), the tambourine was beaten. He said: "What is this?" Umm Salamah said: "O Messenger of Allah, this is Asma' bint 'Umays beating the tambourine for Fatimah, because she says within herself: 'My mother has died and there is no one to rejoice with me.'" The Messenger of Allah raised his hands to the sky and said: "O Allah, grant Asma' bint 'Umays the pleasure with which the daughter of Your Prophet rejoiced," and he prayed for her. Then he said, "O Asma', what do you say when you beat the tambourine?" She said, "O Messenger of Allah, I swear I do not know what to say — I only wished to make her happy." He said: "Do not say what is vain, but say: 'We have come to you, we have come to you, and we greet you.'" Such words and their like, and the beating of the tambourine — those matters for which permission has been given at weddings and banquets — are permissible. As for other words that involve ridicule, vulgarity, and the singing that accompanies them, and entertainment and flutes — these are disliked by the people of Allah.

Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon them) was asked about such matters, and he recited the words of Allah Almighty: *We have not created the heavens and the earth and whatever is between them in play. Had We wished to take a pastime, We would have taken it from near Us — if We were going to do so. Rather, We hurl the truth against falsehood, and it destroys it.* (al-Anbiya': 16–18)

And it was narrated from him that he said: "Singing is a gathering that brings about the wrath of Allah, and it fulfills His promise of punishment."

Allah Almighty looks away from those who engage in it. It was also narrated from him that he was asked about the flute, and he said: "The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: there are two voices that Allah Almighty hates — wailing over the face of calamity, and the flute at the time of blessing." And it was narrated from him that he said, regarding the words of Allah Almighty: *So avoid it* —

<!-- page 135 -->

He said: "Abomination is one of the idols." (al-Hajj: 30) *And avoid the false statement* — He said: chess. And from the false statement is singing. He said regarding the words of Allah Almighty: *And from among people is one who purchases idle speech* (Luqman: 6) — He said: that refers to singing.

One of his companions said: "I was in Madinah and came upon a place where there was singing, so I sat listening. The next day I entered upon Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon them) and sat before him, and he began staring at me and then recited the words of Allah Almighty: *Indeed, the hearing, the sight, and the heart — about all of these one will be questioned.* (al-Isra': 36) Then he looked at me and said, 'Ask your hearing what it heard, and ask your sight what it saw, and ask the heart what it perceived.'"

It was narrated from him that he said: it is not permissible to earn wealth from singing or to purchase it, and listening to it is hypocrisy, and teaching it is disbelief. It was also narrated from him that one day some of his Shi'a entered upon him as a delegation from various towns. He said to them: "Where did you lodge?" They said: "O son of the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family), we lodged in a house, and when we neared the place we heard singing within it, so we asked for another lodging but could not find one." He said: "Even so, be noble — safeguard your hearing from it and your listening to it." For Allah the Almighty says: *And those who do not bear witness to falsehood, and when they pass by idle talk, they pass by with dignity.* (al-Furqan: 72)

And it was narrated from him that he said: "My father (peace be upon him) once, when I was a young boy, stood me one day near two musicians to hear from them. He took my hand and said: 'Step away; do not be one of those whom Adam (peace be upon him) was made to suffer on account of.'" He said: "All that you see of amusement, play, and singing was made by Satan to gloat over Adam (peace be upon him) when he was expelled from Paradise." He said (peace be upon him): "The house in which singing takes place is a house in which mourning is not answered, the supplication is not accepted, and the angels do not enter it." It was narrated from him that he said: "Singing causes hypocrisy to grow in the heart as pollen grows on the date palm." And it was narrated from him that he said: "Whoever has a tambourine beaten in his house for forty days, Allah sends upon him a devil called al-Raqqas, who does not leave him. If he is thus afflicted with the loss of his modesty, he no longer cares what he says or what is said to him."

<!-- page 136 -->

It was said of him (peace be upon him) that a man from his Shi'a entered upon one who was known to him, and that man brought him a slave-girl who played and sang, and she sang and played in his presence. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon them) said to him: "Do you not fear that the command of Allah Almighty will come upon you while you are in this state — in a gathering that He does not look upon with mercy?" It was also narrated from him that a man asked him about singing, and he said to him: "Do you see that Allah Almighty distinguishes between truth and falsehood? Where then do you place singing?" The man said: "O son of the Prophet of Allah, I see it only with falsehood." He said: "Then you have judged it yourself — there is no good in it, and it is among that for which Allah Almighty has promised the Fire." Then he recited the saying of Allah Almighty: *And from among people is one who purchases idle speech* (Luqman: 6). It was also narrated from him that singing inherits hypocrisy and brings about poverty.

And peace be upon him, he said that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) forbade his nation from al-zaffin, the flute, the cups, and the reed flutes. And it was reported that a man who had broken a tambourine was brought before Ali (peace be upon him), and he had done so deliberately, so Ali ruled that the one who broke it was absolved of liability for breaking it. All of this indicates the prohibition of entertainment, listening to singing, psalms, the canopy, and the like when these are used as distractions. However, what is used in war — when mobilizing soldiers and the like, such as the celebrations of the people in the presence of the Imams and the commanders of armies, with the beating of drums and shields and the like, and the blowing of horns and whistles — this is similar to the Psalms of David in that it is not used for idle enjoyment and distraction. This is therefore not what was forbidden, nor does it fall under what was prohibited in the foregoing discussion. Rather, it is among what is recommended in its proper place.

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) passed by a group of Ethiopians beating drums. He said to them: "Beat on, O sons of Arfidah, so that the Jews may know that there is breadth in our religion." We see in the lands where the Imams hold authority (may the prayers of Allah be upon them) places of entertainment that they have forbidden. It was said to them: "This is less serious than the immorality that Allah Almighty has forbidden, yet people commit it openly, in front of their doors and under their windows without restraint." The response is that Allah has established clear limits regarding the immorality He has commanded be subject to prescribed penalties — limits based on what is openly apparent and witnessed publicly, and united with the knowledge of what is hidden from it, and so punishment was enacted upon it.


<!-- page 137 -->

Pardoning her is what the Almighty intends to offer, and what had been delayed has now become clear. All people, and the Imams, peace be upon them, are well known for denouncing such acts and withdrawing any license from those who commit them. They hold that this is not a matter requiring the hadd penalty, but rather one requiring denunciation and prohibition.

The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said: "Marry your brides at night and feed them during the day." It was also narrated from him, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, that he said: "There is no wakefulness save in three: tahajjud with the Quran, seeking knowledge, or a bride being brought to her husband." It was further narrated from him, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, that a man should prepare for his wife as he would wish her to prepare for him.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon them, said: When a man's wife is brought to him and she enters upon him, he should pray two rak'ahs, then place his hand upon her forehead and say: "O Allah, bless my family and bless her in what You have joined between us; bring us together in goodness, felicity, and blessing, and if You decree a separation, let it be a separation toward all good." He should then sit beside her, place his hand upon her forehead, and say: "Praise be to Allah who guided my misguidance, enriched my poverty, lightened my burden, honored my humiliation, sheltered my family, ended my estrangement, carried my burden, and served my purpose. Praise be to Allah for what He has granted me. O Allah, for what I have pledged and what I have honored."

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali, peace be upon them, said: A woman is likewise commanded to perform ablution on her wedding night; when her husband prays, she prays. He increased in supplication, invoking prayers upon the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, at the beginning, middle, and end. He said in his supplication: "O Allah, grant me her affection, her warmth, and her contentment with me, and my contentment with her; and bring us together in the best and most harmonious union, for You love the lawful and hate the unlawful."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon them, said: When a man wishes to have intercourse with his wife, let him say: "O Allah, You have decreed from me today a creation, so make it pure for You, and do not give the devil a share or a portion in it, for You are able to do all things." From the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, it was said: "When one of you comes to his wife, let him not hasten her, and if he does not hasten her, let him believe in her." Peace be upon him and his family. It was permitted for a man to have intercourse with a free woman while another wife is present in the home. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: There is no objection to that regarding the ummah. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, and Ali, peace be upon him, said that a man should not have intercourse with his wife

<!-- page 138 -->

facing the qibla. It was narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: Looking during intercourse causes blindness. He, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, and the Imams from his progeny, would look away from any animal mounted by a stallion on the road, and he would turn his face away, peace be upon him.

A man should have intercourse with a woman, and if he feels that the water is about to come forth, he should draw back to release it elsewhere, and he should not do otherwise. The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, forbade withdrawal from a free woman except with her permission, and from a slave woman except with the permission of her master. Ali, peace be upon him, said: He may withdraw from his slave woman as he wishes.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: A man should not sleep between two free women, and he should not look at the 'awrah of a free woman nor expose his own 'awrah to her. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali, peace be upon him, said that intercourse is disliked at the time of dawn until sunrise, from the disappearance of the sun until the disappearance of twilight, and at night when the moon is eclipsed, and on the day when the sun is eclipsed, and on days and nights in which earthquakes occur, and when yellow, black, or red winds blow.

He said, peace be upon him: The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, went to one of his wives on a night in which the moon was eclipsed. It was said: "O Messenger of Allah, what is this?" And he said: "What is this? I only thought that I am the best this night, and so I would be among those with us." Allah Almighty, in what He wrote, said: "And if they see a falling fragment from the sky, they say: a massing cloud." Then Abu Ja'far said, may the prayers of Allah be upon him: By He who sent Muhammad with prophethood and singled him out for the message and honored him with dignity — none of you should have intercourse at any of these times he has deemed disliked, for intercourse at such times produces offspring in whom one sees the apple of his eye.

It was narrated from Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: He should not have intercourse with a slave girl under nine years of age; if he does and she is harmed, he is liable. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him and his family, said: If a man marries a young slave girl, he should not enter upon her until she reaches the age of maturity, nine years from the day she was born.

It was narrated from Ali, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that he disliked women coming to him in a particular manner. He used to forbid that and cite the words of Allah Almighty: "I will grant you the pleasure of the qibla." It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, that he said: A man should not be alone with a woman who is not his mahram, for the devil is the third of them. He forbade speaking with women other than

<!-- page 139 -->

their husbands and the women who are their mahrams. Ja'far ibn Muhammad said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: Excessive conversation with women brings forgetfulness and distraction of the heart, which are among the traps of the devil.

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, said: "Fear Allah regarding women, for they are vulnerable, and you have made them lawful to you by the faithfulness of Allah." He said: They have vulnerability; protect it by keeping them silent, not exposing their vulnerability in homes, not confining them to rooms, not teaching them to write, but teaching them Surat al-Nur. From him, peace be upon him and his family, he said: "Indeed, the best occupation for a believing woman is the spindle." From him, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, it was narrated that women taking the pledge of allegiance should not be related to men except their husbands and their mahrams.

It was narrated from him, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that he forbade women from looking at men, and that it is forbidden for a woman to leave her house without her husband's permission, even for a bath unless there is necessity. It was narrated from him, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that he said one day to his companions: "The woman is 'awrah." "O Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family," they said, "then what?" He said: "Yes." When she draws near to her Lord, she receives no response to that. Ali mentioned this to Fatimah, may Allah's prayers be upon her, and she said: "Go back to him and tell him: O Messenger of Allah, the closest a woman can be to her Lord is when she is at the lowest point of her home." He departed and informed the Prophet, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, who said: "This is from you, O Ali." He said: "No, O Messenger of Allah, rather it was Fatimah who said it." The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, said: "Indeed it is a part of me."

From the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family: He forbade a woman from walking naked before her husband, and forbade the man from being naked before her. He forbade women from combing their hair with wine or drinking alcohol, and forbade them from imitating men, just as he forbade men from imitating women, and he cursed those who did that.

He said, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: "Whoever is virtuous among women should not leave her house, and should not attend the prayer with us." From him, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him: He forbade women, when they go out, from walking in the middle of the road with men, and he forbade them from wearing a garment of fame when going out, or from having a voice that carries, or from striking her leg so that the sound of her anklet is heard. He forbade women from wearing adornments, wailing, and displaying their voices except out of necessity, and from speaking with men, and from staying overnight away from their homes.

<!-- page 140 -->

Peace be upon him forbade extending greetings to women, and the prayers of Allah be upon him — it was forbidden for a man to be alone with a woman except with a garment between them, and a woman with a woman except with a garment between them.

It was narrated from him, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, that a woman came to him and said: "O Messenger of Allah, I have a husband who has thick hair, and I have done something to it." The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said to her: "O you who have disgraced your religion — upon you is the curse of the angels of heaven and the angels of earth." From him, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, he said: "If I had commanded anyone to prostrate to another, I would have commanded a woman to prostrate to her husband." He forbade a woman from refusing her husband when he called her to bed. He said: "The best adornment a woman can wear is piety toward Allah and obedience to her husband."

Peace be upon him and his family — he said: If a woman knows her Lord and believes in Him and in His Messenger, and recognizes the virtue of the people of her Prophet's household, and performs the prayer, and fasts the month of Ramadan, and guards her private parts, and obeys her husband, she may enter through any gate of Paradise she wishes. From him, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him: A woman sent to him and said: "O Messenger of Allah, my husband has ordered me not to go out to any near or distant place until he returns from his travels, and my father is in the market — I did not go out to him." The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said to her: "Sit in your house and obey your husband." She did so, and her father died. The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, sent to her saying: "Indeed, Allah Almighty has forgiven your father on account of your obedience to your husband."

May Allah's peace and blessings be upon him — a woman asked him and said: "O Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, what is the right of a husband over his wife?" He said: "She must not leave his house except with his permission, must not withhold herself from him even on the back of a saddle, must not fast a voluntary day except with his permission, and must not leave her house except with his permission. If she goes out without his permission, the angels of heaven, the angels of earth, the angels of contentment, and the angels of wrath all curse her until she returns." She said: "O Messenger of Allah, who truly has the greatest right over men?" He said: "Their parents." "And who has the greatest right over women?" She said: "Her husband." She said: "O Messenger of Allah, does he have no right comparable to that?" He said: "Not even one in a hundred."

It was narrated from him, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, that he assigned to his daughter Fatimah, may Allah's prayers be upon her, the service of what was inside the house, and he assigned to Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, the service of what was outside it. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, was asked about the rights of women


<!-- page 141 -->
On her husband: if he did good, he would satisfy her and clothe her, and if she wronged him, he pardoned her.

It is related that there was a woman with Abu Salawat, may Allah be pleased with him, and he used to forgive her and say: The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said, "A woman is like a rib — if you leave it as it is, you benefit from it, but if you try to straighten it, it breaks." When it was said to him that a woman is jealous of her husband, he replied, "That is love." He was also asked, peace be upon him, about a man who is presented with a woman who is wholly absorbed in worship, fasting, and prayer.

'Uthman ibn Maz'un came to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, and his wife said to him: O Messenger of Allah, 'Uthman fasts during the day and rises in prayer through the night. The Prophet, peace be upon him, understood what she meant, so he went out to the mosque, where he found 'Uthman ibn Maz'un standing in prayer, and he said: O 'Uthman, Allah Almighty did not send me toward monasticism — He sent me toward the upright natural way. Whoever loves my nature, let him not abandon my Sunnah; and among my Sunnahs is marriage. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, also forbade the beating of women, for it is not obligatory.

It is narrated from 'Ali, peace be upon him, that a man from the Ansar came to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, with his daughter, saying: O Messenger of Allah, her husband beat her and she fled from him. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, received her, and then Allah Almighty sent down: *Men are the protectors and maintainers of women, because Allah has made one of them to excel over the other, and because they spend from their wealth. So righteous women are devoutly obedient, guarding in the husband's absence what Allah would have them guard. As for those women from whom you fear disobedience, admonish them, then leave them in bed, then strike them. But if they obey you, seek no means against them. Surely Allah is Most High.* (79) The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, then said: I intended something, and Allah intended something else.

It is also narrated from him, peace be upon him, that he said: Jealousy that stems from faith is jealousy in what is forbidden, while jealousy that stems from hypocrisy is jealousy in what is permitted. This applies to both men and women in their respective situations. In another hadith from 'Ali, peace be upon him, he said: The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, forbade being humiliated by blame — meaning abandoning proper jealousy. Such humiliation consists in a man exposing his private matters, that is, revealing them, so that he ends up displaying his household to other men.

(79) Al-Nisa': 34

<!-- page 142 -->
As for jealousy in what is lawful, it is disliked and forbidden. He said, peace be upon him: There is no jealousy in what is lawful after the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family. He said: Do not say anything — I will come to you on the night of his entry upon Fatimah, may Allah's prayers be upon her, after she had become his wife. He said this to them after they had lain down, and he took hold of them. The Messenger of Allah entered, peace be upon him and his family, and prayed for them and blessed them, then he departed.

The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said: Jihad has been prescribed for the men of my nation, and jealousy for its women. Whoever among them is patient and takes account of her reward, Allah Almighty will grant her the reward of a martyr, and that is immense.

It is narrated from him, peace be upon him, that he mentioned women and what men say of them — that they adorn themselves with gold, wear silk, burden the wealthy, and exhaust the poor. It is also narrated from 'Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: Whoever obeys his wife in four things, Allah will cast him face-down into the Fire: obeying her in attending wedding celebrations, in lamentation, in mixing gatherings, and in going to the bathhouses.

**Mentioning the Marriage of the Saints**

We have narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family, that there is no marriage except with a guardian and two just witnesses. It is forbidden for a woman to marry until she is consulted. It is narrated from 'Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: None of you should marry his daughter off without first consulting her, for she knows best for herself — whether she keeps silent, weeps, or laughs, that is her permission.

If a father did not arrange a marriage for her, then the authority of fathers over their minor sons and daughters in marriage is permissible, and the children have no recourse when they come of age. If the husband's agent is authorized to arrange a marriage, it is permissible. It is also narrated, peace be upon him, that if a Muslim woman entrusts her Christian father to arrange her marriage when she is an adult, it is permissible for her husband. However, her husband when she was a child was not permissible, because he has no guardianship over her.

If a woman entrusts two guardians and delegates to both of them, and each one of them marries her to a different man, then the marriage belongs to the first of them, provided the first was not aware of the other's contract. If two contracts are concluded simultaneously, one of them is invalidated and the matter is resumed thereafter. From Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, peace be upon him, and from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him: They said that a paternal grandfather may contract the marriage of his son's minor daughter just as a father contracts it, and he has priority in this — even if they both contract simultaneously.

<!-- page 143 -->
All of them are bound by the first contract concluded among them, even if the one who dissolves the contract was unaware and later resumes. From Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, peace be upon him, it is related that he said: What is permissible as testimony in financial matters is also permissible as testimony in marriage, and it is permissible to accept the testimony of women and slaves in this regard.

From Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him: He was asked about two witnesses who testified in the marriage of a woman that her father had given her in marriage, while she disliked this, and another witness testified otherwise. He said: The marriage is permissible with her consent. He was also asked about two witnesses, one of whom testified that the dowry was one thousand and the other testified it was two thousand. He said: If she claims the higher amount, she takes an oath alongside her witness. If one of them testifies that her father gave her in marriage when she was a child, and the other testifies that he gave her in marriage when she was an adult without her consent, the testimony is invalid.

**Mentioning the Testimonies in Marriage**

I mentioned before this chapter that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said: There is no marriage except with a guardian and two witnesses. This is what is commanded and by which action is to be taken, and by which marriage is established. It is narrated from 'Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: It is not permissible to marry except with a guardian and witnesses.

Abu Ja'far and Abu 'Abdullah, peace be upon them, stipulated witnesses in marriage so that the child may know who his father is, and to guard against either spouse denying the marriage, and out of caution toward the authorities if the couple were to be accused of being unrelated. For these reasons and whatever resembles them, it is obligatory to have witnesses in marriage. As for the relationship between a person and Allah Almighty, there is nothing upon him if he does not seek witnesses, for Allah Almighty mentioned testimony only in the context of divorce and did not mention it explicitly in the context of marriage. Nevertheless, testimony in marriage is necessary because through it the marriage is established, lineage is confirmed, and suspicion is removed. Both women and slaves may serve as witnesses if they are upright.

<!-- page 144 -->
**Male Dowries**

We narrate concerning the Commander of the Believers, peace be upon him.

Allah Almighty said: *And give the women their dowries as a gift.* (80)

May Allah's prayers be upon him — he said concerning the words of Allah Almighty: *And give the women their dowries willingly.* (81) He said: Allah Almighty says, give them the gift by which you have made their private parts lawful for you. Whoever wrongs a woman in her dowry makes her private part lawful through fornication. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said: Allah Almighty forgives all sins except the sin of a man who deprives a hired man of his wage or wrongs a woman.

It is narrated from him, peace be upon him, that he said: The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, did not marry any of his wives, nor did any of his daughters' husbands give, more than twelve uqiyyahs and a nashsh of silver — an uqiyyah being forty dirhams, and a nashsh being half an uqiyyah, that is five hundred dirhams in total — along with a bed and a ram. This is the dowry of the Sunnah, and there is no fixed limit in terms of what is greater, for whatever is named and for which the marriage contract is made, whether little or much, is valid. It is recommended to make it easy. He said, peace be upon him: Among the blessings of a woman is the ease of her marriage and the ease of her womb, so do not make dowries excessive, for that will breed enmity.

He also said: A man came to the Prophet, peace be upon him, and said: O Messenger of Allah, I wish to marry a woman. He said: Do you have anything to give her? The man said: No, by God, I have nothing. The Prophet looked at a ring on his hand and said: What is this ring in your hand? He said: It is mine, O Messenger of Allah. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said: You have lost it — go and marry her with this seal. So ease in marriage is a virtue, as has been stated, and there is nothing wrong with going beyond it. Allah Almighty says: *And if you have given one of them a great amount of wealth, do not take anything back from it.* (82)

It is narrated from al-Husayn ibn 'Ali, peace be upon him, that he married a woman and sent her one hundred slave girls, each worth one thousand dirhams. We have also narrated from Abu 'Abdullah, peace be upon him, that he was asked about the dowry — he said: It is whatever the couple agree upon, little or much, and it is obligatory. A marriage is not permissible without a dowry, though it may be contracted without one being named, and it is not permissible to consummate it until he gives her something.

(80) Al-Nisa': 4
(81) Al-Nisa': 4
(82) Al-Nisa': 20

<!-- page 145 -->
For the saying of Allah Almighty: *There is no blame upon you if you divorce women before you have touched them or specified a dowry for them.* (83) Thus the contract without naming a dowry is permissible, but it may not be consummated until he gives her something.

Abu 'Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, was asked about this and said: My father, peace be upon him, married a woman without naming a dowry for her, and when he wished to enter into the marriage, he took from me what he gave to her. He was also asked about the saying of Allah Almighty: *And a believing woman, if she gives herself to the Prophet.* (84) He said that is something specific to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him. A marriage is not valid except with a dowry. If he does not name a dowry and he dies before consummation, or divorces her before entering into the marriage, she has no dowry, but she does inherit from him and he from her, and she must observe the waiting period. If he does consummate the marriage, she is entitled to a dowry equivalent to that of women of similar status, taking into account her own qualities and his circumstances and wealth — though it must not exceed the dowry of the Sunnah. If it does exceed that amount, it is to be returned to that limit. The dowry of the Sunnah, as we have mentioned, is five hundred dirhams.

It is related, peace be upon him, that a man who married a woman according to her own ruling was told: The marriage is permissible, but if they disagree about the amount of the dowry, it becomes equivalent to the dowry of her peers, and it is not permissible for that to exceed the dowry of the Sunnah.

It is narrated, peace be upon him, that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, forbade exchange marriages. Abu 'Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: It is offensive for a man to give his daughter in marriage to another man on the condition that the other man gives his daughter to him in return, with no dowry between them. He said: Two men came to him, peace be upon him, so he married each man's daughter to the other without a dowry. He said: No — there must be a dowry. This applies when the contract stipulates that the marriage of one is the marriage price for the other, in which case both contracts are annulled. But if the contract is a straightforward marriage contract with no such stipulation, the contract is permissible and each woman is entitled to a dowry equivalent to that of her peers, as we have described.

It is also permitted for them to contract a marriage based on specified goods — the narrators reported from 'Ali, peace be upon him, that he said concerning a man who married a woman with a bridesmaid as her dowry. Abu 'Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: Whoever marries —

(83) Al-Baqarah: 236
(84) Al-Ahzab: 50


<!-- page 146 -->

A woman whose dowry is unknown does not thereby have her marriage invalidated; rather, she is entitled to a dowry comparable to that of women of her standing, and it shall not exceed one standard dowry. The Prophet (peace be upon him) was asked about a man who had married a woman in exchange for the service of a slave-girl who was his manager, and who then divorced her before consummating the marriage. He said that the woman was entitled to one day of the slave-girl's service and her master to one day; and if the master returned the slave-girl to her managerial duties or sold her, the woman would receive half her value. Some held that such an arrangement constitutes a form of friendship for women.

It was narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's peace be upon him) that he said: It is not permissible in Islam to conclude a marriage in exchange for a wage paid to the father, as Moses himself received a reward, but the friendship must belong to the wife. If a man marries a woman on the basis that he will perform some work for her, the marriage is permissible and the wage counts as the dowry; but before he consummates it he must either fulfill the agreed work for her or give her something in its place.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: A woman came to the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) and said, "O Messenger of Allah, marry me." He said, "Who is present?" A man from among the people said, "I am, O Messenger of Allah." He said, "What will you give her?" He said, "I have nothing." He said, "Nothing at all?" So the woman turned away. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) called her back, and no one else stood up, so she returned again. He called her back a third time and said to the man, "Do you know anything of the Qur'an?" He said, "Yes." He said, "Then she is your wife by virtue of what you know of the Qur'an."

'Ali (peace be upon him) said: If a man marries a woman on the condition that he will teach her a chapter of the Book of Allah, then it is preferable that before consummating the marriage he either teach it to her or give her something in lieu of it. It was also narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's peace be upon him) that he said: Once a woman has entered upon her husband, she has no claim to the unpaid portion of her dowry unless she produces evidence — meaning that if she claims he paid her nothing and that nothing of her dowry remains due, the burden of proof lies with her.

Abu 'Abd-Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: If a dowry is divided into an advance and a deferred portion, the advance must be paid before consummation; if she refuses to allow consummation until it is paid, she may not be compelled to do so. The deferred portion falls due upon death or separation — that is, if no specific term was stipulated; but if it was stipulated that the deferred portion would become due after consummation, it was narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that a woman brought a man before him who had married her, consummated the marriage, and then named a dowry for her. He said, by the prayers of Allah upon him: Do not grant him any extension for her dowry; since he has consummated the marriage, she is entitled to it in full. If he pays her her right, well and good — and Allah knows best. If he paid nothing in advance, or deferred the entire dowry, whether named and paid early or late, the time

<!-- page 147 -->

limit is that which the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) indicated: people are bound by their conditions. If he names no term but does name a dowry for her, she may withhold herself from consummation until he pays it; that right belongs to her.

It was narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said concerning a man who concludes a marriage contract with a woman secretly, agreeing to a certain dowry, and then publicly announces a different, higher dowry: the first is the true marriage contract and it is by that contract that the dowry is determined. He also said, by the prayers of Allah upon him: If the curtain is lowered and the doors are closed, the full dowry becomes obligatory.

Abu Ja'far and Abu 'Abd-Allah (may Allah's prayers be upon them) said: If the woman is described as having been secluded with her husband but she herself acknowledges that he did not touch her — even if he had been alone with her, covered her, and closed the door upon them — and he then divorces her, she is entitled to only half the dowry, and she must observe the waiting period, because the 'iddah is not waived merely by her claim that he did not touch her. But if he closed the door and lowered the curtain over her, the full dowry is due.

From 'Ali, Abu Ja'far, and Abu 'Abd-Allah (may Allah's prayers be upon them all) it was narrated that they said concerning a man who frees his slave-woman and makes that manumission her dowry: Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali said, by the prayers of Allah upon him, that this is valid provided the condition is stated before the manumission — that is, he says to her: "I am marrying you on the condition that your dowry is your freedom," and then frees her; or he says to her: "I free you for the sake of Allah, on the condition that your freedom is your dowry and I marry you on that basis." In that case the choice belongs to her: if she wishes she may marry him, and if she wishes she need not, because she has been freed and has come to own herself independently of him. If he marries her afterwards, he must marry her with a proper dowry.

It was narrated from him that he was asked about a man who steals money and uses it to contract a marriage, or buys a slave-girl with it. He said: The private relations are lawful, but he is required to return the money, and he is a sinner for what he has done, and he must make restitution for what he stole.

**Mentioning the Conditions in Marriage:**

We have narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) and from the imams of his household that every condition that contradicts the Book of Allah is void. From 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) it is narrated that he adjudicated concerning a man who married a woman and stipulated to her family that if he were to take another wife or take a concubine, she would be free. He said, by the prayers of Allah: The condition of Allah Almighty takes precedence over their conditions; if he wishes he may fulfill his promise, and if he wishes he may marry another or take a concubine, and no blame attaches to him for doing so, provided he accepts the consequence stipulated if he does.

From Abu Ja'far and Abu 'Abd-Allah (may Allah's prayers be upon them), as likewise from 'Ali (may Allah's peace be upon him), it was narrated that he adjudicated concerning a man who married a woman and stipulated that she should have authority over

<!-- page 148 -->

her own affairs. He said: This violates the Sunnah and assigns a right to one who is not its proper holder. The husband is the guardian; consummation and divorce rest in his hand. The condition is therefore nullified.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: A condition that is lawful — one that does not contradict the Book of Allah — is binding on the one who married the woman on its basis. Such valid conditions include stipulating that she shall remain with her family in a known land, or in a specified dwelling. This is permissible.

It was also narrated from him (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: Whoever marries a woman on the condition that he will provide her a fixed daily maintenance and not be absent from her during the day — this condition does not invalidate the marriage but is not itself obligatory; she retains her right to the division of nights and maintenance that is due to wives generally. But if a man marries a woman who fears he may harm her or divorce her, and he agrees with her to forgo some of his rights regarding her maintenance and division of nights, that is permissible, in accordance with the words of Allah the Almighty: *"If a woman fears discord or desertion from her husband, there is no blame upon them if they come to an agreement between themselves."*

Abu 'Abd-Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: If he stipulates for her that should he take another wife or take a concubine she shall have one hundred dinars or whatever sum he has committed to, the marriage is permissible. If the sum he has committed to is drawn from the original dowry, it is obligatory; if it rests only on the additional stipulation, it is not obligatory. This is the meaning of the saying of Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him): A man should not use divorce against one wife as a condition for marrying another. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) forbade a woman to seek the divorce of her sister wife so that she might take her place. And the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) forbade the marriage of pleasure.

Regarding the marriage of pleasure which they permitted: it is narrated that a man would say to a woman, "Give yourself to me in marriage," and she would marry him for a dirham or two dirhams or whatever sum was named, for a stated term — one day, two days, or whatever duration they agreed upon. They said that if they agreed on this, it was permissible for him until the end of the agreed term; and when the term expired, the separation was automatic without divorce. They claimed there was no 'iddah, and no mutual inheritance. They also said it was not permissible with free women already married, though some of them claimed it was permissible with married women — and this is pure fornication, of which there is no doubt,

<!-- page 149 -->

and no concealment. Allah Almighty has permitted intimacy only through two paths: through the bond of marriage, and through the right hand of possession, for the Almighty said: *"Those who guard their private parts — except with their wives or what their right hands possess, for they are not blameworthy."*

If this pleasure union is not a marriage, then the woman cannot be separated from her putative husband without divorce and without 'iddah, for Allah the Almighty says: *"O Prophet, when you divorce women, divorce them at the beginning of their waiting period"* (87); and He says: *"And you shall have half of what your wives have left"* (88); and: *"They have the half of what you have left"* (89). Moreover, any child would follow its father, because the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said: The child belongs to the bed — meaning to the one who owns the bed — and if the union is neither a marriage nor a right of possession, then it is fornication. Reports forbidding pleasure marriage from the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) are abundant, as is his statement: "There is no marriage except with a guardian and two upright witnesses." Those who hold that pleasure marriage requires a guardian and witnesses thereby acknowledge that it cannot stand without these, yet the pleasure arrangement fails to satisfy these conditions. It was narrated from 'Ali, Abu Ja'far, and Abu 'Abd-Allah (may Allah's prayers be upon them all) that they declared the marriage of pleasure void, forbade it, and prohibited it.

It was narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's peace be upon him) that he adjudicated concerning a slave-woman who had fled from her master and deceived a man by claiming to be free, so he married her and had children by her; then her master established his claim over her. He decreed the slave-woman be returned to her master, and that the man who had been deceived was responsible for the value of his children by her to their master — unless the master wished to grant them to their father. If the man bought her and she bore him a child, and then her master later established his claim, she would be returned to the one whose right had been vindicated, and the value of her child would be owed by the father. This ruling applies as well to the one who sold her, even if he had violated her or had known when he married her that she was owned and that her master had not given her in marriage — in that case, both she and her child belong to the master.

From 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) it was narrated that he said concerning a free woman whom a male slave had approached, claiming to be free, and she had married him on that basis: he may separate them if the woman wishes. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: She owns her own decision; if she wishes she may remain with him, and if she wishes she may leave him. If she chooses

<!-- page 150 -->

to separate from him and he has not consummated the marriage, she is owed nothing; but if he has consummated it, she is entitled to her dowry, and the liability rests with whoever deceived her — whether that be the slave himself or another. When the slave is eventually freed, if his lord does not give him in marriage to her, and if his lord does give him in marriage or gives him permission to marry her, then her dowry is owed by him.

It was narrated from 'Ali, Abu Ja'far, and Abu 'Abd-Allah (may Allah's prayers be upon them) that they said concerning a man who was brought a woman by another woman as a bride, and he consummated the marriage believing her to be his wife, but later learned otherwise: he must return the woman and she is entitled to a dowry for what was done to her, claimed against the one who deceived him, and his true wife — the one he had actually married — is to be restored to him.

It was narrated from them that they said concerning a man who is impotent and unable to have intercourse with women, yet conceals this from a woman he marries — she has the right of choice once she knows, and she is entitled to the dowry if she exercises her choice and he had consummated the marriage. They also said concerning a man who marries a woman who then proves to have leprosy, or vitiligo, or blindness, or insanity, or a condition in the private parts that prevents intercourse, or an obvious lameness: the husband has the right of choice — if he wishes he may retain her, and if he wishes he may return her, and she is entitled to a dowry if he has had intercourse with her, and his claim for return is against the one who deceived him with her, while the woman herself owes nothing in return.

It was narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's peace be upon him) that he said concerning a man who marries a woman claiming she is a virgin and finds her otherwise: he saw nothing objectionable in that, and said that virginity may be lost through a leap, through ablution, or through the passage of long years.

**Mentioning the Forbidden Marriage and the Permissible Marriage:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"And do not marry what your fathers married of women — except what has already passed — for it is an indecency and an abomination and an evil way. Forbidden to you are your mothers, your daughters..."* (90)

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and the Imams from his family (may Allah's prayers be upon them all) confirmed this obligation. It came from 'Ali and Abu 'Abd-Allah (may Allah's prayers be upon them) that they said, regarding the words of Allah the Almighty:

(90) Al-Nisa': 22–23


<!-- page 151 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on the Effects of Archaeology.

On the matter of mothers-in-law: it is ambiguous whether, if a man marries a woman, her mother becomes forbidden to him regardless of whether he has consummated the marriage with her daughter. The scholars said: if a man marries a woman and then divorces her, or she dies before he has consummated the marriage with her, then the marriage of her daughter is still forbidden, based on the saying of Allah Almighty: "and your stepmothers whom you have entered, but if you have not entered them, there is no blame upon you." (91)

He said: "And the stepdaughter is also forbidden," reciting the words of Allah the Almighty, who says that if a man consummates his marriage with a woman, her daughter is forbidden to him. And if he sees from her what is forbidden to others, then he may not marry her daughter.

It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) that he said concerning a man who marries a woman and then she dies or he divorces her before consummating the marriage: she is forbidden to his son, and his father may not marry her. If a man looked upon his bondswoman with desire, or touched her, or looked upon her nakedness, she becomes forbidden to his sons and his fathers. As for one who has done none of these things — neither touched her, nor looked upon her with desire — she remains his property; and they permitted the combining of two sisters in ownership while forbidding combining them in marriage. They said: if a man has consummated his relationship with one of them, the other becomes forbidden to him, and he may not approach the second until the first has left his possession through manumission, sale, or gift. It is not permissible for him to give her away to his son. If she dies, it is permissible for him to approach the other, but it is forbidden for him to combine the two in marriage, because Allah Almighty says: "and that you combine between two sisters" (92), and He made no exception for those who are owned as opposed to those who are free.

It is narrated from 'Ali, Abu Ja'far, and Abu 'Abd-Allah (peace be upon them all) that they said: a man should not marry the sister of a woman he has divorced until her waiting period ('iddah) expires and his right of revocation is extinguished, for if that right remains, he effectively holds both sisters together. If, however, the divorce was a final and irrevocable one, he may marry her sister and others, provided he does not exceed three other free women. They also said that it is not permissible to combine a woman with her maternal aunt, nor with her paternal aunt. 'Ali (peace be upon him) said: the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) forbade that.

(91) Al-Nisa': 23
(92) Al-Nisa': 23

<!-- page 152 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on the Effects.

He (peace be upon him and his family) forbade it. And the daughter of Hamza was offered to him, and he said: I do not know her, for she is my niece through breastfeeding, because he (peace be upon him and his family) was nursed along with Hamza.

They (peace be upon them) did not see any prohibition in combining the daughters of paternal cousins or maternal cousins with a man's wife. The same applies to a woman whom a man divorces or who dies, and another man then marries her — it is permissible for the son of her first husband to marry the other, and the first husband is not related to the other.

They said: if a man commits fornication with his wife's mother, sister, or daughter, this does not invalidate the marriage contract or render the lawful unlawful. However, if he commits fornication with a woman and she gives birth thereafter, it is not permissible for her son to marry the other's daughter.

They gave permission (peace be upon them) regarding a woman whom a man has tested in illicit conduct. It was narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that he said: if a woman commits adultery before her husband has consummated the marriage with her, it creates a separation between them, she is subjected to the prescribed punishment (hadd), and her marriage contract is invalidated because the offense occurred prior to consummation. However, the separation is conditional upon the husband's wish: if he chooses to remain with her, she is his wife.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace be upon him) said, when asked about a sick man who contracts a marriage and then dies: the marriage is valid and she inherits from him.

It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) that he said concerning a man who marries two sisters or five women in a single marriage contract: the one whose name was mentioned first in the contract — in the case of two sisters — has a valid marriage, and four of the five women have valid marriages, while the marriage of the second sister and the fifth woman after the four is annulled. Even if the identity of the annulled marriage is not known, all of them (peace be upon them) said that a man who marries a woman during her waiting period ('iddah) commits a forbidden act. If he knows it is impermissible, he may never be reunited with her. If they were ignorant and contracted the marriage, the contract is invalidated once the 'iddah has ended, and the woman may then remarry.

From Abu 'Abd-Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, from his father, from his grandfather, from the Commander of the Believers (peace be upon them all), and from the Imams of his progeny: it is narrated that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) forbade a man from marrying the wife of his father and the daughter of his wife.

Mentioning the Missing Husband:

We have narrated from Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace be upon him) that he said: if a man goes missing from his family and his wife then remarries, the first husband, upon his return, has the greater right to her, whether or not the second husband has consummated the marriage.

<!-- page 153 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on the Effects.

If the second husband has consummated the marriage with her, then she is entitled to her dowry for what was made lawful from her.

Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: leave the wife of the missing man in her situation. If her case is brought before the governor, he should give a waiting period of four years and write to inquire about the missing man. If four years elapse and he does not appear, the governor should summon the guardian of the missing man and ask whether the missing man has wealth. If he has wealth from which his wife's maintenance can be provided, and if the guardian spends on her from the missing man's wealth or from his own means, then she has no right to remarry. If the guardian does not have the means to support her and the missing man has no wealth, the guardian should divorce her, and she must observe her waiting period of three monthly cycles. If this divorce is carried out and the missing husband returns before the 'iddah expires, he has the greater right to her. If her 'iddah has ended, it is permissible for her to remarry.

It was said to him: O son of the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family), what if the guardian spends on her and she says, "I am not patient; I want what other women have"? He said: she has no recourse if the guardian spends on her.

The missing man is the one who has left his home and his whereabouts are unknown — whether he was seen between the two battle lines in time of war and then the people dispersed, or he set sail on a ship that capsized, or whether no one on the journey knows where he has settled, or he has gone missing. As for one whose location is known, his ruling is not that of the missing man, and the ruling regarding his wife is that of a woman whose husband is merely absent — she may not remarry unless he dies or divorces her. He (peace be upon him) said: if the whereabouts of the absent man are known, his wife may not remarry.

Mentioning Breastfeeding:

I have mentioned above the saying of Allah Almighty: "and your mothers who have suckled you" (93). We have narrated from the Imams of the family of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family, and peace be upon them all) that it is forbidden through breastfeeding what is forbidden through lineage. I have mentioned above who is forbidden from women, and whoever descends through breastfeeding holds the same status of prohibition.

It is permissible for a man to marry a woman who has breastfed his sons, and to marry from among her daughters those she did not breastfeed him from, because she is not his mother through breastfeeding and is not a wife to his father. He may also marry whomever he wishes from:

(93) Al-Nisa': 23

<!-- page 154 -->
Volume Two.
A Book Brief on the Effects.

Her daughters, and he may marry her daughter if the woman is among the daughters of a woman who breastfed his brother or sister. Likewise, a man may marry the daughters of women who breastfed his son and their daughters, because they did not breastfeed his son — there is no relationship between them and him through breastfeeding or through any other bond. Rather, it is forbidden to marry the breastfeeding woman herself and her cousins through breastfeeding, while the marriage of female cousins through breastfeeding is permissible, just as they are permissible through lineage. A man may marry a woman while his father marries her daughter from another man, and the father and son may each marry one of two sisters.

It was narrated from 'Ali (peace be upon him) that he said: breastfeeding is made forbidden by little and much alike — even a single suckle is forbidden, based on the saying of Allah Almighty: "and your mothers who have suckled you" (94). Breastfeeding applies whether it is little or much, for even a small amount nourishes and from it flesh and blood grow and bones are strengthened proportionately.

It was narrated from 'Ali (peace be upon him) that he said: breastfeeding that creates prohibition is that which occurs within the two years, because Allah says: "mothers shall suckle their children for two complete years" (95). What occurs after the two years does not constitute breastfeeding and does not create any prohibition. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said: "There is no breastfeeding after weaning."

It is narrated from 'Ali, Abu Ja'far, and Abu 'Abd-Allah (peace be upon them all) that they said concerning a woman who causes her husband to drink her milk in order to make herself forbidden to him through that claim, or who causes her bondswoman to drink it, or breastfeeds her: they said (peace be upon them) that the husband should discipline his wife, but this does not create any prohibition for him, because the breastfeeding of an elder is not breastfeeding. If a man is breastfed after two years from his birth, or if a husband's wife causes him to drink her milk, nothing is made forbidden thereby. However, if a child drinks from a woman's milk before the age of two years — whether by suckling or by being given it to drink through the nose — that constitutes breastfeeding.

They said (peace be upon them): the milk of the stallion (i.e., the man who fathered the milk) creates prohibition. This applies when a man has wives or nursing mothers among his household, and one of them breastfeeds a slave boy while another breastfeeds a slave girl — these two become siblings through breastfeeding, even if they did not nurse from the same breast, as long as the same man's fatherhood encompasses them all and the milk is attributed to him.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace be upon him) was asked about a woman of a man who breastfed a slave girl — is it appropriate:

(94) Al-Nisa': 23
(95) Al-Baqarah: 233

<!-- page 155 -->
A Book Brief on the Effects.
Volume Two.

For his son who was not suckled by her to marry her? He said: No. It was said to him: and does she become like a sister to him through breastfeeding? He said: Yes, from the father's side.

He said: there was a man who had two wives who both gave birth to sons, and one of his wives breastfed a slave girl belonging to other people. He said: the other son may not marry this slave girl, because she was breastfed with the milk of the same father, and breastfeeding makes forbidden what lineage makes forbidden.

He (peace be upon him) was asked about a slave girl belonging to a man whose wife or the mother of his child claimed to have breastfed her. He said: do not believe her, for she is pulling something toward herself.

Concerning a woman who breastfed a slave boy and a slave girl and then denied it, he said: if she denies it, so be it. It was said to him: if she later retracts her denial and claims again that she breastfed them, he said: do not believe her.

It is permissible for one just woman to testify in matters of breastfeeding. If there is no just woman available, her testimony alone does not suffice.

He (peace be upon them) forbade breastfeeding through the milk of fornication — as when a woman commits adultery and gives birth through that act — and he said that such a woman's breastfeeding, or that of a daughter born of adultery, is not permitted. However, they did permit breastfeeding by polytheist women if they are within a marriage relationship, while disliking breastfeeding through the milk of those known for enmity toward the family of Muhammad (peace be upon him and his family).

Breastfeeding through the milk of fornication does not make anything forbidden. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: forbidden milk does not make the lawful unlawful — such as a woman who was breastfed with lawful milk and then later with milk from fornication. He said: whoever breastfeeds a girl with the milk of fornication, it does not make marrying her forbidden, because forbidden milk does not render the lawful impermissible.

It was narrated from 'Ali (peace be upon him) that he ruled concerning a man who had married a woman, given her her dowry, and not yet consummated the marriage, and then it became established that there was a nursing relationship between them: she must return to him what she had taken.

It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) that he forbade women from excessively breastfeeding those boys whom they do not know.

Mentioning the Marriage of a Slave:

Allah Almighty said: "And whoever among you cannot afford to marry free believing women, then let him marry from those whom your right hands possess of your believing slave girls." He said: "That is for him among you who fears falling into sin. And to be patient is better for you, and Allah is Forgiving, Merciful." (96)

It was narrated from 'Ali (peace be upon him) that:

(96) Al-Nisa': 25


<!-- page 156 -->
In commenting on the words of Allah Almighty — "That is for those among you who fear falling into sin" — he said: this refers to adultery, nothing else.

A free Muslim may marry a slave woman only when he fears being overcome by desire and cannot afford to marry a free woman. If he can afford a free woman, he should not marry a slave woman, and if he does not fear falling into sin, he should likewise not marry her. He must meet both conditions, for Allah has permitted the marriage of a slave woman to a free man only under these two conditions.

Abu Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: a free man may not marry a slave woman until two conditions are met — fear of sin and inability to afford a free woman. If compelled, he may marry her. When a free man does marry a slave woman, the children she bears him belong to her master as slaves, unless it is stipulated at the time of marriage that the children born of her shall be free. This is the condition imposed on the free man: the status of the child follows the status of the mother in freedom or bondage. If a slave man marries a free woman, their children are free, and it is not permissible for his master to stipulate that the children of the free woman shall be slaves — as he might with children born of the slave woman — because the freeing of the slave woman's children rests with her master, who may free them if he wishes. A free woman cannot be made to surrender her children while they are free, nor can any other person compel that.

A slave woman may not be given in marriage except with the permission of her master, for Allah says: "So marry them with the permission of their families." If a slave woman is jointly owned by two men, neither of them may lawfully approach her, even if the other permits it, because her person is indivisible and cannot be assigned to one of them without the other's consent. Her marriage is therefore not valid unless the other owner also grants permission, and her husband may not consummate the marriage until the second owner's consent is obtained. If it is not permissible for him to annul the marriage —

Abu Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, was asked about a slave woman jointly owned by two men, one of whom has given her in marriage while the other is absent — is the marriage valid? He said: the hatred of the absent owner makes it impermissible.

It was narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: it is not permissible for a free Muslim to marry a Jewish or Christian slave woman, for Allah Almighty says: "From among your believing women." Likewise, a free man should not marry a Magian or polytheist slave woman, for if that were permissible, the children of Muslims would be enslaved to the polytheists. If a man wishes to marry a slave woman, he should free her first and then marry her if he wishes.

The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, married Safiyyah bint Huyayy ibn

<!-- page 157 -->
Akhtab and drew the veil over her. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: any man who frees his slave woman and wishes to marry her should do so with witnesses and with a proper dower that is obligatory. It is permissible to make her emancipation her dower, and I have explained how that is permissible in the foregoing discussion. She may also be married with the permission of her master.

He was asked about the marriage of a female slave who has entered into a contract of emancipation, and he said: she may marry, and whatever is born to her son during the period of her emancipation contract belongs to the position she is in; what is emancipated of her passes to freedom. We will mention in the chapter on offices how that reward is to be reckoned, God willing.

It was narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said concerning a man who marries a slave woman to a free woman: a difference must be made between them, and he is to be fined her dower for what has been permitted of her person if he has consummated the marriage; if he has not consummated it, she is owed nothing.

Abu Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: if a man marries the free woman alongside the slave woman, and the free woman is aware of it, he may divide his time between the free woman and the slave woman — allotting two portions to the free woman and one portion to the slave woman. If the free woman did not know that he had a wife when he married her, and she later learns of it, she has the option: if she wishes she may remain, and if she wishes she may leave his protection, and she is owed a dower for what has been permitted of her person if he has consummated the marriage; if he has not consummated it and she chooses to part from him, she is owed nothing.

It was narrated from him that he said: it is not permissible for a free man to marry a slave woman and a free woman in a single contract. Whoever is named first in the marriage contract — if the free woman's name is mentioned first, the marriage of the slave woman is void; if the slave woman's name is mentioned first alongside a free woman who accepts that arrangement, then the marriage of both is established, provided the man is one for whom the marriage of a slave woman is permissible as we have described. The contract may proceed for the free woman, but for him to find no impediment on account of the free woman, the marriage of the slave woman is invalid. If it is not known with which of them the marriage of the slave woman began, and this was asked of him, peace be upon him —

He was asked about maintenance for a wife in this situation. He said: if a free man marries a slave woman, she serves her family during the day and comes to her husband at night. If they insist on separating them at night, he owes her no maintenance.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, was asked about a man who has young children and they own a slave woman. He said: if he desires her, let him set a fair value upon himself for her, so that the price is upon him as a debt to his children — he may then approach her if he wishes. However, if the children are grown, it is not permissible for him to take her except by purchasing her from them, and it is not permissible for him to do otherwise. A man has no right to any part of his child's wealth except by the child's own consent, save that he may be compelled to provide the child's sustenance by virtue of natural obligation.

<!-- page 158 -->
He should not take from the child's wealth if he himself is insolvent and the child is wealthy. It was narrated from him, peace be upon him, that he was asked about a man who had a slave woman, and his wife feared that he would take her for himself, so she ordered her son to approach her and he did so. He said: she has sinned, and her son has sinned, and the son is liable to the prescribed punishment if he is an adult. He disliked men gaining access to her even if she is forbidden to the master, because what is unlawful does not make lawful what is otherwise lawful.

It was narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he disliked a man approaching a slave woman when another person has a stipulated right over her. The master may give her in marriage with the permission of her master, and her ruling remains that of a slave woman until her master dies, at which point she is freed. If a slave woman is freed while she is with a husband — if her husband is free, she remains his wife and has no option; if he is a slave, she has the option to remain with him or leave upon being freed.

The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him and his family: Barirah was a slave woman and she had a slave husband, and she was grieved. When she was freed, the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, gave her the choice, and she chose herself and he ordered her to observe a waiting period of three menstrual cycles.

He said, peace be upon him: if her master helps her to fulfill her emancipation contract — even if she is elderly — she has no option over her husband on account of that.

**Mentioning the marriage of slaves:**

Allah Almighty said: "A slave who is owned and has no power over anything." The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, forbade a slave from marrying without the permission of his master. He said: any free woman who gives herself in marriage to a slave without the permission of her guardian has made her person unlawful and is owed no dower — meaning if the master denies it. If the master permits the slave to marry, then it is permissible, and the dower is the obligation of the one who undertook the marriage contract.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, was asked about a man who gives his slave woman in marriage to one of his male slaves: the marriage is valid, he may give her something as a dower, and the right of separation rests with the master — if he wishes he may divorce them, and if he sells the slave man, that constitutes a separation between them, and if he sells them together, the buyer may separate them if he wishes.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: if a slave woman is sold and she has a free husband, she remains his wife and her person is not permissible to the buyer until her husband divorces her or dies, and she observes her waiting period. He said, peace be upon him: if a woman comes to own her husband or any portion of him, the marriage between them is invalidated — she owns him by virtue of the ownership itself. It is not permissible for a woman to marry her own slave unless she first frees him; if she wishes she may free him and then

<!-- page 159 -->
marry him afterward. It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, that he directed the slave to purchase: he may approach the slave woman as his master has permitted him, and he may not marry more than two free women. He may marry four slave women, and their divorce is in the hand of his master, who may divorce them whenever he wishes.

**Mentioning the marriage of polytheists:**

The marriages of polytheists among themselves — whether from the same sect or from different communities — are valid if contracted according to their own customs. We have narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: if a man and his wife are among captives, their marriage holds even if her husband is taken captive a few days later. This remains so unless one of them enters the House of Islam; if that happens, their marriage is invalidated, and remarriage is not permissible except by a new contract — even if one had been a Sabbath observer before the other. Whichever of them remains captive among the spoils, for as long as they remain there, their marriage is upheld between them. If one of them enters the House of Islam, their marriage is invalidated. If she becomes Muslim and her husband is then taken captive after her Islam, she observes three menstrual cycles but does not thereby acquire him.
