<!-- page 195 -->

disliked because of the element of ostentation it introduces. This applies to all acts of worship. Whoever owns something and has not received a charity for it may give it in charity and give it away. Whoever gives something in charity is not permitted to return to it. Whoever imprisons something as a waqf for his son, or his freed client, or specific named individuals, and removes it from his hand and delivers it to them — this is permissible and in accordance with whatever condition is stipulated in it. It is held in trust for them. If they later disagree about it and he fears corruption between them over it, they may sell it if they wish and divide its price in proportion to their shares as it was set for them, if it was imprisoned with their personal interests in view. If he imprisoned it for their descendants, or said that if they became extinct it would become a charity, then it is a charity — they are not permitted to pledge allegiance over it. If he makes the final disposition of it a charity, it passes to the poor and the needy after those for whom it was made are extinct. Even if he did not make its final disposition a charity, and those for whom he established it become extinct, it returns to him if he is alive, or to whoever has the right to his inheritance if he has died. If a condition is stipulated that whoever among the women imprisoned in it marries loses her right to it, and they are assured, they return to it — this is permissible. If he says that if he protests he is entitled to abolish the endowment, then it is inheritance to his heirs if he dies, and he had the right to retract it when he wished.

On Bequests

God Almighty said: "It is prescribed for you that when death approaches one of you, if he leaves any good, he make a bequest." Death is ever-present for every living soul, for none knows when it will come.(140) "Let there be witnesses among you when death approaches one of you at the time of making the bequest."(141) The Messenger of God — peace be upon him — said: "A Muslim should not let two nights pass without having his will written at his head." He also said: "Whoever does not make a good bequest at the time of death has lacked in his mind and his reason." They asked: "O Messenger of God, how can the dying person make a bequest when death comes to him?" He testified and made his bequest and spoke at length. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may God's prayers be upon him, said: "The bequest is a right upon every Muslim." Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may God's prayers be upon him, said: "The bequest is obligatory upon every Muslim, and no one should let two nights pass

(140) Al-Baqarah: 180
(141) Al-Ma'idah: 106

<!-- page 196 -->

without having his will written. No dying person dies except that God Almighty returns to him his reason, his hearing, and his sight before death so that he may make his bequest — he may take it or leave it." We have narrated from the Imams of the House of the Messenger of God — peace be upon him — that they authorized the bequest of up to one-third and below of what the testator leaves behind. They did not permit more than one-third except with the permission of his heirs. Whoever makes bequests in which he stipulates that Hajj be performed on his behalf, that a slave be freed, and that an expiation be made for his obligations — if he did not perform Hajj and is unable to, and the bequest is within one-third — then the order is: Hajj first, then expiation, then manumission, and what remains of this shall be distributed. If he renews the bequest saying that two-thirds of it should be spent on such-and-such, then either he made a bequest and named only the two-thirds with no others, and he bequeathed that Hajj be performed on his behalf — then Hajj is not to be excluded from all that he left. Similarly, if he says that he owes zakat or other obligations, and he bequeaths that they be paid, they shall be paid from all of his estate. What was voluntary is to be taken from the one-third. If he has already performed Hajj and bequeaths that Hajj be performed on his behalf, it is to be taken out only from the one-third. There is no bequest for an heir unless the other heirs permit it — God Almighty has assigned the heirs their shares of inheritance, and nothing is added to them unless the testator makes a bequest for a parent or a relative who does not inherit from him, such as if his father were enslaved or a polytheist, or if the relationship is through nursing, or for the parents of the underworld. If a man, during the illness from which he dies, acknowledges before his heirs or one of them a debt — and his case is one of suspicion — his acknowledgment is not valid except by the testimony of the nearest person. This is unless the remaining heirs permit it. If he is ill but otherwise sound, such an acknowledgment is permissible. If a man makes a bequest and then wishes to retract it, he may do so, and he may increase or decrease in his bequest as long as he is alive and of sound mind. If he makes a bequest that exceeds one-third and his heirs learn of it during his lifetime and approve and ratify it, they have no right to retract their approval after his death.

<!-- page 197 -->

Chapters of the Obligations

<!-- page 198 -->


<!-- page 199 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

He mentioned the duties of the child, parents, spouse, grandfather, grandmother, and brothers.

Allah Almighty said: "Allah advises you concerning your children: for a male the equivalent of the share of two females." And: "If there are more than two daughters, they receive two-thirds of what is left; if there is only one, she receives half. For each of his parents, one-sixth of what he leaves, if he has a child; but if he has no child and his parents are his heirs, then his mother receives one-third — if he has brothers, his mother receives one-sixth — after any bequest he has made or debt." (142)

The prayers of Allah be upon the Ahl al-Bayt. They relate that whoever dies leaving behind both male and female children, with no other heirs, the inheritance is divided among them such that a male receives the equivalent of the share of two females. If he leaves behind one son and one daughter, the son receives two-thirds and the daughter one-third. If he leaves behind a single son with no other heir, the entire inheritance belongs to him. If he leaves behind two sons or a group of males, the inheritance is divided equally among them. If he leaves behind one daughter with no other heir, she receives half by virtue of the name Allah has designated for her in what we have recited, and the remaining half is returned to her through the bond of kinship, because Allah Almighty says: "Those who believed afterward and emigrated and strove alongside you — they are of you, and those related by blood are more entitled to each other in the Book of Allah." (143) Whether they are two daughters or a group, the first two-thirds belong to them by name and the remaining third is returned through the bond of kinship.

Even when a child is present alongside obligatory heirs such as the husband, wife, grandmother, and parents, the obligatory shares that Allah Almighty has designated for those heirs are given first, and whatever remains goes to the child or children, divided as we have mentioned. If there is no son, but the son of a son is present, he takes the place of the child — male standing in for male, female for female. Among the grandchildren, those closer to the deceased exclude those who are more remote. Among all those whom Allah Almighty has named in the Book as heirs, the one more closely related to the deceased is more deserving than the one less closely related. If something remains beyond the named shares, it is returned to the sons and their group if they are present, in proportion to their shares of the inheritance — except that the husband and wife do not receive the return unless they have a kinship tie that entitles them to it.

(142) Al-Nisa': 11
(143) Al-Anfal: 75

<!-- page 200 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

They are entitled to receive the return by virtue of kinship. An example: a man dies leaving behind his father and a daughter. The parents receive one-sixth each, and the daughter receives half by name. The remaining one-sixth is returned in proportion to their shares: two-sixths belong to the parents and three-sixths to the daughter, making five shares in total with one share remaining. That one share is divided proportionally among the five, so the return is distributed from five — three-fifths of the inheritance going to the daughter and two-fifths to the parents.

If he leaves behind a daughter, a father, and a mother, the daughter receives half as mentioned, and the father or mother receives one-sixth as an obligation. The return then distributes the remaining share from four: one-quarter to the father or mother, and three-quarters to the daughter. This is the established practice.

If only the parents survive with no other heir, the father receives two-thirds and the mother receives one-third. If a son is also present, the rule proceeds as we have described, even if there are multiple male and female children — they receive what remains, with a male receiving the equivalent of the share of two females, however few or many they may be. If two males are present, they share what remains; if two are present alongside parents, they receive two-thirds together.

We have mentioned what applies to a single child. If a person leaves behind two parents and brothers — whether full brothers or paternal brothers — the brothers receive nothing; the mother receives one-sixth and the father takes what remains, as Allah Almighty has stated in His Book. The brothers from the mother did not deprive the mother of one-third in the sense of inheriting themselves — they simply reduce her share, so the father receives two-thirds and the mother receives one-third. The father inherits alongside the child and alongside the parents, except in the case of the husband, wife, and grandmother.

It is narrated from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that he allocated an inheritance to the grandmother and ruled that one-sixth be imposed upon her, because the grandmother inherits alongside the parents and alongside either parent alone, receiving one-sixth. If she is alone with no one who excludes her, she receives one-sixth by name, and the remainder is returned to her, so she ultimately receives the entire inheritance. Even if there are several grandmothers — the father's grandmother, the mother's grandmother, and the mother's mother's grandmother — they share two-thirds, while the mother's mother alone receives one-third. Grandmothers who are closer in degree exclude those who are more remote.

The paternal grandfather stands in the position of brothers: brothers through the father inherit as they inherit, and he is treated as one of them — whoever excludes them also excludes him. The maternal grandfather is like a brother from the mother and likewise inherits as they inherit, being excluded as they are excluded. This is because the grandfather's relationship to the deceased mirrors that of a brother: both are connected to the deceased through his father — the grandfather being that father's father, and the brother being that father's son. If the deceased leaves behind no father or mother, and is survived by a brother,

<!-- page 201 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

the inheritance belongs to those brothers. Full siblings exclude paternal brothers; if there are no full siblings, the paternal brothers take their place. If the brothers are both full and paternal, the inheritance is divided among them as with male and female shares. If they are from the mother only, the inheritance is divided equally between male and female.

As Allah Almighty said: "If a man dies leaving no children, and he has a brother or a sister, each of them receives one-sixth; but if there are more than two, they share one-third." (144) It is narrated that this verse was revealed concerning a brother and sister from the mother. If full siblings and maternal siblings are present together — along with a paternal brother and a maternal brother — the full brothers receive two-thirds divided among them, with males receiving the equivalent of the share of two females, whether they are few or many. If maternal sisters or brothers, male or female, are present with them, one-third belongs to those maternal siblings, whether they are two sisters, a brother and sister, or brothers and sisters; and the maternal siblings divide it equally regardless of sex. The paternal brothers are treated similarly.

If a person leaves behind one brother or one sister from the mother only, she or he receives one-third; a single maternal sibling receives one-sixth. If she is a sister, she receives half, and the remainder is returned to her through kinship. Brothers and sisters inherit alongside the spouse: the spouse's share is given first, then the brothers receive their respective shares as we have described. The husband receives half of his wife's inheritance if she has no son, and a quarter if she has a male or female son or several children. The wife receives a quarter of her husband's inheritance if he has no son, and one-eighth if he has a son — whether male, female, or several — according to the explicit text of the Book. Two, three, or four wives share equally in the same share as a single wife, with the child present, and a quarter if he has no son.

If a man dies leaving behind two parents and a wife, or a woman dies leaving behind two parents and a husband, the husband receives half of his wife's share, the wife receives a quarter of her husband's share, and the mother receives one-third; the father receives what remains from the text of the Book. Just as the father has precedence over the mother when brothers are present, he similarly bears the deficiency here, because Allah Almighty said: "If he has brothers, his mother receives one-sixth." (145) Since the brothers do not inherit alongside the parents, the remaining wealth here is inherited by the parents.

(144) Al-Nisa': 12
(145) Al-Nisa': 11

<!-- page 202 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

The mother receives one-third when there are no brothers, and one-sixth when there are brothers. This is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

**On the inheritance of those with kinship ties and alliances, and on inheritance by share without reliance or exclusion:**

We narrate from the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them, that whoever dies leaving behind those who have obligatory shares named in the Book, the inheritance is distributed according to the shares that Allah Almighty has named. If none of the obligatory sharers survive, then the inheritance passes to those of his kinsmen who are closest to him. When heirs are equal in closeness, they share equally. Whoever is close by the kinship of one who has a named share is more deserving than one who is close by the kinship of one who has no named share. Those who are close exclude those who are remote. Whoever is related through the deceased's father inherits the father's share as if the father were alive; whoever is related through the deceased's mother inherits the mother's share; whoever is related through a brother inherits that brother's share; and whoever is related through a sister inherits her share.

For example: a man dies leaving behind his son's son and his son's daughter. The inheritance between them is divided with the male receiving the equivalent of the share of two females. If the son's daughter and the son's son's daughter both survive, the son's daughter receives two-thirds of the father's share and one-third goes to the son's son's daughter, who represents the mother's share. Even if he leaves behind sons and daughters from a group of sons who predeceased him, and a son or daughter from a daughter who predeceased him, one-third goes to the son and daughter from the predeceased daughter, while two-thirds go to the sons of sons, divided among them with the male receiving the equivalent of the share of two females — each group inheriting the share their parent would have received if alive. Those related through the mother's kinship inherit as brothers from the mother inherit, equally between male and female. Those related through the father's kinship inherit with the male receiving the equivalent of the share of two females.

Sons of brothers and their daughters, and sisters, inherit as we have described, when no direct child or grandchild is present. Whether they are through one father or one mother, the wealth is divided among the brothers with the male receiving the equivalent of the share of two females. Paternal and maternal uncles divide the wealth between them, each inheriting the share of his father or mother if they were alive, whether fewer or more in number. Likewise, uncles and aunts divide the wealth — with the male receiving the equivalent of the share of two females for the uncle and aunt together — and their sons similarly. The sons of paternal uncles and the sons of paternal aunts receive one-third between them

<!-- page 203 -->
Volume Two — A Book That Is Brief on the Effects

and the sons of maternal cousins and maternal aunts receive two-thirds between them, divided as with male and female shares. Those who are closer in kinship to the deceased exclude those who are more remote: the uncle excludes the cousin, the son of the uncle and the son of the aunt exclude the cousin of the cousin, and the aunt excludes the cousin and his cousin, because they are closer to the deceased by one degree. Whoever is close through a full brother is more deserving than one who is close through a paternal brother. The maternal relatives' share of the sixth is that which belonged to those through whom they are related, whether male or female.

Whoever is close through someone who has a named share excludes one who is close through someone who has no named share. If multiple groups are each related through a different person with a named share, each group shares the portion belonging to the one through whom they are related, as stated above.

No share is diminished from one who has an obligatory allocation that Allah Almighty established and named, if that heir is the sole heir; nor is anything deducted from the second named share when others are also present. For example, the husband does not receive less than a quarter; the wife does not receive less than an eighth; and neither parent receives less than one-sixth. Whoever has a remainder takes it in full, whether through excess or deficiency. An example: a woman dies leaving behind her husband, her maternal brothers, and her paternal sister. Allah Almighty named half for the husband, since she has no son; the sister receives half; and the maternal brother receives one-third of what remains of the named shares. But those who carry out the obligations are perplexed by this, so they made the six shares into eight — yet Allah Almighty said: "If a woman like this has no son and she has a sister, she receives half of what he left; and he inherits from her if she has no sister." (146) The Almighty has thus made clear that this applies only to the individual case and not when others are present alongside.

He also said: "If a man dies leaving no children, and he has a brother or a sister, each of them receives one-sixth; if they are more than that, they are partners in one-third." (147)

They say that the brother whom Allah named — if he were in the place of the sister — would receive only one-sixth. This results in: the husband receiving half, the maternal brothers receiving one-third, and the paternal sister receiving one-sixth.

(146) Al-Nisa': 176
(147) Al-Nisa': 12


<!-- page 204 -->
Similarly, if she leaves a husband, parents, and a son, the husband receives a quarter and the parents receive one-sixth. What remains goes to the daughter, because if she were male it would be no different — she would not diminish the share of whoever holds the surplus. Accordingly, all matters of dependence are handled on this basis, as are the related questions within them. Brothers and sisters are mentioned alongside their mother, as we have noted that they inherit nothing in her presence. The issues of dependence are carried on this same meaning, and all of this is the saying of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

**Mentioning the Inheritance of the Son Whose Lineage Is Disputed:**

Regarding the son of adultery, the son born of a curse (li'an), the pregnant woman, the fetus, the Muslim, the disbeliever, the Magian, the apostate, and slaves:

We have mentioned above that the curse (li'an) is performed and how it is performed. Whoever disavows a son, and his wife then undergoes the curse, the son is not attributed to the husband; his lineage follows his mother, and his inheritance is established through his kinship with her. He does not inherit from the one who disavowed him, nor from the one whose mother cursed him, nor does anyone inherit through him — except one who is close to him after the curse. However, if the husband pronounced the curse and then drew the child close to himself afterward, the boy inherits from him. The one who cursed his mother does not inherit the child, nor does the child inherit from him, nor does anyone inherit through him in that direction. The child inherits through maternal kinship.

The child of adultery does not follow the adulterer who lay with his mother and from whom she bore the child. The child is not attributed to him, and neither the adulterer's relatives nor those who deny him inherit from the child. His mother inherits from him, and those close to her through kinship also draw near to him through her. He does not draw near to his father's side. As for the bastard who knows neither mother nor father, he neither inherits nor is inherited, and no one draws near to him through his parents.

The bearer — meaning one who is captured from the land of polytheism and brought under custody, whether together with others, before them, or after them — upon entering the abode of Islam becomes known to others like himself, and they are affiliated with one another. They remain in that same standing until one of them dies; the survivor inherits from the deceased by virtue of their mutual kinship during their lifetimes, and they become close through that kinship.

It is narrated from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, that he said: "The people of two different faiths do not inherit from one another." The meaning of mutual inheritance here is that each party inherits from the other. Its estimation in the language operates reciprocally, just as reciprocal action in Arabic refers to something proceeding from two agents — it is said that two men clashed when each struck the other, and

<!-- page 205 -->
that two men beat one another when each beats his companion. A Christian does not inherit from a Jew, nor a Jew from a Christian, nor a Magian from a Muslim. A Muslim, however, may inherit from them by virtue of kinship, as narrated from the Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them. The disbeliever does not obscure or block this meaning, because if polytheists do not inherit from Muslims, yet Muslims inherit from them, the people of each sect inherit among themselves according to what is obligatory and judged among them. If they seek judgment from Muslims in a matter of inheritance that is valid under their own religion, and they have not brought it before Muslims previously, it is left as it is and not presented to them.

A slave does not inherit from a free person, and a free person likewise does not inherit from a slave through kinship. The wealth of a slave belongs to his master. Whoever is a Muslim inherits before a polytheist. If someone is freed and the one who freed him dies before the inheritance is divided, the freed person has a share in it. He has no share beyond that. All of this is the saying of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

**Mentioning the Inheritance of the Murderer, the Blood Money, the Hermaphrodite, Those Who Drown Together, the Freed Slave (al-wala'), the Divorced Woman, and the One Who Has No Heir:**

Among the heirs, one who acknowledges another heir and matters of obligation: whoever kills a person deliberately or by mistake does not inherit from him, nor from his descendants, even if he is among those who would otherwise deserve the inheritance. If the parents do not kill him, he inherits from them. Among the duties ordained by Allah Almighty: a man inherits from his wife's blood money and a woman inherits from her husband's blood money — except for brothers through the mother, who inherit nothing from the blood money.

The hermaphrodite is one who possesses both the male organ and the female organ. Inheritance is assigned to such a person according to their situation: they inherit and are inherited from, they may marry and be married. They are judged according to their own declaration — if they declare themselves a man, they are judged by the rulings of men; men may marry women, and men may not marry a hermaphrodite in that case. They inherit and are inherited according to the inheritance of men. If, however, the mind inclines toward the female organ, the rulings of women apply, and they follow the course of women rather than the course of men in any respect. The matter is examined by observing from which organ the urine flows first: if it flows first from the male organ, the ruling is that of men; if it flows first from the female organ, the ruling is that of women. If both flow simultaneously, or if there is neither a male organ nor a remedy, then it is a problem that the ruler addresses. He writes on one card "Servant of Allah" and on another card "Handmaid of Allah," and on each card he places a lump of clay

<!-- page 206 -->
which he holds in his hand and says: "O Allah, You know best about this person and how You created them, so bring out the arrow for the one You have made heir among them and the one You have appointed as ruler over the rulings." He then draws one of the two pieces of clay. Whoever has not seen them and does not know what is in them — what emerges determines the ruling applied, and then the urine is tested: the person stands facing a wall, extends their hands, and is told to stand apart from the wall as far as possible. If the urine penetrates past the wall, it is known that it emerged from the male organ; if it runs between the legs, it is known that it emerged from the female organ. Neither men nor women look at the person beforehand in order to determine this.

If people drown together in a ship, or a house collapses upon them, or they are burned in a fire, or they are killed in battle, and it is not known who died before his companion — and they were relatives — then each one of them inherits from the other according to what each would have inherited, and then the other inherits from him in turn according to his own inheritance. They inherit from one another what each has left behind. This is like two brothers, one of whom is the freed client (mawla) of one people and the other is the freed client of other people, who die in such a situation with no heir other than their respective masters. If the estate of one is a thousand dinars and the estate of the other is a hundred dinars: the owner of the hundred inherits from the thousand as though he were alive and his brother died first, inheriting from him accordingly; and the owner of the thousand inherits the hundred as though he were alive and his brother died first, inheriting from him accordingly. Thus it falls to the master who left the thousand, and to the master who left the hundred, in this manner — each master inherits what the deceased client inherited, and they inherit from each of them accordingly.

Whoever divorces his wife with an irrevocable divorce — there is no inheritance between them — unless he divorces her while he is ill, in which case she inherits from him as long as he remains ill, unless the divorce becomes legally sound or she remarries. If the divorce is sound or she remarries, she has no inheritance from him. As for the wife's origin: if he divorces her, he inherits from her and she inherits from him, unless her waiting period has expired. Whoever dies and has no heir except the clients (mawali) who freed him, or those who caused their causes — their inheritance belongs to those clients. If he has a relative, his inheritance goes to the relative by virtue of kinship, not by virtue of the client relationship. Loyalty (al-wala') is inherited by men and women alike, and through the father who freed the slave. Through him closeness is established, as with men in general. Whoever is closest in degree of loyalty is more deserving of it than one who is more distant, as we mentioned in the chapter on loyalty concerning the elder — that is, the man who frees a slave. Then the freed person dies leaving two children; one of the children dies leaving a son. Then the master dies without an heir. His inheritance goes to the son of his son, who preceded him in degree, not to the son of the freed slave who is more remote. This is similarly the case with inheritance by kinship. Whoever has no heir in the degree of an inheritor except a slave whom he freed

<!-- page 207 -->
inherits from him just as he would inherit from him. If he leaves a slave whom he freed and a master who freed him, his inheritance goes to his master who freed him rather than to his slave whom he freed.

If some of the heirs acknowledge another heir whom others do not know and for whom they have no testimony supported by evidence, and the remaining heirs deny him, then the one who is closer inherits according to what he would have inherited. If a group of heirs draws the claimant close or rules that he is a relative, they give him what remains after their own shares — that is, whatever has accrued to them — and those who do not have a complete inheritance owe him nothing. This is like a man who has died leaving two sons: one of them acknowledges a third brother and the other denies him. The one who denies inherits one-half from his father. The one who acknowledges inherits one-third, and pays one-sixth to the one he acknowledged — corresponding to what the latter would have inherited had they been three sharing in thirds.

The shroud of the deceased is taken from his principal estate — whatever he left — before any division, as is his debt. If he has a debt and has made a bequest from one-third of his estate, the bequest is not carried out without the consent of the heirs unless they choose to extract it in full. What remains thereafter is divided according to the obligations of inheritance. Offices (al-kitabah) — some of those bound by them inherit and are inherited according to the extent of their freedom, as we have mentioned. The ruling on the validity of offices and how the intention of manumission is established in the chapter on offices has been addressed. Whoever has no known heir — his inheritance goes to the treasury of the Muslims. If some of the heirs acknowledge a debt, each is obliged to pay from his share of the inheritance in proportion to what he acknowledged, even if he bears testimony to it as a just witness — testimony that would be admissible on behalf of all the heirs. Even if a man acknowledges a child from his slave-woman and his wife, and then disavows the child afterward, such disavowal is not permissible; the child is his son. All of this is the saying of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

<!-- page 208 -->
<!-- page 209 -->
<!-- page 210 -->
<!-- page 211 -->
**Mention of Retribution for Killing and Wounds:**

Allah Almighty said: "And do not kill the soul which Allah has forbidden except by right, and whoever is killed unjustly — We have given his heir authority." (148) And He said: "And whoever kills a believer intentionally, his recompense is Hell, wherein he will abide eternally, and Allah has become angry with him and has cursed him and has prepared for him a great punishment." (149) And He said: "O you who have believed, do not consume one another's wealth unjustly but only in lawful business by mutual consent. And do not kill yourselves; indeed, Allah is to you ever Merciful. And whoever does that in aggression and injustice — then We will drive him into a Fire." (150) And He said: "Because of that, We decreed upon the Children of Israel that whoever kills a soul unless for a soul or for corruption in the land — it is as if he had slain all mankind entirely. And whoever saves one — it is as if he had saved all mankind entirely." (151)

The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, said: "If the entire nation were to gather to kill a believer, Allah Almighty would cast them all into the Fire of Hell." And it is said regarding the worst of creation before Allah Almighty: He who kills other than his killer is rewarded against Allah, and the people who kill someone who did not kill them are rewarded against Allah.

He said, the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him: "In Hell there is a valley called Sa'ir; if that valley were opened, it would fill with fire — Allah has prepared it for murderers." And he, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "The deliberate murder of a believer is among the major sins." And he said, regarding the words of Allah Almighty: "Those who disbelieve said, 'Our Lord, show us those who misled us among jinn and men; we will put them under our feet.'" (152) He, peace be upon him, said: they are Satan or the one who enacted sin and committed it. The son of Adam who killed his brother was the first to be killed unjustly.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said, regarding the words of Allah Almighty:

---
(148) Al-Isra': 33
(149) Al-Nisa': 93
(150) Al-Nisa': 29–30
(151) Al-Ma'idah: 32
(152) Fussilat: 29


<!-- page 212 -->
He killed a soul without just cause or corruption in the land — it is as if he had killed all of humanity. He will have a seat in Hell whether he kills one person or many, and his punishment will not increase beyond that. It is narrated that a man will come on the Day of Resurrection carrying a small trace of blood and will say, "By God, I did not kill you, nor did I participate in any bloodshed." Yet it will be said to him, "You did not strike the blow yourself, but you mentioned so-and-so and praised the act until he was killed, and that blood is upon you."

Thus Allah Almighty has gravely condemned unjust killing and threatened its perpetrator in His Book and on the tongue of His Messenger, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him. Whoever kills a believer unjustly and deliberately is subject to being killed, if the guardian of the slain demands it. This applies whether the murdered person was young or old, honored or lowly, so long as he was among the people of Islam. The same applies to the deliberate murderer, provided he is an adult, regardless of who he is. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said that believers are equal in blood. A man is killed for killing a woman, and if he kills her and her guardians wish to take compensation rather than retribution, they receive half the blood money — five hundred dinars — to be paid to them. If they choose the blood money, the ransom for a woman is five hundred dinars, half that of a man. If a woman is killed, her killer is killed in return, as Allah Almighty has decreed. If a slave is killed in place of a free person, the blood money is paid accordingly. Allah Almighty says: *"The free for the free, the slave for the slave."* (154)

The guardians of the slain may choose to have the killer put to death or, if the killer is a slave, to take him as compensation — and his master owes no more than the value of the slave for his crime. If a free man kills a slave, he pays the slave's value as blood money, and the ruler inflicts upon him a severe and painful punishment as a deterrent, so that people will not dare to kill with impunity. If a Muslim kills a dhimmi, he is killed in retaliation. If a Muslim kills a dhimmi, he is fined eight hundred dirhams as blood money, and he is subjected to a severe and painful punishment — though if he was accustomed to killing dhimmis, he may be put to death. The ruler is the one who oversees this and delivers the killer to the guardians of the dhimmi. A child is killed for his father or other relatives if he kills one of them and retribution is sought. The father, however, is not killed for his son. Retribution is carried out between slaves toward one another, and between dhimmis toward one another, and the guardians of the dhimmi entitled to inheritance are the ones who hold the right of retaliation. If some of them pardon the murderer or accept blood money, the murderer is not killed for those who have pardoned him, and those who have not pardoned may still take their share of the blood money if they wish. If some of the guardians are children,

(153) Al-Ma'idah: 32
(154) Al-Baqarah: 178

<!-- page 213 -->
or absent, and some are adults, then the adults hold the right to take retribution — the murderer does not wait until the children come of age or the absent ones return. Al-Hasan ibn Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him and his family, was killed by the enemy of Allah, Ibn Muljam, may Allah curse him — he struck Ali, peace be upon him, while his children had not yet reached puberty.

As for one who is killed with or without an iron weapon — if the killer intended the murder and struck the victim deliberately, and the victim died from that blow, then the guardians may demand retaliation. However, retaliation is not carried out except by means of a sword. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said: "There is no execution except by the sword." The condemned is to be struck on the neck with the sword, and he is not to be tortured or mutilated. It is permissible to question the accused about the killing and to gently seek his confession, without beating. If he is beaten and confesses under duress, his confession is not binding, for a confession obtained by compulsion is not legally valid. If two witnesses testify that he committed the killing, or if he voluntarily confesses, then the killing is established. It is not permissible for women to testify in cases of murder or in any of the cases of prescribed punishments (hudud) and retaliation (qisas), though their testimony is acceptable in cases involving financial obligations.

If one man is killed by two or by a group who cooperated in the act — each one striking him until he died — then the relatives of the slain choose one of them to be killed, for Allah Almighty says: *"A soul for a soul."* They may not kill more than one person. The guardians of the murdered then return to the remaining killers and take blood money from each of them proportionally. If there are three killers and one is put to death, his guardians take two-thirds of the blood money from the remaining two. If there are two killers and one is put to death, his guardians take half the blood money from the other. If there are more than two, the proportions are calculated accordingly, and they bear a punishment as well.

If a man restrains another while a third man kills the victim, then the killer is killed and the one who held the victim is imprisoned until he dies — after being flogged. If a man kills his own slave, he is punished with a painful punishment. If the guardians pardon the murderer, there is no claim against him, and whoever kills him after that is himself killed in retaliation. If they accept blood money from him, then whoever transgresses after that faces a painful punishment, as Allah Almighty has said. (155)

All wounds that call for retaliation — those that can be measured without risk of aggression or harm to the person from whom retaliation is taken — are subject to it, such as surface wounds and those below them. The wound is measured in length along the skin, and the cut is made in the same place to the same depth in the flesh.

(155) Al-Baqarah: 178

<!-- page 214 -->
A tooth is extracted for a tooth, an eye for an eye, a nose for a nose, an ear for an ear, or whatever portion of them has been cut, in accordance with the word of Allah Almighty: *"And the eye for the eye, the nose for the nose, the ear for the ear, and the tooth."* (156) This applies when the act was deliberate. If, however, the wound was inflicted in error, or if the nature of the wound is such that cutting in retaliation would pose a risk of causing more harm, or if the wound has dried and healed, or if a bone has been broken or damaged — in all such cases, blood money is required, and we shall mention that in its proper place.

Whoever commits such wounds deliberately is punished — it is hoped that the people of means will find it easier to pay the blood money. Retaliation is carried out between a woman and a man in wounds, and between a man and a woman in those that are explicitly defined and below. Retaliation for an eye is taken from the eye that is not shielded by iron or a mirror. The eye is then held open by bringing it close to the cutting instrument, and it is opened until it flows. All of this reflects the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

---

**On Blood Money and Its Categories:**

The blood money for a free Muslim man, whether young or old, is one thousand dinars for those who deal in gold, or its equivalent value. Those who deal in other wealth are not required to sell their goods to pay in gold — rather, the equivalent value is taken from them in the currency of their land: one thousand dinars from those who use paper currency, one hundred camels from those who possess camels, two hundred cows from those who possess cattle, one thousand sheep from those who keep flocks, and two hundred garments of such value from those who deal in cloth. This is the established Sunnah. This is the blood money for the soul, and the same applies to his parents, in the case of an error — that is, when the killer did not intend to kill the victim, as when he intended to throw at someone else and struck this person instead.

If the act was deliberate, it is treated as intentional killing, and the killer is subject to retaliation unless the relatives of the deceased accept the blood money from him or pardon him. The killer who committed the act in error must, after paying the blood money, expiate by freeing a believing slave. If he cannot find one, he must fast for two consecutive months. If he is unable to do that, he must feed sixty poor people. The blood money is the obligation of his 'aqila — the group of kin who bear responsibility — who are the heirs of the one who would inherit from him, excluding his maternal aunts, who neither bear this responsibility nor inherit from the blood money. Children and the insane are not held responsible for errors — the blood money falls upon the sane members of the 'aqila, not upon the destitute or the sane who have nothing. The sane person is not obligated beyond what is established by evidence, but whoever is in good standing and confesses and acts accordingly —

(156) Al-Ma'idah: 45

<!-- page 215 -->
— and a slave is involved, then the sane person bears no responsibility for him. The sane person among the 'aqila is not obligated to contribute to unintentional harm except when the amount reaches one-third of the blood money or more. Whatever is less than one-third is paid from the offender's own wealth.

As for the people of the dhimma, the financial burden is theirs; their 'aqila bears none of it. Whoever commits an offense independently bears the consequence alone. If a woman is struck and as a result miscarries after the fetus had settled in the womb, the offender owes twenty dinars. If she miscarries a leech-like clot of blood, the penalty is forty dinars. For a morsel-like clump of flesh, it is sixty dinars. If the fetus has developed bones, it is eighty dinars. If a fully formed child is expelled before the soul has been breathed into it, the penalty is one hundred dinars. If the soul has already been created within it, the full blood money applies.

As for one who has already died — if someone severs his head or does to him what would have caused his death had he been alive — the penalty is one hundred dinars. Whatever is done to such a body is calculated from its blood money proportionally. One should hasten to perform Hajj on his behalf if he had not done so, and his heirs do not inherit from the penalty against the dead. The penalty for harm done to animals is their price: whoever kills an animal is fined its value, and whoever injures or breaks a limb of it owes the amount by which its value has decreased. If it escapes after being struck, the harm done is wasted. If an animal enters its owner's property and they send it away or stop it unlawfully, they guarantee whatever harm results.

If a thief seeks to take a man's wealth and the man kills him while defending it, the thief's blood is forfeit. Whoever is killed while defending his property is a martyr. If the man abandons his property rather than fight, there is nothing wrong with that. If someone seeks to assault a woman and she kills him or injures him while repelling him, whatever she inflicts is without liability. Whoever dies as a result of a prescribed punishment (hadd) or lawful retaliation bears no compensatory claim.

Whoever peers into the private space of a people and they put out his eye, his claim is forfeit. The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, ruled by al-Qasamah, which applies when a man is killed and his killer is unknown — if he names his killer before dying, saying "so-and-so killed me," or if his guardians come forward and accuse someone of throwing him to his death and say "he killed him," then fifty of them swear that he killed him and thus compel the oath against the accused. If they do not reach fifty, those who are available swear additional oaths until they complete fifty. If they wish, the accused may swear the oaths on their own behalf. If the accused bring proof of their innocence, it is accepted from them.

If the deceased is found in a village and no one knows who killed him, and the people of that village are the only ones present without any mixing with outsiders, they swear that they did not kill him and do not know his killer — in that case, they are fined his blood money. Even if he is found between two villages, the closer one bears the responsibility. However, if he is found in a large city or in a village that is frequented by a mixture of people, or in


<!-- page 216 -->
A road along which vehicles pass between villages serves as evidence for the one who claims it among them, provided there is no suspicion attached to them, and the Qasama is thereby established. If two mounted riders collide and each was moving toward the other's property, and one of them dies, then half the blood money is due, because the death resulted from what both did. If the action came from one party while the other was stationary or walking, and the one who was struck died, then nothing is owed by the one who stood still. If the victim dies, the one who caused the collision owes the blood money if the act was unintentional; if it was intentional, he owes the full blood money. The physician and the veterinarian are liable for what they cause if they are ignorant of their craft; if they are knowledgeable but intend harm, they are equally liable. Circumcision carries liability for whatever is cut beyond the proper place of circumcision, whether on a boy or otherwise, and the same applies if the cut exceeds its prescribed limit. Whoever digs a well or a trench on a public road, or places an obstruction on it, is liable for any injury caused thereby. If the excavation or placement is done on land that is his by right, then he bears no liability. A rider is liable for whatever his horse strikes with its foreleg or chest, but not for what it strikes with its hind leg, since that is not within his control. If a wall leans and its owner is informed of it or the matter is brought to his attention, yet he neither repairs nor demolishes it until damage occurs, he is liable for whatever results. If a man hires a laborer who then perishes in the course of his work, nothing is owed. If a dog is kept in a house and people enter without permission, no liability attaches; but if they were given permission to enter and were then bitten, liability is incurred. If a woman severs a man's sexual organ or a man severs a woman's vulva, there is no retaliation between them — rather, the perpetrator owes the full blood money and is severely punished if the act was deliberate. If a man falls upon a woman in a manner not befitting her station, or uses force against her, and she subsequently fails to give birth as before, or loses control of her bowels, or dies from his violence, then the full blood money is owed in every such case. Whoever is found dead with no trace of the cause and no knowledge of who killed him, nothing is required except the oath from the one accused. This is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's blessings be upon them. Al-Bitar is the one who treats animals — that is, the veterinarian — the word written without the letter alif in its standard form.

Mentioning offenses against those who are wounded:

We have mentioned above that retaliation is due for intentional acts in every case where retaliation is possible without fear of exceeding the just limit in carrying it out. As for whatever does not lend itself to retaliation and where retaliation is not warranted, blood money is owed by the offender if the act was deliberate,

<!-- page 217 -->
and the same applies to one who is sane if the act was a mistake. For every bodily part of which a person has only one, the full blood money is owed; for every part of which there are two, half the blood money is owed for each. For the hair of the head, if it is plucked, falls out as a result of injury, or is burned and does not regrow, the full blood money is owed; the same applies to the eyelashes if they are stripped away. If the forehead is struck and the bone is exposed, one hundred dinars are due. If the nose or tongue is severed, or if the beard is removed and does not regrow, the full blood money is owed in each of these cases. For the eyebrow, the eye, the ear, the hand, the male sexual organ, and the female ovaries — half the blood money is owed for each one, and the full blood money is owed for both together. For the loss of an eye's vision, the full blood money is due; for the loss of hearing, the full blood money is due; for the loss of speech, the full blood money is due; and for the loss of sight, the full blood money is owed even if the pupils themselves remain intact. The same principle applies to partial loss in all of these. If a man is struck and some of his vision is lost in one eye, the unaffected eye is covered, and an egg is emptied of its contents, suspended by a hair, and the injured person is instructed to look at it; it is then moved further away until he says he can no longer see the hair, and that distance is noted. It is then measured again from four directions. The measurement is verified by covering the other eye and repeating the procedure from four sides. If his statement is consistent with what he says, the deficiency in the affected eye is established by comparison with the sound eye, and blood money is owed proportionally — whether the deficiency amounts to half, a third, less, or more — and this is calculated accordingly. Even one whose sight is entirely lacking in one eye may be assessed by comparing him to a man of average vision confirmed to be sound. Two or three such men are consulted, and if they agree or come close to agreement, his sight is measured against theirs from four directions. If there is consistency and his truthfulness is established, he receives blood money in proportion to the established deficiency. The same procedure applies to partial loss of hearing: a dirham is struck near him until he says he cannot hear it, following the same method described for sight. For loss of smell, a known scent is brought close to him and gradually moved further away, applying the same method. For loss of speech, one examines which letters he can no longer pronounce relative to the full count of letters,

<!-- page 218 -->
which total twenty-eight, and blood money is owed proportionally for the letters he is unable to pronounce. For the lips: half the blood money is due for the upper lip, and two-thirds of the blood money is due for the lower lip — one half and one sixth — reckoning from its role in eating and softness of speech. For the teeth located at the front of the mouth — four incisors, four lateral incisors, and four canines — each tooth carries fifty dinars. For the teeth at the back of the mouth, which are the molars, each tooth carries twenty-five dinars, and together they account for the full blood money. The molars number four in each jaw — four above and four below — which represents the complete count of teeth in the perfection of natural creation. Some people have five molars in each jaw, the extra being superfluous; blood money is apportioned according to the perfect original count, and if the additional tooth is not considered excessive, it carries twenty-five dinars. If part of a tooth is broken, blood money is owed in proportion to what is broken. If a tooth is blackened, it has been rendered valueless. For a child's tooth, if it is knocked out before its natural time to fall, ten dinars are owed; five dinars are owed for its replacement tooth if it grows back afterward; and if it does not grow back, the full blood money for it is owed. For the neck, the upper arm, and the elbow — if any of these is broken or dislocated and healed without defect — one hundred dinars are owed for each. For the forearm and the wrist, one-third of the blood money is owed for each, amounting to one hundred dinars. For each finger of the hands and feet, forty dinars are owed for the shoulder. For the ribs on the side of the chest that are connected — twenty-five dinars for each rib. For the ribs that are not connected and do not articulate with the sternum — ten dinars for each rib. For the hip, the thigh, and the knee — one-fifth of the blood money for each. For a blow to the shin that reaches the hollow of the bone — one-third of the blood money. For a blow to the face that causes redness without lasting effect — two and a half dinars. If the bruise is black or green — three dinars. If it is green around the eye and remains green — three dinars; if it is black — six dinars. For a wound with significant bleeding, where blood flows freely — ten dinars. For a wound that breaks through the skin and reaches the flesh without cutting it — twelve dinars. For a wound that cuts through the skin and into the flesh, severing some of it — twenty dinars. For a wound that cuts through the flesh —

<!-- page 219 -->
thirty dinars. For a wound that cuts through the skin and all the flesh down to the bone without severing it — forty dinars. For a wound that cuts through all of this and exposes the bone, laying it bare — fifty dinars. For a wound that does all of this and breaks the bone — one hundred dinars. For a wound that breaks the bone and extracts a fragment of it — one hundred and fifty dinars. For a wound in which the membrane of the brain becomes visible and is reached, incurring no further injury — one-third of the blood money. All of this applies to injuries of the head and face. For injuries to the body, half of those amounts apply. For injuries to specific organs — such as fingers and their like — blood money is owed in proportion to what they count toward the full blood money of that organ, as we have mentioned previously. All of this represents the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's blessings be upon them all.

<!-- page 220 -->

<!-- page 221 -->

<!-- page 222 -->

<!-- page 223 -->
Mentioning the establishment of hudud:

Allah Almighty said: "Whoever transgresses the limits of Allah has wronged himself." (Verse 57, Divorce: 1)

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) spoke emphatically against the abandonment of the prescribed limits, saying: "The Children of Israel perished because, when a person of noble standing committed an offense, they would leave him unpunished, but when a person of lowly standing committed the same offense, they would establish the limit against him." A woman from among the Quraysh stole a garment during the time of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family), and he ordered her hand to be cut off. Some members of Quraysh gathered around him and said: "O Messenger of Allah, she is a woman of standing among us and has honor." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "It was precisely because of this that those before you were destroyed — they would establish the limit against the weak and leave the mighty and the noble, and they perished on account of that." It was accordingly forbidden to intercede in the prescribed limits. It is narrated from him (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said: "Whoever intercedes to nullify one of the limits of Allah has dared to invalidate what Allah has established, and those who sought to nullify His limits — Allah Almighty will punish them on the Day of Resurrection." Safwan ibn Umayyah came to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) after a man had stolen from him, and the Messenger of Allah ordered the man's hand to be cut off. Safwan said: "O Messenger of Allah, for the sake of my cloak I gave it to him" — meaning he wished to pardon him. The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said to him: "Why did you not say so before you brought it to me? For once the hadd has been brought before the ruler, it must not be abandoned — it must be carried out." He (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) also said: "The back of a believer is inviolable except for a prescribed limit that is due." He further stated that whoever exceeds one of the limits of Allah diminishes what is due and transgresses beyond what has been prescribed. He said: "Allah Almighty has set boundaries, and whoever transgresses them commits injustice, as He Himself has declared." The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "The most hateful of creation to Allah is the one who strikes the back of a Muslim unjustly." He (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) also forbade the carrying out of the prescribed punishments inside mosques. It is narrated from him (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) that he said: "Whoever commits a sin and is punished for it in this world, Allah is too just to punish His servant for it again in the Hereafter; and whoever commits a sin and Allah conceals it,


<!-- page 224 -->
Ali, peace be upon him, wrote to Rifa'a ibn Shaddad, his judge over Ahwaz, a letter in which he instructed that the hudud should be upheld against the distant and the near alike, that bloodshed should not be overlooked, and that the hudud should not be neglected. When Uthman brought al-Waleed ibn 'Utbah before him and witnesses testified against al-Waleed and made clear his offenses against the Muslims, they said that he had violated the limits of Allah. So Ali brought him into a room and said, "Let him stand, and let the hadd be carried out upon him." When a man entered to administer the punishment, al-Waleed said, "I appeal to you by Allah — do not cut off my ties of kinship and do not expose me before the Commander of the Faithful." The man turned away from him. When Ali, peace be upon him, saw that one of the limits of Allah was being abandoned, he himself took the whip and said to al-Husayn ibn Ali, peace be upon him, who had followed him, "O father of Malik, step aside — he has forfeited his right." Ali then struck al-Waleed in the chest, and al-Husayn, peace be upon him, took the whip and struck him until the hadd was completed.

It is also narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: "Three things, if you do them, no calamity will come upon you: strive against your enemy, raise your limits to your imams and let them rule in them with justice, and submit to their command." He also said to 'Umar, advising him: "I advise you to judge by the Book of Allah in contentment and discontent, to swear justice between the red and the black, to uphold the hudud against the distant and the near alike." 'Umar replied, "You have summarized well and informed well."

Ali, peace be upon him, used to present prisoners every Friday, and whoever had a hadd punishment due against him would have it carried out without delay. When three witnesses testified against a man for adultery, he said, "Where is the fourth? He is not here." They said, "We will bring him after this, O Commander of the Faithful." But the hadd could only be delayed for a brief time, and so the three were given the hadd punishment for slander.

Some people came to him and said, "O Commander of the Faithful, we have come to ask you for something," and he said, "Do not ask me for anything except that I give it to you." They mentioned a man from Banu Asad against whom the hadd had been established, and they said that such-and-such a person had interceded for him. Ali kept them waiting through the night. They said, "Did you not promise us, O Commander of the Faithful, to release him?" He replied, "By Allah, I do not possess that right." He then ordered his servant Qanbar to strike two men. Qanbar, however, struck three additional lashes in anger. When this was reported to Ali, peace be upon him, he took the man from Qanbar and flogged him three lashes in recompense. He also wrote to Rifa'ah a letter regarding the believer, instructing that the believer should be treated as much as was within his capacity.

<!-- page 225 -->
The soul of the believer is generous before Allah, and to him belongs the reward of Allah. It is narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said regarding those who abstained from enemy territory and sought a pledge that nothing from what had passed would be demanded of them: "Do not give a covenant that would nullify a limit from the limits of Allah. How can the limits be set aside from those to whom they apply? Rather, the guardians of Allah and the Mujahideen go out in His way to establish them."

It is narrated from Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: "Do not exempt anyone from the limits belonging to Allah Almighty, and from whatever was known of it, one should only step forward to apply it. As for the rights of people within the hudud, it is permissible to pardon such rights before the matter reaches the ruler, but it is not permissible to pardon them after it reaches him." Whoever pardons a right that belongs to him and then seeks to enforce it has no standing.

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, that he said: "Whoever seizes a thief and pardons him, then if the thief is brought before the Sultan, let him cut off his hand. If the one from whom he stole says, 'I give him to the Sultan,' then the hadd is suspended before the matter was raised. But if it has already been raised to the ruler, the hadd is not suspended," by reason of the word of Allah Almighty: "And those who keep the limits of Allah." Once the hadd has been raised before the ruler, no one may set it aside.

**Mentioning the hadd punishment for adultery:**

Allah Almighty said: *"And do not approach adultery, for it is an indecency and an evil way."* (158) And He said: *"And those who do not invoke another god alongside Allah, and do not kill the soul that Allah has forbidden except in truth, and do not commit adultery — and whoever does that will meet punishment on the Day of Judgment and will abide therein."* (159)

The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, said: "The wrath of Allah is intense against a man who is brought to a people other than his own, who gazes at their sanctuaries and treads upon their beds." And the most severe in punishment on the Day of Resurrection is one who plants his seed in the womb of one who is forbidden to him. He also said, peace be upon him and his family: "The adulterer will stand over the people of Hell on the Day of Resurrection, and a foul drip from his private parts will fall upon the people of Hell, who will say, 'What is this stinking smell?' and they will be told, 'This is the smell of adultery.'" And an adulteress will come, and a drop from her will fall, and the people of the Fire will be harmed by her stench.

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

*(158) Al-Isra': 32*
*(159) Al-Furqan: 68–70*

<!-- page 226 -->
There is no greater sin in the sight of Allah Almighty after associating partners with Him than a man who places forbidden seed in a womb that is not lawful for him. He said: "Adultery and goodness do not gather in one house." And he delivered a sermon, peace be upon him and his family, in which he said: "Three types of people — Allah will not speak to them on the Day of Resurrection, will not purify them, and they will have a painful punishment: an adulterous elder, an arrogant king, and a lying poor man."

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "He does not commit adultery while he is a believer." Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: "When he lies upon her belly, the spirit of faith departs from him, and when he rises and repents, it returns to him." Adultery means placing the private parts in one other than a spouse, or in someone not lawfully possessed by the right hand.

Allah Almighty said: *"And those who guard their private parts, except from their wives or those their right hands possess — for then they are not to be blamed. But whoever seeks beyond that, those are the transgressors."* (160)

If an adulterer and four people testify against him that they saw him commit adultery with a woman — seeing the male organ enter her private parts — and he had a woman in his lawful bond with whom he had entered into marriage, and the woman against whom they testified had a husband in her lawful bond, or if the man and the woman both confess to adultery, or one of them confesses and the testimony of witnesses is established against the other, then each of them shall receive a hundred lashes. They are then taken out, and a hole is dug for each to a depth reaching the navel. Each descends into it upright and is settled in place. If the admission was a confession, the ruler begins with the one who confessed and orders stoning, then orders the people to stone with stones of moderate size — enough to fill the palm — until they die. They are then brought out, washed, prayed over, and buried.

The confession of an adulterer is not established until he confesses four times, provided he is of sound mind and is not under coercion. If the conviction is by the testimony of witnesses, the witnesses stone first, then the ruler, then the people. A man is not considered protected until he has consummated marriage with his wife, and she is not considered protected unless she is free, or a free Mamluk. Even if one of them is protected and the other is not, the protected one is stoned after being flogged, and the unprotected one is flogged a hundred lashes. The most severe flogging is for the adulterer of the skin, then for the adulterer with the whip, then with the mustache, then with a stick, and one does not strike a pregnant woman until she has given birth and her child is cared for, and no harm shall come to the child. If the adultery involves a major hadd,

*(160) Al-Mu'minun: 5–7*

<!-- page 227 -->
the elder takes precedence, and the one who withheld the child from one of them bears responsibility. As for the act of the people of Lot, the one who commits the act receives stoning just as adulterers are stoned. If they are unmarried, they are punished and flogged. Women who commit such acts shall receive a hundred lashes each. Whoever commits adultery with a forbidden relative shall be killed, and obedience is due to him if they are not protected. If they are protected, they are flogged and stoned. Whoever rapes a woman against her will shall be killed if he is not protected, and flogged the hadd and stoned if he is protected. No hadd falls upon the woman who was coerced, even if she screams for help. She is owed a dowry equivalent to her kind. If she is a slave girl, she is owed a tenth of her price, and if she is a garment, she is owed half a tenth of her price. There is no hadd upon a madman, and no hadd in a case resembling permissibility — such as a woman who marries during her 'iddah, or on a basis of an uncertain obligation. If two men are found together under a single garment, or a man and a woman, the hadd is applied. If two women are found together, they are flogged without the hadd of stoning. If a slave or a slave-girl commits adultery, each of them is punished with fifty lashes, whether or not they are protected.

When an adulterer is brought to be flogged, Allah Almighty commands that witnesses among a group of believers attend the punishment. (161) The adulterer is flogged according to the condition in which he is found: if he is naked, he is left naked; if he is clothed, he is left in his clothing and beaten over it. Nothing that covers him — such as a thick fur or anything that prevents the effect of the beating — shall be left on him. The woman is seated, covered in a garment, and every limb is struck except the face, the private parts, and the vital areas. If striking the back alone is what is practiced, that is permissible and is what is customarily used. The beating shall not be of a kind that kills, and a menstruating woman shall not be struck until she is cleansed. If the adulterer and the adulteress confess to adultery, then retract and leave before confessing four times, and then retract again, their retraction shall not be accepted. Whoever commits adultery with his wife's mother shall receive the hadd punishment, even if she stepped down from him. A slave girl in which he holds a share shall be struck in proportion to the share he does not hold, and he is protected from the hadd in proportion to his share. If his share constitutes the full hadd, then he is spared from it, yet he is still disobedient in proportion to his share. Whoever likens men to women in manner — if he goes too far in that — shall be denied entry to houses. Whoever commits an act with an animal shall be punished for it, and the price of the beast is paid. If the beast is of a kind that is eaten, it is slaughtered and buried, and it is not permissible to eat it. If it is not of a kind that is eaten, it is sold and the proceeds are disposed of so that it may not be recognized. Whoever commits this act is reminded of it

*(161) Al-Noor: 2*

<!-- page 228 -->
and the offices receive flogging in proportion to the hadd of the free, reduced according to the proportion of the slave. This is the unanimous position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

**Mentioning the hadd punishment for slander:**

Allah Almighty said: *"Indeed, those who accuse chaste, heedless, believing women are cursed in this world and the Hereafter."* (162) And He said: *"And those who accuse chaste women, then do not produce four witnesses, flog them eighty lashes, and do not ever accept their testimony, and those are the defiantly disobedient."* (163)

It is narrated from Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that he said: "The major sins are shirk with Allah Almighty, killing a believer in covenant, fleeing from the crawling — except in a perverse way to fight or to take a position — eating usury after evidence of its prohibition, consuming the wealth of orphans unjustly, apostasy after emigration, and falsely accusing chaste, heedless, believing women."

It is narrated from Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, that he said: "Whoever falsely accuses a believer of something that is not in them, Allah Almighty places him in the mud of deception until he finds a way out of what he said." He was asked about a man who slanders his Muslim brother who has an immune status, and said: "The hadd punishment will be established against him if it is proven, and his repentance is to lie against himself before the people and repent. If he does so, his testimony shall be accepted."

The slanderer is flogged eighty lashes. If he slanders a group, he receives the hadd once for all of them if he repents. But if they come to him one after another, he receives the hadd once for each of them. If the one who is slandered — whether a man or a woman — confesses to what was said of them, or if the evidence against them is established, as Allah Almighty has indicated, then there is no hadd upon the slanderer. Whoever slanders a polytheist incurs no hadd, unless the polytheist has a Muslim son — in that case, the hadd is applied to the slanderer for the sanctity of Islam. A Muslim should not slander a polytheist nor a non-polytheist. If a free man slanders another free man, the full hadd is applied. If a slave slanders a free man, the full hadd is also applied. If a dhimmi slanders a Muslim man, the Muslim man takes him to the Sultan. Whoever says to his wife, "I did not find you a virgin," incurs no hadd. Whoever slanders a man

*(162) Al-Noor: 23*
*(163) Al-Noor: 4–5*


<!-- page 229 -->
Whoever comes to the hadd punishment and charges another with it is flogged. Whoever acknowledges the birth of a child and then denies it incurs the flogging of the hadd, and the child's lineage is established, and no one may expose him. Whoever insults the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) is to be killed. Whoever insults any of the Imams (may Allah's prayers be upon them) is likewise killed. Whoever slanders with any tongue, the hadd punishment is imposed upon him. Whoever slanders another and then pardons the one he accused before raising the matter to the Sultan, that pardon is permissible, and the accuser does not retain the right to pursue the matter afterward. However, if the matter has already been raised to the Sultan and the Sultan has been petitioned, no pardon is accepted thereafter. If someone makes an accusation against a child or an insane person, nothing is due from them, though the child is to be disciplined so that he does not dare to repeat such an act. If a man or a woman slanders their own child, there is no hadd punishment upon them, but if the child slanders them, the hadd applies. If a man says to another man, "O sodomite," there is no hadd punishment upon him, because the attribution is only to the people of Lot (peace be upon him). However, if he says, "One who does the work of the people of Lot," then the hadd becomes obligatory upon him. If a man slanders an absent or deceased woman, her guardian may rise against him in her behalf. Whoever abuses a man or a woman with expressions that are considered insults, such as saying "O immoral one," "O malicious one," "Son of a Christian," "Son of a Zoroastrian," and the like — involving cursing and reviling — this is all included in the slander for which the hadd applies, according to the saying of the People of the House (may Allah's prayers be upon them).

**Mention of the punishment for intoxicants and alcohol, and related issues of the hudud:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"O you who believe, indeed wine, gambling, idols, and divining arrows are but defilement from the work of Satan, so avoid it that you may succeed."* (164) The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "May Allah curse wine, the one who presses it, the one who has it pressed, its seller, its buyer, its drinker, and the one who consumes its price." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) also said: "Whoever does not drink wine in this world shall drink from the rivers of Paradise." Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: "A drunkard who meets Allah the Almighty meets Him as one who worships idols." Whoever drinks any intoxicant, Allah does not accept his prayer for forty nights. The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "Every intoxicant is forbidden, and whatever intoxicates in large quantities is forbidden in small quantities as well." Thus, whoever drinks a little or a great deal of alcohol is subject to the hadd. If he repeats the offense, he is struck again. If he drinks a third time, he is to be killed — though he is not killed until the hadd has been administered twice before. Whoever drinks an intoxicant

<!-- page 230 -->
and becomes intoxicated from it such that he does not know what he is doing is beaten the hadd of eighty lashes. If he returns to the offense after being beaten once, he is beaten again. If he returns after being beaten twice, he is killed on the third occasion. If he drinks without becoming intoxicated, he is punished with a severe punishment each time he returns. Whoever drinks wine or an intoxicant not knowing that it is forbidden — there is no hadd upon him. Whoever acknowledges drinking alcohol and then retracts is still given the hadd, and his retraction is not taken into account. The hadd for a slave in the matter of alcohol is eighty lashes, the same as for a free person. If the people of the dhimma drink alcohol or intoxicants, they are beaten as Muslims are beaten, unless they had previously committed to refraining from openly displaying what is forbidden in the religion of Islam. If there is any doubt regarding the hadd, it is suspended. There is no testimony upon testimony, no oath, and no judge may rule on the basis of a written record alone. No one may be imprisoned or beaten through intimidation or hearsay. Whoever is subject to multiple hadd punishments — if the killing is among them, he is killed, and the other punishments fall away. A man may not carry out the hadd punishment on his servant without the Sultan's permission. If one who is obligated to receive the hadd is not named, he is beaten until the one administering the beating stops. Whoever testifies against him is imprisoned until the evidence is properly established; if it is not, he is not imprisoned.

**Mention of the rulings on thieves and warriors:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"And do not consume your wealth among yourselves through falsehood."* (165) *"And the thief, male and female — cut off their hands."* (166) The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "I have seen in the Fire the owner of the cave who used to steal from pilgrims with his staff." He also said: "The thief does not steal while he is a believer." Whoever enters the house of a people without their permission and steals their wealth, or exits having taken goods worth five dinars, and this is acknowledged or evidenced — his hand is cut off at the joint of the four fingers of the palm of his right hand, leaving him with the thumb for continued use.
