If he steals again, his left leg is cut off at half the foot, leaving him with the heel so that he may walk. If he steals after that, he is imprisoned permanently. If he steals while in prison, he is killed. When the cutting is performed,

<!-- page 231 -->
the place of amputation is cauterized with fire to hasten recovery and protect against harm. Even if the cutter errs and cuts the left hand or right leg, that does not undo the punishment, and no further action is taken. Whatever he stole that is still in his possession is returned to its rightful owners, and he is liable for whatever he has destroyed. After the cutting, the thief is banished to a country other than the one in which the cutting was carried out. A guest is not to have his hand cut off if he steals from those who are hosting him. An employee is not to have his hand cut off if he steals from the house of the one who hired him and was permitted entry. Nor is the hand cut off for open or declared snatching in a city — such as one who seizes something and overcomes the owner openly in a populated area. If this is done on the road, the perpetrator is considered a warrior. When such a person is brought before the authority, the ruler has discretion: if he wishes, he may kill him; if he wishes, he may crucify him and leave him alive until he dies; if he wishes, he may cut off his hand and leg from opposite sides; and if he wishes, he may banish him from one country to another — this is done if the warrior has endangered the roads and waylaid Muslims. If the warrior repents before he is captured, he is pardoned. Whoever fights back against such an aggressor in self-defense and kills or wounds him bears no liability for that. If the warrior is captured and found to have money on him, it is returned to his family if they are known. If his family is not known and he confesses, testimony is given that what he took is to be placed in the treasury of the Muslims until whoever is entitled to it comes forward. Whoever steals something in which he has a share is not subject to the cutting. There is no cutting for taking permissible food out of necessity, nor for one who opens a door and removes nothing — there is no cutting for that. There is no cutting for any stone other than precious gems, nor for fruit stolen from trees, nor for a camel or a bird not in an enclosure. Nor is there cutting for what is stolen from places open to general access, such as mosques, inns, bathhouses, public spaces, and every place that people are permitted to enter without permission — unless the stolen item is removed from such a place. Whoever steals sheep from pastures is not subject to cutting. Whoever was previously unable to have the hadd carried out upon him and then becomes subject to it — his hand is cut off, and all he stole is included in the reckoning. Whoever is found to have stolen property in his hand but cannot be proven to have stolen it and does not admit to it — his hand is not cut off, but the property is taken from him and returned to those who rightfully own it. A madman is not subject to cutting if he steals, nor is a boy who has not yet reached the age of responsibility — though the child is to be disciplined so that he does not dare to steal again. The hand of a grave robber is cut off if he takes the shroud out of a grave, for the grave constitutes a protected place. If one whose hand is cut off dies from the cutting, there is no liability. After the cutting, the thief is to be admonished. It is narrated that Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) cut off the hands of a group who had stolen, then brought fire and fed them and gave them water until they recovered, then covered their wounds and instructed them to take their severed hands with their remaining hands.

<!-- page 232 -->
Then he said: "Raise them toward the sky and say: 'O Allah, Ali has cut off our hands according to Your Book and the Sunnah of Your Messenger.'" Then he said to them: "These are your hands. I have preceded you to the Fire. Whoever among you repents, it will drag him to Paradise, and whoever does not repent, it will drag him to Hell." If a slave steals the property of his master, his hand is not cut off. If he steals from someone other than his master, his hand is cut off. If a father steals from his son's property, or a child from his father's property, or a wife from her husband's property, or a husband from his wife's property, or a sister from her brother's property, or a brother from his sister's property — there is no cutting in any of these cases. If a thief steals in a year of famine and hardship, he is not to have his hand cut off. All of this is the saying of the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah's prayers be upon them).

**Mention of the rulings on apostates and innovators:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"How shall Allah guide a people who disbelieved after their faith?"* (167) And: *"Whoever of you turns back from his religion and dies while he is a disbeliever — those are the ones whose deeds have become worthless."* (168) And: *"It has been revealed to you and to those before you: if you associate partners with Allah, your deeds will surely become worthless, and you will surely be among the losers."* (169) The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "It is not permissible to kill a believer except for one of three things: disbelief after faith, fornication after the establishment of marriage, or murder." He also said: "Every innovation is misguidance, and every misguidance leads to the Fire." And he said: "Whoever changes his religion, kill him." Whoever was born in Islam and then left it for any religion other than the accepted religions of the People of the Book is killed without being given a chance to repent — even if he is a woman, who is imprisoned until she repents. As for the heretic who does not believe in resurrection, reckoning, or the prophethood of the Prophets, or one who held a heretical religion other than Islam before submitting — when he submits and then reverts, Islam and repentance are presented to him for three days. If he repents, he is released. If he persists and remains as he was, he is killed on the fourth day and is not given any additional time. If a slave apostatizes in such a manner, he is killed. A slave woman who apostatizes is bound and subjected to the harshest of service, and is not fed except what

<!-- page 233 -->
sustains her until she repents or dies, and she is not killed. Her family is not to be harmed on her account. Similarly, the mother of a child who apostatizes. Whatever property the apostate leaves behind is an inheritance for his heirs, and his apostasy is treated as his death. His wife's bond is dissolved and she may remarry if she wishes. His Muslim children remain Muslim. Those born to him after his apostasy from a Muslim mother are Muslims by virtue of their mother's Islam. If they grow up and reject Islam, they are treated as apostates, and what is done to apostates is done to them. Whoever practices sorcery against Muslims is a disbeliever, for Allah says regarding those who learned from the two angels: *"And they taught no one until they said: 'We are a trial, so do not disbelieve.'"* (170) *"And they follow what the devils recited during the reign of Solomon — and Solomon did not disbelieve, but the devils disbelieved."* (171) The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "The sorcerer among the Muslims is to be killed." As for the sorcerer among the polytheists, he is not killed — it was asked: "O Messenger of Allah, why is that?" He replied: "The shirk he is upon is greater than his sorcery." Sorcery and polytheism are thus coupled together. Whoever embraces Islam is forbidden from consuming what is forbidden in Islam, even from what was previously permitted to him before Islam. All of this is the saying of the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah's prayers be upon them).

**Mention of usurpation and transgression:**

Allah said: *"And do not transgress, for Allah does not love those who transgress."* (172) And He said: *"And do not consume your wealth among yourselves through falsehood."* (173) The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "The sanctity of your wealth among you is like the sanctity of the sacred day in the sacred month in the sacred land." Whoever possesses unlawful wealth is obligated to repent and rid himself of it. There is no repentance except through calling upon those from whom it was taken, and returning it to its rightful owners if they are known. If they are not known, it is to be placed in the treasury of the Muslims until whoever is entitled to it comes forward. If no such way is found, his companions should be informed, and a fine is assessed. It is not permissible for him to keep it or benefit from it. Whoever transgresses, usurps something, or


<!-- page 234 -->

Volume Two — A Brief Book on the Effects

Whoever steals from another is not permitted to transgress, assault, or steal from the one who wronged him, even if he is able to do so, because that would itself constitute usurpation. Betrayal, theft, and transgression are all forbidden, and the one who has been wronged must not respond in kind. Rather, he must fulfil his trustworthiness and must not betray or steal. Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "Fulfil your trusts, and do not betray those who betrayed you."

Whoever rapes a woman and she gives birth must return the child to the one from whom she was taken, and the child is a slave belonging to the one who assaulted her. If he assaulted her and sold her to a man, and the buyer fathered a child by her knowing that she was taken by force, then she is to be returned to the one from whom she was seized, and the value of the child is to be paid to the buyer, who in turn returns the price to the usurper who sold her. Whoever forcibly takes a slave, an animal, or any property and it perishes in his possession shall be liable for its value or its equivalent. If he seized it and it increased in value while in his hands, the increase belongs to the owner from whom it was usurped, even if it later diminishes — the usurper is liable for whatever is lost. If he rents out the property, the rent belongs to the owner. If he seized sheep or animals and they reproduced, the offspring are not his but belong to the one wronged by the usurpation. Whoever transgresses upon something belonging to a place of entertainment and destroys it with wine or intoxicants incurs no liability in that case. Whoever has his money seized, and is able to recover it by his own means, may take it back, but it is not permissible for him to take something else in its place.

**On Loans and Deposits**

Allah Almighty said — I seek refuge in Allah from the accursed Satan — "And do not forget generosity among yourselves."

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, said (174): the loan, the deposit, and the guest are obligations established in the Sunnah. And Ali, peace be upon him, said: whoever protects an orphan whose wealth Allah Almighty has obligated — He has promised Paradise to the one who fulfils this, just as He has promised the Fire to the one who consumes it unjustly. Just as the sin falls upon the betrayer for what was entrusted to him, so too shall the reward be given to the one who was entrusted with something and faithfully discharged that trust.

Whoever borrows a piece of property is responsible for it from the moment he takes it. If it perishes or is lost, he is liable for it. If he transgresses with it or fails to return it and it is then lost, he is also liable. However, if it is destroyed or damaged without any act of wrongdoing or negligence on his part, there is no liability upon him. He is trusted if he is trustworthy. If he borrows dinars or dirhams and they perish,

(174) Al-Baqarah: 237

<!-- page 235 -->

Volume Two — A Brief Book on the Effects

he is liable for them, because they become a loan. However, if he borrows something identified as a particular item — such as a specific dirham — it cannot rightly be treated as a loan. Whoever borrows a piece of property and then mortgages it or sells it: if its owner is at hand, he may recover it, and the buyer may seek restitution from the seller for the price paid. Whoever pledges borrowed property is liable for an equivalent pledge, or for what that pledge was worth, unless the owner of the borrowed item had given permission for the borrower to pledge or sell it. If it is in his possession, then no obligation falls upon him.

Whoever receives a deposit must preserve it and safeguard it as someone of comparable standing would. If it is destroyed or lost through no act of wrongdoing on his part after he has taken proper care of it, there is no liability upon him. But if he loses it through negligence, misuse, or transgression, he is liable. If the owner of the deposit instructs him to deliver it to a man and he claims to have done so, but that man denies receiving it and the owner of the deposit does not accept his word, then he must pay for it after all of them have sworn oaths.

Whoever holds a deposit is permitted to borrow from it if he has a corresponding debt and can attest to that — this becomes his responsibility. If a child or an insane person makes a deposit and it is lost, there is no recourse against the depositor, for he was misled through no fault of his own. If a slave makes a deposit without the permission of his master and the deposit is lost, the slave takes responsibility for it — but this claim is made against him when he is freed, not against his master. If two men jointly deposit something with a third party and one of them comes to claim it while the other is absent, the depositor should give the one present only half, as it was deposited jointly. He may not pay it all to one without the other's presence. Nor may the one present take any portion until his companion arrives — unless the absent party has died and his heirs come in his stead, in which case it may be released to them. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them.

**On Found Property, the Foundling, and Related Matters**

Whoever finds lost property in the Haram of Makkah, or in a place where he believes it to be safe and is not afraid it will be taken, should announce it and leave it where it is until its owner comes. If it is not in the Haram and he fears for its safety, he should take it if he is able to do so and cannot otherwise leave it, for the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, forbade the wasting of wealth. If he takes it, he should make it known. If its owner comes and can testify to or describe it, it must be returned to him. If no owner comes forward after the period specified in the Sunnah, it is placed in the treasury of the Muslims if there is a means to do so, or he may give it in charity on the owner's behalf — and if the owner later comes, it is returned to him, or left with him if

<!-- page 236 -->

Volume Two — A Brief Book on the Effects

he was unable to place it in the treasury of the Muslims. It is disliked to take stray camels if they are in a safe place, because they can endure thirst and hunger until they find their companions. However, if the time grows long and the finder fears for the animal, he may take it and care for it until its owners come, spending on it and then being reimbursed by them when they claim it. If the time extends and no claimant comes, the matter is referred to the governing authority, who sells it and keeps the proceeds — dealing with the price as one would deal with found property, whether it is a sheep, cattle, a slave, or any other animal. No stray animal is to be left if one finds it; rather, one does as has been described. If a claimant comes, he must approach the one who took it, and the finder is not obligated to return it without recompense, because Allah Almighty has made it obligatory to safeguard the wealth of believers — the believers owe this duty to one another, and the finder earns a reward if his intention was righteous, and the expenditure he incurred in caring for it must be repaid.

If one finds perishable property that would spoil if left, he should sell it and do with the proceeds as he would with found property. Whoever finds buried property in someone's home, it belongs to the people of that home if they claim it, even if it is a ruin that people pass through — in which case the procedure for found property applies. If it is found buried and appears to be from the wealth of a prior age, it is to be taken to the treasury of the Muslims. We have already mentioned what is obligatory for the finder in such cases.

A foundling is free if his parents are unknown. His inheritance goes to the treasury of the Muslims, and no one inherits from him by lineage alone — only by marriage. When he grows up and his circumstances become clear, if it is apparent who raised him, he is asked to pay for that upbringing. Whoever made a gift to someone who asked to be taken in or who went astray: that gift is for him, and returning it belongs to him — to make of it what he was given and to have it as a condition upon himself. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them.

**On Division and Property Structures**

Whatever lies between the four walls, and wealth held between partners, is subject to division and must not be refused if the partners or some of them request it — even if the division would be disadvantageous, requiring them to sell it and divide the proceeds. It is permissible to divide a house such that the lower portion belongs to some partners and the upper to others. The roof of the lower level and its walls are the responsibility of the owner of the lower portion, while what lies above belongs to the owner of the upper level. The ground beneath the upper level is the land of the upper owner. If the lower portion falls into disrepair without any cause attributable to the upper owner, the one below is responsible for the repairs, as are the lower walls upon which the upper level rests. Whoever is

<!-- page 237 -->

Volume Two — A Brief Book on the Effects

obligated by this is required to repair what has deteriorated under his care, and whatever belongs to the lower level must be repaired by the lower owner — because it is his property, even though the upper level rests upon it and stands upon it when it is divided. When a wall stands between two neighbours and it falls, both are obligated to rebuild it. If one of them builds it, the other may not benefit from it until he pays half of the cost. If one of them is required to pay and refuses, the other may build it, and the one who refused forfeits his claim to it.

No one is permitted to open in his wall an aperture that would expose his neighbour's private areas to view, but he may open it for light and air provided it does not permit a view of his neighbour's private parts — in which case he has the right to do so, and the neighbour has the right to build a wall in his own home to block it if he wishes, but he may not prevent someone from building on his own property in a way that covers his neighbours' view, if that is within his right. Whoever opens a door onto a public thoroughfare or digs a well within the boundary of his own wall may not be prevented from doing so if it is on a passable public street. If it is on a dead-end street and it causes harm, he may not do so except with the permission of those who share that street. Even if it is at the far end of the dead end and none of them passes by it, still, whoever has a share in the way may not obstruct it or change its course. If the path belongs to him and the others' access rises beyond it, then he alone may redirect it — and that is his right alone. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them.

**On Testimony**

Allah Almighty said: "And call to witness two witnesses from among your men." (175)

Allah Almighty said: "And raise up the testimony for" — "your men, and if there are not two men, then a man and two women of those whom you approve as witnesses." (176)

"And let witnesses testify justly among you." And He said: "Do not conceal testimony, for whoever conceals it, his heart is sinful." (177) And He said: "It is more proper that testimony be given in its true form." (178)

And He said regarding false witness. (179)

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, said: Whoever suppresses knowledge —

(175) Al-Talaq: 2
(176) Al-Baqarah: 282
(177) Al-Baqarah: 283
(178) Al-Ma'idah: 108
(179) Al-Hajj: 30


<!-- page 238 -->
He knows that on the Day of Resurrection he will come bridled with fire. And the Prophet (peace be upon him) said that the false witness will have his tongue bitten into fire on the Day of Resurrection, as a dog bites his tongue into a vessel. He said: "May Allah curse the false witness." Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali said: "May Allah's prayers be upon him, so that the witness may perform what he witnessed and fear Allah, for it is false testimony to bear witness to what one does not know and to deny what one knows." Whoever testifies falsely, and this is known of him, shall be flogged with a painful flogging, and his disgrace shall be made public. His testimony shall be destroyed, whereas the testimony of the just believer carries a reward for every person — near or far — who testifies on his behalf. The testimony of a just person is permissible except regarding his own loyalists or in matters pertaining to those he has freed. If he testifies on behalf of someone from whose servitude he has been freed, his testimony is permissible in that regard.

The testimony of boys is tainted, and it is not permissible for them to testify otherwise. It is also not permissible to accept the testimony of two suspects, nor of an accused person, nor of a child born of adultery, nor of a partner against his partner in matters between them. However, it is permissible for such a person to testify regarding matters outside their shared dealings, if he is just. If a follower, a hired person, or a servant is just, his testimony is permissible on behalf of the one he serves, works for, or follows — except in matters that would benefit himself or in which he is suspected. Nor is it permissible for a neighbor to testify in his own favor, nor is the testimony of an adversary against an opponent admissible. Nor is the testimony of the people of deviant inclinations admissible against the believers. If the slanderer repents, his testimony becomes permissible. If a testimony is established and the witness is then challenged, and witnesses come forward to discredit him through denial, suspicion, or conduct unbecoming of just people, his testimony is dropped. Whoever prays in the congregation mosque openly shows goodness; if nothing bad is known of him, his testimony is permissible unless his discrediting and the testimony against him are proven.

It is permissible for a Muslim to testify against the people of shirk, but it is not permissible for a polytheist to testify against Muslims except in matters of a will made during travel, when no one else is available, provided he swears an oath regarding it. The testimony of some non-Muslims against each other is permissible if they are truthful in their religion and nothing is known of their discrediting. If a person testifies during his state of disbelief, or while a minor, and then the disbeliever submits to Islam and the minor comes of age, their testimony is permissible if they are just at the time they testify — unless they had already given that testimony previously, in which case the testimony must be repeated. The testimony of women is permissible in marriage and in financial matters, but is not permissible in divorce, prescribed punishments (hudud), or in matters between them. If three men and two women testify to adultery, stoning becomes obligatory; but if two men and four women testify, it is not permissible to rule on adultery. Women's testimony is permissible

<!-- page 239 -->
in matters of initiation. The testimony of a midwife alone is acceptable if she is just, and not otherwise. Initiation refers to the cry of the newborn at birth; if he cries and then dies, it is known that he lived as a child. It is not permissible for women to testify regarding the crescent moon of Ramadan — upon whose sighting fasting begins or ends — but their testimony is permissible in matters of 'iddah, postpartum periods, and financial matters that men are not permitted to witness from women. It is not permissible to testify on the basis of another witness's testimony in a case of a prescribed punishment, killing, or retaliation. It is permissible for a single man to give testimony only on the testimony of two men, or if the original witness is absent in a location to which the prayer is shortened and the traveling faster breaks his fast, and he is ill or for a period in which he is unable to attend the judicial council, or if he has died after giving his testimony, even if he was a valid witness. One is not permitted to testify except based on personal knowledge.

If witnesses differ regarding divorce — one witness saying he divorced her for the 'iddah period and another saying otherwise — their testimony is not admissible. If they differ regarding the dowry, one testifying to a lesser amount and the other to a greater amount, the woman may, if she wishes, take the testimony of the one who testified to the greater amount and receive it, or take the lesser amount if she prefers, without an oath — unless one of them disputes with her and swears alongside the witnesses. It is permissible for two witnesses to testify on behalf of a person regarding his right, for rights may sometimes be waived in ways the witnesses do not know of, or the witness does not testify on a written document unless the witness can confirm his testimony and knows it. Allah said: "Except those who bear witness to the truth while they know." If witnesses retract their testimony before the verdict is issued, they are dismissed. If they retract after the verdict, their retraction is not acted upon. If they retract before the verdict but then return to their testimony, judgment is not made on the basis of their testimony. If they retract after the verdict, they must compensate for whatever was destroyed by virtue of their testimony. Whoever is called upon to testify must not refrain from doing so. Allah said:

*(181)* "And do not refuse when you are called upon as a witness." His testimony must be fulfilled according to the words of Allah Almighty: "And do not conceal testimony, and whoever conceals it" *(182)*. Whoever hears a people in a binding agreement, or hears a person acknowledge something but does not testify — if the one who acknowledged it asks him to testify to what he heard, he has the choice: if he wishes to testify, he may testify, and if he testifies

*(180) Al-Zukhruf: 86*
*(181) Al-Baqarah: 282*
*(182) Al-Baqarah: 283*

<!-- page 240 -->
he must testify truthfully; and if he does not testify, it is not because he was forbidden to testify. Testimony in matters of death, lineage, accounting, and matters lost in antiquity is permissible based on hearing and reporting, and through widespread knowledge among the people. They testify to the death of one who dies, and a man is attributed to his father, yet most of them did not witness it with their own eyes, nor did they testify to it firsthand. In such matters, the heard acknowledgment is accepted, and all of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them.

**Mentioning the Case and the Evidence:**

Allah Almighty said: "And do not consume your wealth among yourselves unjustly, and take it to the judges." *(183)* He said: "To eat some of the wealth of the people in sin, while you know." *(184)* He said: "Indeed, Allah commands you to render trusts to their rightful owners, and when you judge between people, judge with justice."

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said: "I judge between you by evidence and oaths. Some of you may be more persuasive in argument than others, and if I cut off something from his brother's wealth for him, it is a piece of fire that has been cut off for him." The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) and his family forbade deducting the wealth of Muslims through false oaths, for the ruling of a judge does not make lawful what is otherwise unlawful for the one ruled in his favor. It is not permissible for him to take it, and the judge rules based on outward appearances, while Allah knows the unseen and the hidden.

The burden of evidence in financial claims lies upon the plaintiff, and the oath lies upon the defendant. The oath is taken by Allah Almighty and by none other. The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) said: "Whoever swears an oath, let him swear by Allah, and whoever swears by Allah, let him be believed; and whoever does not do so is not a Muslim." If the plaintiff makes a claim and the defendant denies it, the judge requests his oath. If the defendant refers the oath back to the plaintiff and says to swear regarding what he has claimed, he may do so. If the plaintiff wishes to combine his oath with evidence, he may swear it, and judgment is rendered against the defendant by the plaintiff's oath. If the defendant does not swear and says that the plaintiff's father had sworn an oath, the plaintiff must then swear an oath, and rather than giving him what he swore to, no other course is taken.

The evidence is established, and the defendant has the right to request from the plaintiff that he has a right over him as evidenced by some of the evidence. If this diminishes it somewhat or not at all, it remains his right, because rights — as we mentioned — may be waived

*(183) Al-Baqarah: 188*
*(184) Al-Nisa': 58*

<!-- page 241 -->
in ways the witnesses do not know of, or what is required. If the defendant has nothing by which to swear, and if he does not request the oath, the judge who knows this should prompt him — for most people do not know what they are entitled to do. If he knows and is asked, the oath is administered to him; if he does not ask for it, the matter is left to him. If the testimony is regarding a minor or an absent party, the judge does not rule in favor of the plaintiff until the plaintiff swears alongside the evidence. If the plaintiff swears and judgment is rendered against the defendant, and the defendant then comes with evidence to the contrary, the judge does not rescind his ruling or turn away from the right of the defendant.

Judgment based on the testimony of a single witness accompanied by the plaintiff's oath is valid in purely financial matters; it is not ruled upon in other than financial matters. If the disputed object is in the possession of one of the two parties, or in the hands of both together, or in neither of their hands, and both parties bring witnesses, all the witnesses being reliable — then the two adversaries take oaths. If one of them refuses the oath, judgment is rendered in favor of the one who swore. If they both take the oath, the matter is decided by lot between them, and whichever one's share comes out in the lot, that thing is his. If both take the oath and the object is in all their hands, the lot is cast among them as well. If it is in the hands of a third party, they both take the oath and it is not adjudicated in favor of either of them alone. If it is in the possession of one of them and the other takes an oath, judgment is not rendered in favor of the other, and the object remains with the one in whose possession it is. If that one refuses the oath as well, the object remains in his possession. If the evidence of one of them testifies that the origin of the object is his, or that its proceeds are his, and the other testifies that it is his but the origin is not established, then the thing is adjudicated to the one whose testimony establishes both the origin and the proceeds.

Whoever acknowledges slavery willingly is a slave; if he then claims freedom or it is claimed for him, this is not accepted except with evidence. If he acknowledges slavery and then mentions that he is free, the burden of evidence lies upon the one who claims to be a free man. Whoever receives dinars or dirhams from a man and the man later returns claiming they were defective — if the recipient denies this and swears that he only gave him sound currency and does not know of any defect — if he breaks his oath that the currency he gave him was sound, he must replace it for the recipient.

If a man and a woman, their heirs, or one party disputes with the heirs of the other regarding the property of the house, whoever among them has evidence for what he claims is awarded it. If there is no evidence, an oath is taken, and whoever refuses the oath is ruled against on the claim of his opponent in what he claimed. If they both swear and there is no evidence for either of them, the man receives what is recognized as belonging to men and the woman receives what is recognized as belonging to women.

<!-- page 242 -->
If two men or a group share the same dwelling, whether free or enslaved, or free non-Muslims — and they dispute over a child among them — the lot is cast among them, and whoever's share comes out is attributed the child. If the polytheist's share comes out but the child's mother is a Muslim, then the child is Muslim, and there is no way for the polytheist to claim the child as a Muslim woman's child. If the result comes out in favor of a polytheist and the polytheist is drawn to it, then it follows the religion of the polytheist. If a Muslim's or a freed slave's share comes out regarding the child, then the child is a free Muslim.

Whoever sends a man with something to another and the recipient says, "I delivered it to him," while the one who was sent to it denies receiving it — the word of the one claiming delivery is not accepted without evidence. If the payment was made to the recipient to fulfill a purpose and the payer says, "You gave it to him as charity," then the word of the recipient is accepted regarding what he claimed as his right, if he is trustworthy. This is all the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

**Mentioning the Literature of Judges:**

Allah Almighty said: "We have made you a caliph in the land, so judge among the people by truth." *(185)* "And that I may judge between them by what Allah has revealed." *(186)* He said: "And whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed — those are the disbelievers." *(187)* He said: "And whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed — those are the wrongdoers." *(188)* He said: "And whoever does not judge by what Allah has revealed — those are the wicked." *(189)* "Those who claim to believe in what I have sent down to you and what was sent down before you desire to be judged before the Taghut, though they were commanded to disbelieve in it, and Satan wishes to lead them far astray." *(190)*

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him) said: "Do not seek the emirate, for whoever asked for it is left to it, and whoever comes to it without asking is aided in it." He forbade anyone from being subjected to injustice in a judicial matter, and it is incumbent upon one to whom a just imam calls to judge, who does good in it, to accept — except that if he falls short, he has no way to make up for it.

*(185) Sad: 26*
*(186) Al-Ma'idah: 49*
*(187) Al-Ma'idah: 44*
*(188) Al-Ma'idah: 45*
*(189) Al-Ma'idah: 47*
*(190) Al-Nisa': 60*


<!-- page 243 -->
An unjust imam should not judge while he does not know the science of justice. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: Whoever gives a fatwa without knowledge, the angels of heaven and the angels of the earth curse him. And whoever rules and makes a mistake will come on the Day of Resurrection with his hands tied. The Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him and his family) forbade appointing those who do not know, and he wrote to Rifa'a: Do not appoint those whose words you do not trust among us, otherwise Allah will be your opponent and your seeker. It was said to him concerning Mu'awiyah: if you appoint him, he will acknowledge his own removal. He replied: Where then is the word of Allah the Almighty, "And you did not take the misguided as a supporter"?

He addressed the people in his sermon and said: I have been held hostage, and I am a leader who does not stir in anyone lacking piety. He cultivated a people and did not let piety go dry — a branch of origin, of truth, and of good — in one who knew. It is enough for a man to be ignorant if he does not know his own standing. Whoever is hated by Allah the Almighty falls into one of two types: a man whom Allah has left to himself, one who is deliberately unjust in his ways, impassioned by a heresy inspired in him — occupied by fasting and prayer — a temptation for those dazzled by his worship, who has strayed from the guidance of those who came before him and has led others astray. He carries the burden of the sins of those who were misled by his error. And a man clothed in ignorance, blind to his own transgression.

The worst of people are those who parade themselves as scholars before the world, calling themselves learned, yet for one who has genuinely sought knowledge for even a single day, they have denied it safely and increased in what they claimed was superior — until you saw jinn and others gather around them. For a long time he sat among the people as a self-appointed judge, seeking to discharge himself of what he suspected in others. Even if he disagreed with a judge who preceded him, he would not accept that judge's ruling, and whenever a difficult matter came before him, he prepared an answer from his own opinion and issued it — though he was tangled in doubts like a spider's web, not knowing whether he was right or wrong. If he was right, he feared he had erred; if he erred, he hoped he had been right. Knowledge does not count in something he denies, and he does not see that beyond what he has reached there is a doctrine. If he measured something against something else and his view did not hold, and if he was confronted by something concealed from him — knowing it from his own ignorance lest it be said he does not know — he would sit down and continue on his way. He is the key to confusion, suspicion, frustration, and ignorance. He does not apologise for what he does not know; he yields without biting with a sharp tooth in knowledge, seizing narrations like the peak of a wild wind. Inheritances cry out because of him and blood screams because of him, and it is forbidden to make lawful what is forbidden. I swear by issuing what was mentioned to him — he is not worthy of what has been delegated to him. O people, see the defect of those who have claimed this position.

<!-- page 244 -->
You must obey only one whose ignorance you cannot excuse, for knowledge is that with which Adam (peace be upon him) was honoured, and that which all the Prophets (peace be upon them) were endowed with, culminating in the Seal of the Prophets Muhammad (peace be upon him) and in his pure family. If such a person leads you astray, where then will you go?

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) sent me to Yemen. I said: O Messenger of Allah, send me to adjudicate among them when I am a young man and I know nothing of the judiciary. He struck me on the chest and said: O Allah, guide his heart and steady his tongue. And one who splits the grain and perceives the breeze should not doubt thereafter between two judges. He said: The occurrence of fitnah comes from whims and from rulings that are innovated, in which the rule of Allah is absent, while men follow other men. If the truth is the truth, it is settled and there is no disagreement; but if falsehood has been introduced, it has not been hidden from the people of discernment — yet it has been taken from here and mixed from there, with the devil taking control of his guardians while sparing those who had done good before them.

He said: The Muslims must not be given over to one who will be greedy for their wealth, nor to the ignorant who would destroy them with his ignorance, nor to the miserly who would withhold their rights from them, nor to the ignorant who would burden them with his ignorance, nor to a traitor to their lands — one who takes from a people without giving back — nor to the bribed in authority, who takes away the rights of the people. And the one who obstructs the Sunnah will destroy the nation. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's prayers be upon him and his family) said: Whoever acts as a judge deliberately or by mistake is in the Fire. And he wrote to Rifa'a concerning his judge over Ahvaz: Know that this emirate is a trust; whoever makes it a betrayal, upon him is Allah's curse on the Day of Resurrection.

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) forbade the judge from showing favouritism to either of the two parties — he must be very careful and attentive — and the judge should not speak before he has heard what each party says.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon him) said: Whoever knows anything of our rulings, appoint him among you as a judge, for I have made him a judge — so accept his judgment. He also said: The judge must treat the two parties equitably in his ruling. He should be attentive to their words, listen carefully, and remain impartial. He should not speak until he has heard what they say, and he should not conduct proceedings in the mosque without limit. It is permitted for him to discipline those who are required to be respectful. He should be respected and firm, considerate of the circumstances of those who seek his assistance and approach him, and he should refrain from involvement in the marketplace

and from engaging personally in buying and selling. He should delegate such matters to those he trusts in their religion and honesty, and warn them against

<!-- page 245 -->
underestimating or neglecting their duties. He rules according to the Book of Allah the Almighty, and what he does not find therein he seeks in what is authentically established from the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) and from the Imams of his lineage (peace be upon them). If he does not know the ruling, he should refer the matter to the Imam of his time and ask him. He should not rule according to his own opinion, nor his own desires, nor by analogy, nor by approbation. No intercession is to be accepted, nor is the intercessor to be received with approval by the one being interceded for. He must treat the noble and the humble, the virtuous and the questionable, the strong and the weak equally before him. He should not hear one party alone without the other being present, nor instruct one party in his argument, nor assist him, nor lean toward either side.

If he decides a case and it later becomes clear to him that he has violated the right, he must rule correctly — and the same applies to cases that have already been brought before him and upon which he has previously ruled.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon him) said: Among the Children of Israel there was a judge who judged them with truth. When he died, his wife wept at his side. He said: Weep for me, and I hope to be well by Allah's grace, for I know that I was wronged. She said: What is the sign of that? He said: When I am placed in my grave, come down to me and uncover my face. She did so and saw a great worm at his nostrils; she was frightened, covered him, and left. When night came, she saw him in a dream and he said to her: Do not be alarmed by what you saw. What befell me came from you — a dispute between you and your brother, a man who I felt was in the right, so I judged in his favour, and so I did, and I received it as a consequence of my own desire.

Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was asked about the words of Allah the Almighty and Exalted: ﴿And do not consume your wealth among yourselves unjustly, and offer it as a bribe to the rulers so that you may consume a portion of the wealth of the people in sin while you know.﴾ (192)

He said: Allah knows best. The people are in sin, and they know that there are unjust rulers in the nation. But he did not mean the rulers of the people of justice — he meant the rulers of the people of injustice. If a man has a right over another and summons him before the rulers of the people of justice, I will only stand against the one who rises to the rulers of the people of injustice, for such a one is among those who have ruled by al-Taghut. This is the saying of Allah the Almighty: ﴿Have you not seen those who claim to believe in what has been revealed to you and what has been revealed before you?﴾

<!-- page 246 -->
He said: They will be judged by al-Taghut. (193)

The people of injustice are those who disagree with you in your matter. But look for a man among you who knows something of our rulings, appoint him among you, for I have made him a judge — so accept his judgment. As for whatever happened to the family of the wrongdoer from those who gave rulings against them in the days of their power — leave it and do not pursue it. He should not rule in a way that benefits from the arguments of the convicted party. It is permissible for him to accept what comes to him from the public treasury, and it is not permissible for him to take anything for himself in the matter that is being decided between the parties.

He may inspect the markets of the Muslims and their weights and measures, and should delegate this to those he trusts. He shall not imprison anyone on suspicion except in cases involving blood. He may strike with the durrah in matters of slander, discretionary punishment, and discipline. When the prescribed punishments are due, flogging is applied, and he shall not imprison in matters of debt. If a wealthy person is found, he is ordered to pay, and if he refuses, he is beaten until he fulfils what he has been sentenced to. If the debtor is absent, he shall be imprisoned until he produces what is owed, then sentenced accordingly. If he delays upon return, his property shall be sold and his debt settled from it.

He does not accept a written judgment in matters of divorce, nor testimony upon testimony, nor does he take a guarantor from anyone. He executes the judgment of a just judge and does not accept a judge's letter unless it is witnessed by someone he trusts. Whatever was before the judge's court from a previous judge — testimony that was not executed — if the witnesses against the defendant deny it, he should have them sworn if requested. If they swear that it had not been discharged against him, he asks the claimant to bring evidence and accepts the power of attorney. Testimony in such matters shall be heard whether the litigant is present or absent. He rules in favour of the absent party and holds him to his argument if he appears. He adjudicates between the People of the Book with truth if they bring their dispute to him. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah bless them all).

<!-- page 247 -->
Conclusion

The book *Mukhtasar al-Athar*, Volume Two, has been completed. Praise be to Allah, the Lord of the Worlds, and may Allah bestow blessings and peace upon our master Muhammad and upon his noble and pure family. Peace be upon him, and Allah is our sufficiency and the best of trustees, the best of patrons and the best of supporters.

This noble book was transcribed in the presence of our master and Mawlana al-Sharafi, Hussein ibn al-Hasan ibn Abdullah al-Mukarrami — may Allah sanctify his soul — for the sake of Muhammad ibn Abdullah and his family (peace be upon him), in Mansoura in Najran, may Allah protect it, by the hand of the one who is poor before Allah and seeking His pleasure and forgiveness: Abdullah ibn Ali ibn Ali al-Anf. This was completed on the thirtieth of the month of Muharram al-Haram in the year one thousand four hundred and five of the Hijri calendar.

Copies of this book were renewed in the era of our master Ahmed ibn Ali al-Jamali, and the appointed Sidi Ali Hassan Abdullah al-Makrami — may Allah bless us with their long lives — on 21/5/1437 AH.
